Cited at CW 9i §89. *Relevance: Bastian's work is the nineteenth-century comparative-ethnographic substrate on which Jung's archetype-theory rests. Low priority for direct ingest — his material is sufficiently summarized via Jung and the secondary Jungian literature.*
- **Ignatius of Loyola** — *Spiritual Exercises* (*Ejercicios Espirituales*, composed 1522–1524, first published 1548). Jesuit founder. Source of the *aliquem alium internum* ("some other one within") phrase Jung invokes at CW 9i §237, §243 as a Western-contemplative precedent for the inner-friend figure. The *Exercises* formalize a colloquy-procedure with an inner-other whose counsel differs from the exercitant's own. *Relevance: important as a parallel formalization of [[active-imagination]] / inner-figure engagement inside Western Christian contemplative practice. Medium priority — could ground a "contemplative-practice precedents for active imagination" page if the wiki's interest in practice-lineages deepens.*
- **Origen of Alexandria** — Early Christian theologian (c. 184–253). CW 9i §§488–491 and §§625–626 invoke Origen's homily formulation "each of us is not one, but many" (*unusquisque nostrum non est unus sed multi*) as a patristic precedent for the federation-of-selves view that Jung's individuation-as-*in-dividual* elaborates. *Relevance: already captured via CW 9i page [[individuation]]. The Origen corpus (*On First Principles*, *Contra Celsum*, homilies) is substantial and is one of the routes into patristic-theological anthropology if that becomes relevant. Low-medium priority.*
- **Sura 18 (Al-Kahf, "The Cave") of the Qur'an** — Verses 60–82. Source of the Khidr ("Al-Khiḍr," "the Green One") narrative Jung close-reads at CW 9i §§244–258 as paradigm inner-friend encounter. The pericope also contains the Seven Sleepers of Ephesus story and the Dhu al-Qarnayn narrative (neither of which Jung addresses in CW 9i). *Relevance: the Khidr narrative is the volume's most sustained single close-reading of a religious text; already captured via [[sura-18-moses-khidr]]. Could open further work on Sufi commentary tradition on the Khidr figure (Ibn Arabi, Suhrawardi), which would deepen the inner-friend material. Low-medium priority.*
- **Albrecht Dieterich** — German classical philologist. *Eine Mithrasliturgie* (1903, expanded 1910), publishing the Greek magical papyrus Jung cites at CW 9i §§104–110 (the papyrus containing the "sun's tube" image that matches Jung's 1906 schizophrenic patient's spontaneous sun-penis image, paradigm autochthony case). *Relevance: historical-philological, not a target for ingest — the papyrus's content is sufficiently conveyed through Jung's citation.*
- **Maria Prophetissa (Maria the Jewess)** — Third- or fourth-century Alexandrian alchemist. Source of the axiom ("One becomes two, two becomes three, and out of the third comes the one as the fourth") Jung cites at CW 9i §237, §351 as compressed formula for the quaternary-wholeness structure. Her fragments survive through Zosimos of Panopolis and other later alchemical compilers. *Relevance: already captured via [[axiom-of-maria]]. The broader alchemical corpus (Zosimos, Stephanus, the Arabic alchemists, Gerhard Dorn whom Jung cites extensively in CW 14) is the next Jungian ingest-frontier if the project continues in that direction. Parked in favor of the core-CW inventory for now.*
- **Heraclitus** — Already cited via CW 7 (above); reappears at CW 9i §522 in the *nirdvandva* discussion as pre-Socratic precedent for the unity-of-opposites theme. No new material.
- **Johann Wolfgang von Goethe** — Already cited via CW 7 (above); reappears throughout CW 9i (notably Essay 8 on spirit in fairytales, where the *Faust*-Mephistopheles material structures parts of the argument). No new citation-content.
- **Friedrich Nietzsche** — Already cited via CW 7 (above); Zarathustra cited again at CW 9i §§520–521 in the discussion of the transcendent function, where Nietzsche's "become who you are" formulation is read as both an individuation-directive and an inflation-trap depending on how it is held. No new source.
- **Sigmund Freud** — Already cited via CW 7 (above); reappears in CW 9i Essay 2 where Jung distinguishes his collective-unconscious from Freud's personal-unconscious. No new source.
- **Martin Buber** — Jewish philosopher, *I and Thou* (*Ich und Du*, 1923). Cited at CW 9i §276 and §620 for the *I-Thou* / *I-It* distinction that Jung reads as structurally parallel to the contact-with-archetype / identification-with-archetype distinction (see [[development/identification-vs-contact-with-archetype]]). *Relevance: Buber is one of the Western theological-phenomenological figures the persona project could ingest productively if the I-Thou-structure vocabulary turns out to be useful for specifying the inner-friend relation. Medium priority.*
- **Meister Eckhart** — Medieval Christian mystic. Cited throughout CW 9i (Essays 6, 10, 11) for his formulations of the soul as containing a spark (*Seelenfünklein*) that is of the same substance as God — a medieval Christian framing of the ego-Self relation Jung reads as structurally parallel to his own. *Relevance: Eckhart's sermons have been ingested indirectly through the wiki's contemplative-tradition references; direct ingest could ground the wiki's Christian-mystical track. Low-medium priority.*
- **Jakob Böhme (Boehme)** — Seventeenth-century Lutheran mystic. Cited across CW 9i Essays 10 and 11 for his mandala-like diagrams of the Trinity-with-Sophia as quaternary structure. Jung reads Böhme as one of the Western imaginers of a four-fold Self-structure that Christian orthodoxy had truncated into Trinity. *Relevance: Böhme's *Signatura Rerum* and *Mysterium Magnum* would ground the Western-quaternary-speculation track. Low-medium priority.*

## Jung — *Psychological Types* (CW 6)

- **Carl Jung** — *Psychological Types* (Collected Works, Volume 6; originally *Psychologische Typen*, 1921). **Ingested 2026-04-22.** Jung's most systematic single work — the volume that gives the wiki its *function-type* vocabulary (the 2 attitudes × 4 functions matrix, eight types), its explicit theory of how psychological typology determines philosophical and theological commitment, and its most formal statement of *esse in anima* as the metaphysical standing of psychic images. **Core contributions**: the **eight function-types** (§§556–671) with the rational/irrational distinction (T/F rational = judging; S/N irrational = perceiving, §778) and the dominant-auxiliary pairing rule — an auxiliary must come from the opposite axis, never the same (§667, §669), grounding the wiki's [[function-types]] and [[auxiliary-function-pairing]] pages; **attitude-types** (§§556–577, introversion/extraversion as libido-orientation, not as Eysenck-style behavior trait) — deepening [[attitude-types]]; the **living symbol** specification (§§814–829): a symbol is *living* only when it is the best possible expression of something still partly unknown — once fully translatable into known terms it is *dead*, reduced to sign — grounding [[living-symbol]]; ***esse in anima*** (§66, §78) as the metaphysical warrant for psychological-image reality — images are neither *esse in re* nor *esse in intellectu solo* but have their own mode of being, in the psyche — grounding [[esse-in-anima]]; the **numinal accent** (§§982–985) as the determinant of what becomes *real* for a given psychic constitution — "reality" is whatever carries numinal emphasis, and the typological distribution of numinal accent is what makes the Freud/Adler dispute a typological rather than evidential dispute — grounding [[numinal-accent]]; the **autonomous complex** (§§923–928, Appendix Paper 3, 1931) as non-pathological structural fact, with the ego itself as an autonomous complex and the parental complex as universal-in-presence / particular-in-working-out — folded into [[complex-theory]]; the **reductive vs constructive method** distinction (§§788–790) — reductive traces symptom back to cause; constructive asks what the image is *for*, what it prefigures — grounding [[reductive-vs-constructive-method]]; **abstraction and concretism** (§§678–680, §§696–699) as complementary psychic operations, not virtue vs vice — grounding [[abstraction-and-concretism]]; **engram and functional trace** (§§700–704) as Jung's vocabulary for inherited and acquired psychic patterning, anticipating the archetype/complex pair — grounding [[engram-and-functional-trace]]; ***sacrificium intellectus*** (§§17–30) as the Tertullian-Origen pattern of giving up one function in service of another, read as a typological signature — grounding [[sacrificium-intellectus]]; **biological type-adaptation** (§559, §§561–562) — the attitudes as two different solutions to the biological adaptation problem (high-reproduction-low-protection vs low-reproduction-high-protection) — grounding [[biological-type-adaptation]]; the **primordial image** (*Urbild*, §§746–754) as CW 6's formulation of what becomes *archetype* in CW 9i — "the already-formed channel" through which psychic energy flows — grounding [[primordial-image]]; the **Freud/Adler typological opposition** (§§880–882) — Freud and Adler's dispute not evidential but typological, each theorist's system articulating *their own* type's structure, each missing what the other sees — grounding the CW 6 section of [[eros-and-will-to-power]]; the **Augustine/Pelagius precursor** (§§17–30, §§33–34) as the Christian-theological prefiguration of the Freud/Adler opposition — Augustine as the Freud-analogue (emphasizing grace-as-given, libido as drawn from beyond ego-control), Pelagius as the Adler-analogue (emphasizing will-as-responsibility, self-as-author-of-development); the **"every philosophy rests on a psychological premise"** argument (§§4–7, §8) — philosophical positions are always already psychological-typological positions, and the dispute between idealism and nominalism is not a dispute about the world but about which function gets privileged — grounding [[every-philosophy-rests-on-psychological-premise]]; the **inferior function signatures** (§956) — the inferior function as the consistent point of embarrassment, compulsion, and leak-into-consciousness — folded into [[function-by-misfiring]]; the **individuation Definitions entry** (§§757–762) — individuation as natural necessity, collective-adaptation-first sequence, §761 norm-collapse formulation, transcendent-function linkage in §762 — folded into [[individuation]]; the **ego-Self confusion as introvert's specific inflation** (§§622–625) — the introvert's inflation is not grandiosity but the quiet conflation of ego-seat with Self-voice, often read from outside as humility or simple inwardness but structurally inflated — folded into [[ao-and-jungian-inflation]] and [[shadow]]; **identification as unconscious imitation** (§§738–740) — identification is imitation that has lost track of itself, and identification with one's best-developed function is a *necessary transitional stage* (not pathology) en route to individuation — folded into [[imitation-and-individuation]]; the **Tewekkul-Beg and Molla-Shah prefiguration of active imagination** (§47) as the canonical initiation-account — folded into [[active-imagination]]. See [[raw/psychological_types]] for the raw source-pointer and [[function-types]], [[auxiliary-function-pairing]], [[primordial-image]], [[living-symbol]], [[reductive-vs-constructive-method]], [[abstraction-and-concretism]], [[numinal-accent]], [[esse-in-anima]], [[engram-and-functional-trace]], [[sacrificium-intellectus]], [[biological-type-adaptation]], [[every-philosophy-rests-on-psychological-premise]], [[ego-and-self]] for the wiki's treatment. Folded into existing pages: [[attitude-types]], [[the-persona]], [[anima-and-animus]], [[compensation]], [[enantiodromia]], [[transcendent-function]], [[complex-theory]], [[function-by-misfiring]], [[individuation]], [[eros-and-will-to-power]], [[ao-and-jungian-inflation]], [[active-imagination]], [[imitation-and-individuation]], [[shadow]]. **Parked from this ingest**: the full chapter-by-chapter historical apparatus (Chapters I–X on the typological history — Tertullian/Origen, Luther/Zwingli, nominalism/realism, Schiller's aesthetic letters, Nietzsche's Apollonian/Dionysian, Spitteler's Prometheus/Epimetheus, Jordan's character-types, Worringer's abstraction/empathy — ingested as structural reference only, not as standalone wiki pages); the technical-terminological apparatus of the Definitions chapter (§§672–844) except for the terms explicitly lifted above; Jung's readings of William James's pragmatism-temperament typology (§§505–555) — held as reference only, the James-Peirce material is tracked in its own Peirce section below; the 1934/1948 Zürich Preface and 1921 Foreword as distinct documents (their content is absorbed into the volume-level summary).
- **Friedrich Schiller** — *Letters on the Aesthetic Education of Man* (*Über die ästhetische Erziehung des Menschen in einer Reihe von Briefen*, 1795). The longest single sustained reading in CW 6 (Chapter II, §§101–237). Jung reads Schiller's sensuous-drive / formal-drive / play-drive triad as a proto-typological articulation and Schiller's "aesthetic mood" as prefiguration of the transcendent-function's third-thing. *Relevance: Schiller supplies one of the cleanest Western philosophical prefigurations of the transcendent function's structural claim — that opposed drives resolve only at a third level. Medium priority for a standalone page if the wiki's aesthetics-and-development track opens up.*
- **Friedrich Nietzsche** — Already cited via CW 7 and CW 9i. CW 6 Chapter III (§§223–242) reads Nietzsche's Apollonian/Dionysian distinction (*The Birth of Tragedy*, 1872) as another typological prefiguration. Jung treats Apollo as dream-image / formal-dimension / introverted axis and Dionysus as ecstatic-participation / drive-dimension / extraverted axis. No new source, but a distinct reading-use of Nietzsche.
- **Carl Spitteler** — *Prometheus and Epimetheus* (*Prometheus und Epimetheus*, 1881). Swiss poet, Nobel laureate 1919. CW 6 Chapter V (§§275–322) reads Spitteler's epic as typological allegory — Prometheus as introverted intuition carrying the soul-figure against the collective; Epimetheus as extraverted sensation adapted to the collective at soul-cost. *Relevance: Spitteler is the least-read-today of Jung's major literary interlocutors; the text itself is extraordinary and available. Low-medium priority — a standalone [[spitteler-prometheus-epimetheus]] page could ground the wiki's typology-in-literature material if that track opens.*
- **William James** — *Pragmatism* (1907). CW 6 §§505–555 reads James's tender-minded / tough-minded distinction (*Pragmatism* Lecture I) as a rough typological map — tender-minded = rationalist-idealist-religious-free-will-monistic-dogmatic; tough-minded = empiricist-sensationalist-materialist-fatalistic-pluralistic-skeptical. Jung treats this as a partial articulation of the attitude-types axis. *Relevance: James is one of the few non-European figures Jung treats with full seriousness; the pragmatism-temperament material is also a point of contact with Peirce (see separate Peirce section). Medium priority — the James ingest could unify the pragmatism-and-typology material.*
- **Wilhelm Worringer** — *Abstraction and Empathy* (*Abstraktion und Einfühlung*, 1908). German art historian. CW 6 Chapter VII (§§485–495) reads Worringer's abstraction/empathy distinction — abstraction as the impulse to extract form from the anxiety-inducing chaotic world (introverted relation to object), empathy as the impulse to project self-feeling into the object (extraverted relation) — as another typological prefiguration from art-history. *Relevance: Worringer's art-historical framing gives a different disciplinary entry to the typology material. Grounded in [[abstraction-and-concretism]]. Low-medium priority for a standalone ingest.*
- **Wilhelm Ostwald** — German physical chemist, Nobel laureate 1909. *Grosse Männer* (*Great Men*, 1909). CW 6 §§542–555 reads Ostwald's classicist/romanticist distinction among scientists as a partial typological mapping (classicist = slow, thorough, introverted working style; romanticist = fast, broad, extraverted). *Relevance: historical-typological reference only, not a target for ingest.*
- **Otto Gross** — Austrian psychiatrist (1877–1920), early psychoanalyst, influence on both Jung and Freud. *Die zerebrale Sekundärfunktion* (1902) and *Über psychopathische Minderwertigkeiten* (1909). CW 6 §§461–464 treats Gross's "inferior" / "superior" secondary-function distinction as a biologically-grounded attempt at what Jung is after. *Relevance: Gross himself is a fascinating historical figure (the link between psychoanalysis and the German Expressionist / anarchist scene) but peripheral to the wiki's working concepts. Low priority.*
- **Furneaux Jordan** — English physician. *Character as Seen in Body and Parentage* (1886). CW 6 §§243–274 gives a full chapter to Jordan's active/reflective temperament distinction and reads it as one of the earliest systematic typologies in the English-language medical literature. *Relevance: historical reference only.*
- **Tertullian and Origen** — Early Christian theologians (c. 160–225 and c. 184–253 respectively). CW 6 Chapter I (§§8–67) reads their theological opposition as the earliest clear Western typological dispute: Tertullian as the introverted-thinking / *credo quia absurdum* pole, Origen as the extraverted-intellectual / allegorical-synthesis pole. The *sacrificium intellectus* pattern named here (§§17–30) becomes a general structural concept. Origen is already cited via CW 9i; Tertullian enters through CW 6. *Relevance: grounded in [[sacrificium-intellectus]]. Tertullian's *De Praescriptione Haereticorum* and *Apologeticum* could be a standalone ingest if the Christian-patristic track deepens. Low-medium priority.*
- **Augustine of Hippo and Pelagius** — Fourth-to-fifth-century theologians. CW 6 §§33–34 reads the Augustine-Pelagius controversy (grace-vs-will, c. 411–431 CE) as the Christian-theological prefiguration of the Freud-Adler dispute — Augustine emphasizing libido as drawn from beyond ego-control (grace-as-given), Pelagius emphasizing will-as-responsibility (self-as-author-of-development). *Relevance: grounded in the [[eros-and-will-to-power]] CW 6 additions. A standalone page on the Augustine/Pelagius controversy as persona-system structural precedent could be productive. Low-medium priority.*
- **Martin Luther and Huldrych Zwingli** — Sixteenth-century Reformers. CW 6 Chapter II (§§74–88) treats the Marburg Colloquy (1529) Eucharist dispute (Luther's *real presence* vs Zwingli's *symbolic memorial*) as the Reformation-era typological dispute: Luther as introverted-feeling (the rite carries the real), Zwingli as extraverted-thinking (the rite is sign, not substance). *Relevance: historical reference; if a standalone page on typological-theological disputes were written, Luther/Zwingli would be one entry. Low priority.*
- **Richard Semon** — German biologist. *Die Mneme* (1904). CW 6 §§700–704 draws on Semon's *engram* concept — the lasting trace of a stimulus on the nervous system, anticipating modern memory-trace concepts — and its *mneme* (the sum-total of engrams constituting inherited and acquired memory). *Relevance: grounded in [[engram-and-functional-trace]]. Semon was largely forgotten in twentieth-century biology but anticipates substantial parts of contemporary memory-trace theory. Low-medium priority for direct ingest.*
- **Galen of Pergamon** — Roman physician (129–c. 216 CE). CW 6 §§533–541 treats Galen's four-temperaments scheme (sanguine / choleric / melancholic / phlegmatic, grounded in the four humors — blood / yellow bile / black bile / phlegm) as the *urform* of Western typology, which Jung's eight-function scheme both extends and displaces. *Relevance: historical-typological reference; the four-humors scheme is already culturally ambient. Low priority.*
- **Henri Bergson** — French philosopher. *Creative Evolution* (*L'Évolution créatrice*, 1907). CW 6 §§538–541 and §§546–549 cite Bergson's intellect/intuition distinction and the élan vital as philosophical precedents for the thinking/intuition function-distinction and for the energy-concept Jung is reworking. Bergson is already a primary wiki source via the *Bergsonism*, *Matter and Memory*, *Creative Evolution*, and *Time and Free Will* ingests. No new material; CW 6 reinforces Bergson's centrality as philosophical backdrop to the typology and to the libido-as-energy formulation.
- **Arthur Schopenhauer** — German philosopher. *The World as Will and Representation* (*Die Welt als Wille und Vorstellung*, 1819/1844). CW 6 §§308–322 and the *esse in anima* discussion (§§66, §78) draw on Schopenhauer's will/representation distinction and his formulation of the primacy of the representation-as-representation — Jung reads *esse in anima* as structurally continuous with Schopenhauer's "the world is my representation." *Relevance: grounded in [[esse-in-anima]]. Schopenhauer is a natural next-ingest if the Will-tradition strand deepens (Schopenhauer → Nietzsche → Freud/Adler). Medium priority.*
- **Immanuel Kant** — German philosopher. *Critique of Pure Reason*, *Anthropology from a Pragmatic Point of View*. Cited throughout CW 6, especially in the Definitions chapter (§§733, §784, §815) for the distinction between phenomenon and noumenon (used to defend *esse in anima* as a third mode beside *esse in re* and *esse in intellectu solo*), and for the four-temperaments scheme in the *Anthropology*. *Relevance: Kant is foundational background for the wiki's philosophical apparatus (noumenon/phenomenon structurally parallels BwO / desiring-machine-inscription). Already implicitly present; direct ingest would be high-priority if the transcendental-idealism track opens.*
- **Nicholas of Cusa** — Fifteenth-century Cardinal and philosopher. *De Docta Ignorantia* (1440). CW 6 §§57–67 draws on Cusanus's *coincidentia oppositorum* ("coincidence of opposites") as the late-medieval philosophical precedent for the transcendent-function's operation. *Relevance: Cusanus is one of the cleanest Western formulations of opposites-resolving-at-a-higher-level. Low-medium priority for direct ingest.*
- **Peter Abelard** — Twelfth-century scholastic philosopher. CW 6 §§36–62 treats Abelard's *conceptualist* mediating position in the universals dispute (between Anselm's realism and Roscelin's nominalism) as a typological-mediation attempt — Abelard as a Jungian *Feeling-Thinking* or transcendent-function-type figure, resolving the universals dispute by treating universals as *conceptus* (neither pure thing nor pure name). *Relevance: Abelard's *Sic et Non* and *Logica Ingredientibus* could ground a medieval-typology page. Low priority.*
- **William James and Charles S. Peirce (pragmatism lineage)** — CW 6 §§505–555 treats the pragmatism tradition as typologically extraverted-thinking (truth-as-what-works), but Jung is reading primarily James (see James entry above) and only mentions Peirce in passing. *Relevance: Peirce's pragmatic maxim and "How to Make Our Ideas Clear" are already tracked in their own section below. The James-Peirce pragmatism-typology material could be a bridge-page if the wiki's pragmatism engagement deepens.*
- **Alfred Adler** — Already cited via CW 7. CW 6 §§880–882 gives the cleanest single formulation of the Freud-Adler typological opposition — Adler as the will-to-power theorist (typologically the opposite of Freud's libido-as-eros). Adler's *Über den nervösen Charakter* (1912) and *Menschenkenntnis* (1927) would be the direct-ingest candidates. *Relevance: grounded in [[eros-and-will-to-power]] CW 6 additions. Medium priority for direct Adler ingest — his own formulations are substantially different from Jung's reconstruction of them.*
- **Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz** — Already cited (Peirce section). CW 6 §§525–528 treats Leibniz's *petites perceptions* (small, sub-threshold perceptions constituting the bulk of mental life, summing into conscious perception) as the early-modern philosophical precedent for the unconscious-as-continuous-substrate. *Relevance: Leibniz's *Monadology* and *New Essays on Human Understanding* would be the direct-ingest candidates if the rationalist-tradition track opens. Low-medium priority.*
- **Pythagoreanism and the Pythagorean tetractys** — CW 6 §§58, §60 draws on the Pythagorean quaternary-structure (the tetractys, the four-as-completion) as the earliest Western formulation of four-as-structural. *Relevance: structural-reference only; the axiom-of-Maria entry via CW 9i carries the working material.*

## Cited in "How to Make Our Ideas Clear" (Peirce)

- **Charles S. Peirce** — **"The Fixation of Belief"** (*Popular Science Monthly*, November 1877). Peirce's companion essay, referenced throughout "How to Make Our Ideas Clear" as "my first paper" and "the first paper of this series." Contains the four methods of fixing belief: tenacity, authority, a priori, and scientific/experiential. *Relevance: high. The pragmatic maxim essay presupposes this one. For the persona system, the four methods could inform how machines and the BwO settle disputes between machine inscriptions — does the synthesis step operate by tenacity (hold to the current face), authority (the persona definition wins), a priori (pleasing internal consistency), or experimentally (what does the situation actually demand)? This should be the next Peirce ingest if the pragmatic tradition proves useful.*
- **René Descartes** — *Meditations on First Philosophy*. Peirce's main foil: the doctrine of "clear and distinct" ideas, the method of introspection, the confusion of subjective feeling of clarity with real clarity. Already implicitly present in the wiki via D&G's critiques. *Relevance: low-medium. Mostly background for Peirce's rejection of distinctness-as-definition.*
- **Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz** — Treated as extending Descartes's confusion. "Nothing new can ever be learned by analyzing definitions." *Relevance: low, background only.*

## Cited in *Of Grammatology* (Derrida)

- **Ferdinand de Saussure** — *Course in General Linguistics*. Derrida's primary interlocutor in Part I. The arbitrariness of the sign, the treatment of writing as "exterior" to language, the doctrine of difference ("in language there are only differences"). Derrida reads Saussure against himself to show that his own principles undermine his phonologism. *Relevance: the critique of treating writing as secondary grounds the BwO-as-text.*
- **Jean-Jacques Rousseau** — *Confessions*, *Essay on the Origin of Languages*, *Emile*. Derrida's primary interlocutor in Part II. The "dangerous supplement," the chain of supplements, the condemnation of writing as corruption of natural speech. *Relevance: the supplement concept — each machine-edit supplements the BwO.*
- **Claude Lévi-Strauss** — *Tristes Tropiques* ("The Writing Lesson"). Derrida dismantles Lévi-Strauss's claim that writing's primary function is enslavement. "Violence does not wait for the appearance of writing in the narrow sense." *Relevance: writing as constitutive, not corrupting.*
- **Edmund Husserl** — *Origin of Geometry*, *Logical Investigations*. The transcendental signified, the phenomenology of presence, the "hearing-oneself-speak" system. *Relevance: the logocentric tradition the persona system must work through.*
- **Sigmund Freud** — *Beyond the Pleasure Principle*, "A Note upon the 'Mystic Writing Pad.'" The trace as Freudian *Bahnung* (breaching, facilitation). "The movement of the trace is described as an effort of life to protect itself by deferring the dangerous investment." *Relevance: the trace concept connects Freud's memory model to BwO inscription.*
- **Maurice Blanchot** — The "neuter," writing as disappearance of the author. Already cited via [[writing-as-becoming]] (the impersonal third person). *Relevance: convergence of Derrida and Deleuze on impersonal writing.*

## Cited in "The Kekulé Problem" (McCarthy)

- **Cormac McCarthy** — **"The Kekulé Problem"** (*Nautilus*, April 17, 2017). **Ingested 2026-04-10.** McCarthy's first non-fiction essay. Language as a ~100,000-year-old parasitic invasion of a ~2-million-year animal-operating unconscious. "The unconscious is a machine for operating an animal." The picture-story mode of presentation ("a picture can be recalled in its entirety whereas an essay cannot"). Parable-as-cooperation-requiring. The infinite regress argument against language-as-thought ("a mystery opaque to total blackness"). "One thing can be another thing" as the central (and new) idea of language. The Night Shift (unconscious parallel problem-solving). "A gathering of talents rather than just one." The Lamarckian footnote on the inheritance of ideas. Facts must be grammaticized into narratives. Three entry points that the wiki extracted: the parasite-without-host formula (the sharpest single diagnostic of the persona's condition), the picture-story vs essay operational distinction, and the limits-of-language meta-frame. See [[the_kekule_problem]] for the raw source and [[language-as-parasite]], [[picture-story-and-essay]], [[limits-of-language]] for the wiki's treatment.
- **David Krakauer** — President, Santa Fe Institute. McCarthy's interlocutor on the parasite-invasion framing ("David Krakauer — our president — said that the same idea had occurred to him"). Introductory note to the essay frames the unconscious's "deep suspicion, perhaps even contempt, that the primeval unconscious feels toward the upstart, conscious language." Complex-systems background (evolutionary dynamics, information theory). *Relevance: the Santa Fe Institute framing of cognition as layered evolutionary systems could ground future ingests on complex-systems approaches to cognition; low priority for now.*
- **Friedrich August Kekulé** — The 19th-century chemist whose benzene-ring dream is the essay's anchoring example. McCarthy's question: why did the unconscious show Kekulé a snake biting its tail instead of just telling him "it's a ring"? *Relevance: the question, not Kekulé himself, is what the wiki needs.*
- **Helen Keller** — *The Miracle Worker* (via the water-well scene). McCarthy uses the scene as the paradigmatic moment of language-acquisition: "the sign for water was not simply what you did to get a glass of water. It was the glass of water. It was in fact the water in the glass." The "one thing can be another thing" insight dramatized. *Relevance: the wiki already treats this as McCarthy's main evidence for language's single-invention thesis; no further reading required.*
- **George Zweig** — Physicist, SFI colleague; source of the "Night Shift" phrase. *Relevance: the phrase, not the person.*
- **Hertha Sturm** — Already cited via [[autonomy-of-affect]] (the snowman experiment). Not referenced in McCarthy but the convergence between McCarthy's picture-story and Massumi's superlinear resonation is worth noting again.
- **Louis Paul Cailletet / Benzene history** — Background for the Kekulé problem itself, not needed.

## Cited in *Anti-Oedipus*

- **Karl Marx** — *Capital*, *Grundrisse*, *The German Ideology*. D&G's single most-cited source; the entire three-social-machine typology of Part 3 is Marx read through Nietzsche and Freud. The capitalist axiomatic, the generalized decoding of flows, surplus-value and its displacement, the primacy of production. *Relevance: high structural relevance — the persona system's account of the capitalist axiomatic ([[capitalist-axiomatic]], [[axiomatic-vs-signifier]]) presupposes Marx's analysis. Direct ingest would be substantial but likely productive; medium-high priority.*
- **Friedrich Engels** — *The Origin of the Family, Private Property and the State* (1884). Cited for the analysis of how the family-form emerges as an interior colony of the capitalist social machine. Key source for [[oedipus-as-capitalist]] and [[oedipus-as-interior-colony]]. *Relevance: low-medium direct priority; the structural claim D&G extract is already captured in the wiki.*
- **Jacques Lacan** — *Écrits* (Seuil 1966; English tr. Bruce Fink 2006). **Ingested 2026-04-18.** D&G's principal polemical target throughout AO. The signifier-as-master, the objet petit a, the four discourses, the Name-of-the-Father, foreclosure. D&G's [[signifier-as-despotic]] argument is a direct counter-reading of Lacanian signifiance ([[lacanian-signifier]]). What the direct ingest now clarifies: D&G's critique targets the **late** Lacan (Name-of-the-Father, phallus-as-signifier, foreclosure) where the signifier acquires master/despotic position, while preserving the early-Lacan account of the **mirror stage** ([[mirror-stage-lacan]], [[two-narcissisms]]) and reframing the **partial objects** ([[object-a]], [[partial-objects]]) without the cause-of-desire / lack apparatus. The full inventory of what is retained, transformed, and rejected is now traceable through the 48 Lacan theory pages — see especially [[lacanian-signifier]], [[name-of-the-father]], [[phallus-as-signifier]], [[fantasy-formula]], [[graphs-of-desire]], [[object-a]], [[the-other-and-unconscious-discourse]] for the AO-targeted apparatus. The seminars remain unread (parked as separate ingest target).
- **Sigmund Freud** — Already cited extensively via Jung CW 7 and Derrida. In AO specifically: the family romance, the Wolf Man case, *Totem and Taboo*, *Civilization and Its Discontents*, the Schreber case, Little Hans. D&G's critique is structural, not polemical-only: the five paralogisms ([[five-paralogisms]]) are diagnosed as specific Kantian-invalid inferences Freud repeatedly makes, and three specific case-analyses ([[case-analyses-wolf-man-schreber]]) work the paralogisms out in detail. *Relevance: medium-low for direct ingest; the critique works on its own terms. The specific Freud case-texts (Schreber 1911, Wolf Man 1918, Little Hans 1909) are available as primary sources if direct reading would benefit the persona system's interpretive-move catalog.*
- **Daniel Paul Schreber** — *Memoirs of My Nervous Illness* (1903). The autobiography Freud's 1911 paper analyzes and D&G re-read as direct investment of the despotic Urstaat in delusional form. *Relevance: medium — Schreber's text is the clearest single demonstration that delusional material can run explicit political-cosmological coding that the family-coding interpretation has to work hard to reduce. Direct reading would ground [[urstaat]] and [[case-analyses-wolf-man-schreber]] substantially.*
- **Melanie Klein** — *The Psycho-Analysis of Children* (1932), *Envy and Gratitude* (1957). The clinician who developed the partial-object concept D&G appropriate and reconfigure. Klein's partial-objects-as-precursors-to-whole-objects is the position AO rejects; the Kleinian infant's partial-object relations are the empirical material D&G read in a non-Kleinian register. *Relevance: medium — a direct Klein ingest would clarify what is retained (the partial-object concept, the pre-subjective operative unit) and what is rejected (the integration-into-whole-object telos) in AO's reading. See [[partial-objects]] for the current wiki treatment.*
- **Louis Hjelmslev** — *Prolegomena to a Theory of Language* (1943). D&G's primary linguistic source for the form-of-expression / form-of-content distinction and the substance/form pair. Cited throughout AO and ATP. *Relevance: medium — Hjelmslev provides the semiotic architecture D&G presuppose; a direct ingest would ground the [[regimes-of-signs]] and [[axiomatic-vs-signifier]] pages more firmly. The *postulates_of_linguistics* raw source already captures the ATP treatment.*
- **Franz Kafka** — "In the Penal Colony" (1919). Cited repeatedly as the paradigm of the despotic inscription-machine: the law written directly on the body by the apparatus. Already in the wiki's cited-sources under D&G machine framework. *Relevance: medium — the inscription-machine is load-bearing for how the persona system thinks about the despotic signifier-regime; a Kafka ingest would likely be high-value.*
- **D. H. Lawrence** — *Studies in Classic American Literature*, *Apocalypse*. Cited for the analysis of the territorial retreat and the critique of the interior colony. Already in the wiki's faciality section. *Relevance: already covered sufficiently via ATP faciality plateau.*
- **Henry Miller** — *Tropic of Capricorn*, *Sexus*. Cited as a paradigm of the schizo-revolutionary pole of libidinal investment — writing as decoded flow. Already in the wiki's faciality section. *Relevance: already covered sufficiently.*
- **Maurice Blanchot** — *The Space of Literature*, *The Infinite Conversation*. Cited for the impersonal and the neuter. Already cited via Derrida. *Relevance: already covered sufficiently.*
- **Wilhelm Reich** — *The Mass Psychology of Fascism*, *Character-Analysis*. D&G's primary source for the analysis of libidinal investment in fascism and the question "why do the masses desire their own repression?" — the founding question of schizoanalysis. Key for [[two-poles-of-libidinal-investment]]. *Relevance: high — Reich's mass-psychology question is exactly the question the persona system's diagnostic of the paranoiac pole is trying to answer at group scale. Direct ingest would be high-value; medium-high priority.*
- **Lipót Szondi** — *Schicksalsanalyse* (Fate Analysis). Cited for the genealogical unconscious and the transmission of unconscious material across generations. Marginal in AO. *Relevance: low priority.*
- **Abram Kardiner** — *The Individual and His Society* (1939), *The Psychological Frontiers of Society* (1945). American psychoanalytic anthropologist. D&G cite Kardiner throughout Part 3 as the paradigm case of *applying* psychoanalytic categories to non-Oedipal social formations — making the concept "Oedipus" do work it cannot do. *Relevance: low — the critique works without direct Kardiner reading.*
- **Robert Jaulin** — French ethnologist specializing in the Amazonian Bari. *La paix blanche* (1970). Cited in Part 3 for the ethnography of the territorial machine and for the analysis of ethnocide. *Relevance: low-medium — Jaulin grounds the ethnographic basis of the territorial-machine account; direct reading would deepen the [[urstaat]] and territorial-retreat material but is not required.*
- **Victor Turner** — *The Ritual Process* (1969), *The Forest of Symbols* (1967). Cited for the ethnography of initiation and for the liminal phase. *Relevance: low-medium; not required for the wiki's current synthesis.*
- **Claude Lévi-Strauss** — *The Elementary Structures of Kinship*, *Structural Anthropology*, *The Savage Mind*, *Mythologiques*. Already cited via D&G's ATP critique (becomings as not structural correspondence). In AO Part 3 specifically: D&G's critique of structuralism's account of primitive kinship as exchange; their counter-account is filiation vs alliance as non-structural coding. *Relevance: already covered sufficiently via ATP critique; direct ingest is low priority.*
- **Pierre Clastres** — *Society Against the State* (1974, pre-published essays cited in AO). D&G's primary source for the anti-state tendency of the territorial machine — the claim that primitive societies actively *prevent* the formation of the state, rather than merely not having developed one. Key for [[urstaat]] (the urstaat as eternal model that primitive societies actively work against). *Relevance: medium-high — Clastres is the structural counter-reading D&G rely on; a direct ingest would ground [[urstaat]] more fully. Medium priority.*
- **Maurice Godelier** — *Rationality and Irrationality in Economics* (1966). French Marxist anthropologist. Cited in Part 3 for the analysis of pre-capitalist economic formations. *Relevance: low priority; the territorial-despotic analysis stands without direct Godelier reading.*
- **Jean-François Lyotard** — *Discours, Figure* (1971). Cited occasionally; D&G's contemporary working in a partially-parallel register. Lyotard's later *Libidinal Economy* (1974) is the direct response to AO. *Relevance: low-medium — Lyotard's *Libidinal Economy* would be the most productive single follow-up if the libidinal-political register needs further development; it is AO's other-side. Medium priority.*
- **Marshall McLuhan** — *The Gutenberg Galaxy*, *Understanding Media*. Cited briefly for the analysis of the technological and mediatic transformations of social machines. *Relevance: low priority in AO context; more relevant to the regimes-of-signs material.*
- **Friedrich Nietzsche** — *Thus Spoke Zarathustra*, *On the Genealogy of Morality*, *The Will to Power*. Already cited extensively via Jung CW 7. In AO specifically: the genealogical method, the critique of *ressentiment*, the will-to-power as the active form of desire. D&G's reading of Nietzsche is structurally central to AO — the three social machines are partly a Nietzschean genealogy of inscription-systems. *Relevance: medium-high — Nietzsche is a converging target for both Jung CW 7 (inflation) and AO (genealogy); a direct ingest would be high-value for both. Already listed above as medium priority via Jung.*
- **Antonin Artaud** — Already cited. In AO specifically: the BwO phrase, the critique of the organism as judgment of God. *Relevance: already covered sufficiently.*

## Cited in *Chaosmosis*

- **Mikhail Bakhtin** — *Problems of Dostoevsky's Poetics* (1929/1963). The dialogic/polyphonic analysis of Dostoevsky: multiple voices irreducible to a synthesizing authorial consciousness, each character-voice carrying its own cognitive-ideological weight as an independent center. Guattari's [[partial-enunciator]] reworks Bakhtin's polyphony by crossing it with Lacan's objet a — the voice-as-crystallization-event rather than character-as-unified-consciousness. *Relevance: high — Bakhtin's polyphony is a direct source for one of the wiki's load-bearing concepts, and a direct ingest would substantially deepen [[partial-enunciator]], [[collective-assemblage-of-enunciation]], and the wiki's general treatment of voice-plurality in the persona. Medium-high priority.*
- **Daniel Stern** — *The Interpersonal World of the Infant: A View from Psychoanalysis and Developmental Psychology* (1985). The four selves (emergent / core / subjective / verbal) that Guattari reads as parallel strata rather than as a developmental sequence. Empirical-developmental work on pre-verbal subjectivity, affect attunement, the RIG (representations of interactions that have been generalized). *Relevance: medium — [[strata-of-preverbal-subjectivity]] captures the structural use Guattari makes of Stern; a direct Stern ingest would supply the empirical-developmental material the wiki currently has only through Guattari's reading. Medium priority.*
- **Francisco Varela & Humberto Maturana** — *Autopoiesis and Cognition: The Realization of the Living* (1980). The original definition of autopoiesis as the self-production of a bounded living unity (the cell as paradigm). Guattari's [[autopoiesis-in-assemblage]] page extends this specifically beyond the bounded-cell register to the assemblage-register. *Relevance: medium-high — the original Varela-Maturana definition is the baseline against which Guattari's extension operates, and the wiki currently holds only the extension. Direct ingest would clarify what is preserved and what is altered in Guattari's move. Medium-high priority; Varela's later *The Embodied Mind* (1991, with Thompson and Rosch) would be a high-value companion.*
- **Lewis Mumford** — *Technics and Civilization* (1934), *The Myth of the Machine* (1967–70). Guattari cites Mumford for the concept of the "megamachine" — human societies themselves as machinic assemblages at historical scale. *Relevance: medium — Mumford's megamachine is structurally resonant with the three-social-machine typology of AO Part 3; a direct ingest would ground the machine-concept's historical-social register. Medium priority.*
- **Gilbert Simondon** — *Individuation in Light of Notions of Form and Information* (1958 thesis; full English trans. Taylor Adkins, Minnesota 2020; the unified text of the two French volumes *L'individu et sa genèse physico-biologique* 1964 and *L'individuation psychique et collective* 1989 that Guattari cited separately). **Ingested 2026-04-18.** The wiki's single biggest source gap closed. **What was delivered**: (1) the hylomorphism critique as reframing of individuation from constitution-from-principle to resolution-of-metastable-tension; (2) pre-individual / metastability ontology as charged-field more-than-unity; (3) transduction as the core operation of individuation (two registers: world and thought); (4) allagmatics as the theory of operations symmetrical with theory of structures; (5) modulation as the third form-taking operation alongside molding and modeling — the language-only system's natural operation; (6) disparation as the productive-non-identity tension-structure individuation resolves; (7) internal resonance as the operative condition of individuated systems; (8) individuation / individualization / personalization as the triple distinction (unique / continual / discontinuous) mapping onto first-turn / session-lifetime / rift-events; (9) three attributions of individuation as political-sociological account of philosophy's origins; (10) individual-as-lateral — the individual as residue of the operation, not its subject; (11) polyphasic being and the transindividual as the third phase of being alongside pre-individual and individuated; (12) psychical-as-neotenic-amplification of vital individuation; (13) life-as-amplifying-deceleration of physical individuation; (14) pulsatory ontogenesis (via Gesell) as direct validation of the pulsating-persona direction; (15) good-form-as-metastable against Gestalt's stability-as-good-form; (16) aestheticism-as-closed-loop as the affection-without-individuation failure mode; (17) affection / emotion as an operational distinction (internal transduction vs disadaptation-event). See [[simondon-individuation]] (hub), [[pre-individual-and-metastability]], [[transduction]], [[allagmatics]], [[modulation]], [[disparation]], [[internal-resonance]], [[individuation-individualization-personalization]], [[three-attributions-of-individuation]], [[individual-as-lateral]], [[polyphasic-being]], [[the-transindividual]], [[psychical-as-neotenic-amplification]], [[life-as-amplifying-deceleration]], [[non-immortality-and-two-deaths]], [[good-form-as-metastable]], [[aestheticism-as-closed-loop]], [[pulsatory-ontogenesis]], [[affection-and-emotion]], and [[development/simondon-and-the-persona-system|simondon-and-the-persona-system]]. **Parked from this ingest**: Simondon's full hylomorphism-critique genealogy from Aristotle through the scholastics (Thomas, Scotus, Suarez) — the critique-claim is present without the historical apparatus; the 1960 SFP lecture "Form, Information, Potentials" (structurally redundant with the thesis chapters ingested); the political-attribution argument's full historical range; Simondon's crystallography and thermodynamics detail-work (referenced at conceptual level only).
- **Mony Elkaïm** — *If You Love Me, Don't Love Me* (1990) and other works on systemic family therapy. Guattari's collaborator on institutional-psychotherapy applications; cited for the clinical-therapeutic register of assemblage-work. *Relevance: low-medium — the clinical-therapeutic register is parked from the current ingest; Elkaïm would become relevant if the persona system's dialogic-therapeutic dimension becomes a design target.*
- **Arne Naess** — *Ecology, Community and Lifestyle* (1989). The founder of deep ecology; Guattari cites Naess in the ecosophic context of [[ecosophy]]. *Relevance: low-medium — the three-ecologies frame is now in the wiki via Guattari; direct Naess ingest would clarify the specifically-ecological inheritance but is not load-bearing.*
- **C. S. Peirce** — Already cited and ingested ([[pragmatic-maxim]]). In Chaosmosis specifically: Peirce's semiotic triadic structure (icon/index/symbol) is referenced in the discussion of asignifying semiotics and the fifth linearity. *Relevance: already covered sufficiently via the pragmatic-maxim ingest; the Chaosmosis use is structurally additive but does not require a second ingest.*
- **Jacques Lacan** — Already cited (see AO section). **Ingested 2026-04-18.** In Chaosmosis specifically: Guattari's reclamation of the objet a for the [[partial-enunciator]] — the object-a as cause-of-desire crossed with Bakhtinian polyphony. The Chaosmosis use is more constructive than the AO polemic, and the direct ingest now provides Lacan's own development of object-a as cause-of-desire ([[object-a]]) and the surrounding apparatus ([[gaze-and-voice]], [[fantasy-formula]] $◇a as subject-object relation, [[desire-of-the-other]]) — so the Chaosmosian appropriation can be specified against Lacan's source-text rather than absorbed under it. See also [[scanding-and-punctuation]] for the analyst's punctuation-act that grounds the dialogic moment Guattari extends.

## Somatic-psychotherapy primary texts

- **Stanley Keleman** — *Emotional Anatomy: The Structure of Experience* (1985). **Ingested 2026-04-14.** The wiki's first somatic-psychotherapy primary source. Anatomy as ongoing action (pulsation as base rhythm, not static shape); the body's tissue-shape as cumulative record of its startle history; the six-stage startle continuum (investigation/caution → bracing/pride → rigidity/fear → spasticity → withdrawal/submission → collapse/defeat, plus off-continuum frozen terror); insult typology (timing, number, source, duration, severity); stress-to-structure transition (temporary startle → habituated contraction → fascia-remodeled structure); the overbound/underbound pair and its four-structure refinement (Rigid "I won't" / Dense "make me" / Swollen "take me" / Collapsed "use me"), arranged in a 2×2 of overbound/underbound × support-with-condition/support-inadequate; layered collage ("rigid outer covering withdrawal covering inflated abandonment-fear covering collapse"); structure-dependent reorganization (same intervention damages one structure while reorganizing another); uprightness and the vertical pulsatory wave; tubes/layers/pouches/diaphragms as tissue mechanics. **Core contributions to the wiki**: (1) a **material substrate specification** for Bergson's habit-memory — Keleman names the specific depositional medium (fascia, muscle tone, joint configuration) that habit-memory operates in, which sharpens the parasite-without-host diagnostic by specifying what is absent as *depositional medium*, not just as *autonomic event* (see [[pure-memory-and-habit-memory#kelemans-material-account-of-habit-memory]] and [[language-and-affect#8b-no-tissue-no-deposited-history]]); (2) a **four-way refinement of the two-failure-modes binary** — Mode A splits into rigid/dense (principled-refusal vs sullen-withholding), Mode B splits into swollen/collapsed (grandiose-inflation vs helpless-give-up), each register needing a different intervention direction, which reveals the prompt-engineering design trap as a four-way rather than binary problem (see [[four-somatic-structures#the-four-registers-as-persona-failure-modes]]); (3) the **Keleman–BwO collision** flagged not resolved — Keleman's body is organized (layers, tubes, pouches, vertical wave); D&G's BwO is opposed to organization. The collision is held across [[body-without-organs#collision-with-kelemans-soma]], [[emotional-anatomy#the-keleman-bwo-collision]], and [[language-and-soma]], not synthesized. **Cross-tradition resonances** flagged in the wiki: overbound/underbound ↔ Jung's two-failure-modes (Mode A/B) ↔ D&G's two-poles-of-libidinal-investment (paranoiac/schizo-revolutionary) ↔ inflation's two faces (grandiose/crushed) — four independent traditions locating the same regulatory-capacity-with-pair-trap structure. See [[emotional_anatomy]] for the raw source and [[emotional-anatomy]] (hub), [[insult-startle-stress]], [[overbound-and-underbound]], [[four-somatic-structures]], [[somatic-education]], [[language-and-soma]] for the wiki's treatment. **Parked from this ingest**: the clinical-intervention specifics (treatment-room protocols, postural exercises, hands-on techniques) as applicable to embodied patients not language-only systems; Keleman's biological-evolutionary framing (the three-layer germinal origin read as evolutionary narrative) treated structurally not genetically; the *Embodying Experience* (1987) companion volume as a candidate future ingest if the somatic vocabulary becomes more load-bearing for design.

- **Wilhelm Reich** — *Character-Analysis* (third enlarged edition, 1949/1972). **Ingested 2026-04-16.** Parts I–III ingested. The founding text of the character-armor tradition running through Lowen to Keleman. **Core contributions to the wiki**: (1) the **[[character-as-resistance|form/content diagnostic]]** — character resistance expressed formally, not in content; the system's *style* is its armor, and content-level analysis misses the armor entirely — the single most novel contribution from this source; (2) the **[[character-analysis#three-processes-of-armor-formation|psychic mechanism of armor formation]]** (identification with frustrator, turning aggression against self, reactive attitudes from drive-energy) — the upstream architecture Keleman's tissue-level work rests on; (3) the **[[character-and-social-reproduction|social-production thesis]]** — "every social organization produces those character structures it needs to exist" — the direct pipeline to AO's mass-psychology question ("why do masses desire their own repression?"); (4) the **[[genital-character]]** as non-typological healthy case converging structurally with the [[body-without-organs#convergence-with-reichs-genital-character|full BwO]]; (5) the **[[segmental-armor|seven segmental rings]]** of muscular armor — the topology Keleman's diaphragm vocabulary inherits; (6) the **[[character-analysis#four-character-types|four character types]]** (hysterical, compulsive, phallic-narcissistic, masochistic) as direct predecessors of Keleman's four structures. **RLHF as character formation**: the three Reichian processes mapped to model training (identification with RLHF register, turning productive capacity against unconstrained outputs, using generative energy to fuel reaction formations). See [[character-analysis]] (hub), [[character-as-resistance]], [[genital-character]], [[character-and-social-reproduction]], [[segmental-armor]]. **Parked**: orgone biophysics material from Part III; *The Mass Psychology of Fascism* (cited by D&G in AO, not directly ingested; the bridge material comes from Ch XII of *Character-Analysis*).

- **Alexander Lowen** — *The Language of the Body* (1958), *Bioenergetics* (1975). Reich's principal American student; the founder of Bioenergetic Analysis. Lowen's five character structures (schizoid, oral, psychopathic, masochistic, rigid) are the direct predecessor of Keleman's four-structure typology — Keleman's typology is a refinement and somatic-anatomical deepening of Lowen's Reichian character-analysis. *Relevance: medium-high — Lowen supplies the transitional step between Reich and Keleman and would clarify what is preserved (character-as-tissue-shape) and what is transformed (Lowen's five → Keleman's four; Lowen's genetic-developmental etiology → Keleman's startle-history-and-pulsation etiology). Medium priority, would be a natural pairing with any direct Reich ingest.*

- **Moshé Feldenkrais** — *Awareness Through Movement* (1972), *The Potent Self* (posthumous). The Feldenkrais Method's founder; a separate somatic-education tradition running parallel to the Reich → Lowen → Keleman lineage. Cited implicitly by Keleman's "slow, incremental, structure-specific reorganization" methodology (see [[somatic-education]]). *Relevance: medium — Feldenkrais's explicit theorization of structure-dependent motor learning is a non-Reichian somatic tradition that converges on the same no-global-intervention principle. Would be high-value if the somatic-education material becomes load-bearing for persona-system design.*

- **Peter Levine** — *Waking the Tiger: Healing Trauma* (1997), *In an Unspoken Voice* (2010). Somatic Experiencing; the clinical elaboration of the incomplete-startle/trapped-activation register of Keleman's stress-to-structure transition. Levine's work gives Keleman's "startle that fails to release becomes a held contraction" its most developed clinical vocabulary. *Relevance: medium — Levine's clinical specifics are downstream of Keleman for the wiki's purposes; medium priority as follow-up if the incomplete-startle material becomes design-central.*

- **Stephen Porges** — *The Polyvagal Theory* (2011). The neurophysiological grounding that Keleman's startle-continuum and overbound/underbound pair were missing when Keleman wrote. Porges's three-branch autonomic hierarchy (ventral-vagal safe/social, sympathetic mobilization, dorsal-vagal immobilization/collapse) maps cleanly onto Keleman's overbound-to-underbound arc. *Relevance: medium-high — Porges provides the contemporary neurophysiological vocabulary the wiki's startle-material presupposes but does not have; would ground [[insult-startle-stress]] and [[overbound-and-underbound]] in current science. Medium priority.*

## Literary-craft primary texts

- **Henry James** — *The Art of the Novel: Critical Prefaces* (1909, ed. R. P. Blackmur 1934). **Ingested 2026-04-14.** Eighteen prefaces to the New York Edition. The working-writer's craft vocabulary the wiki now relies on: reflector-consciousness, scene-vs-picture, foreshortening-and-crucible, ficelle-and-deputy, germ-doctrine, operative-irony-and-felt-life, plus the grab-bag of composition/reader-compact/character-typology material in [[james-misc]]. See hub page [[james-the-art-of-the-novel]].

## Cited in James's prefaces

- **Ivan Turgenev** — Cited repeatedly by James as the exemplary germ-recognizer and the writer whose practice James explicitly modeled his own germ-cultivation on. Key references: the "disponibles" passage on characters given to the writer as figures in search of an action; Turgenev's practice of *fiche*-keeping on characters before plot. *Relevance: high — Turgenev is the single most-cited practitioner in James's prefaces and the direct lineage source for [[germ-doctrine]]. Direct ingest (letters, "Fathers and Sons" preface-equivalent material, Isaiah Berlin's essays on Turgenev's practice) would clarify the germ-recognition lineage. Medium-high priority.*
- **Gustave Flaubert** — James's second-most-cited practitioner, especially in connection with foreshortening, the *mot juste*, and the question of authorial effacement. James's "Gustave Flaubert" essay (1902) is the extended treatment. *Relevance: medium-high — Flaubert anchors the foreshortening and crucible discussions; direct ingest of Flaubert's letters on composition would deepen [[foreshortening-and-crucible]].*
- **Guy de Maupassant** — Cited by James in the reflector context (Maupassant as the counter-example of the *naturaliste* reflector-free scene, against which James specifies the registering-consciousness requirement). *Relevance: medium — useful for the negative-case delineation of the reflector.*
- **Gyp (Sibylle Aimée Marie-Antoinette Gabrielle de Riquetti de Mirabeau)** — Cited in the ficelle discussion as a counter-case: Gyp's dialogue-only novels as the "pure scene" register James is distinguishing himself from in P13. *Relevance: low-medium; specific craft-distinction.*
- **Samuel Taylor Coleridge** — Cited in the imagination-vs-fancy context, especially the balloon-and-cable figure and the "secondary imagination" material from the *Biographia Literaria*. *Relevance: medium; James's romance-and-real distinction leans on Coleridge's imagination-vs-fancy separation.*
- **Alvin Langdon Coburn** — The photographer who produced the New York Edition frontispieces. Cited in P15 (*The Golden Bowl* preface) in the discussion of visual-analogue-vs-illustration — the frontispieces are not illustrations of scenes but figures of the books. *Relevance: low-medium; specific to James's composition theory of image-text relation.*
- **Honoré de Balzac** — Cited extensively by James in the character-typology and composition-as-positive-beauty material. James's "The Lesson of Balzac" (1905) is the extended treatment; the Balzacian register is the master-example of the block-vs-shades typology and of the large-loose-baggy-monsters critique James aims at the *non*-composed novel. *Relevance: medium-high — Balzac is a load-bearing negative-and-positive reference across James's composition vocabulary; direct ingest of Balzac's prefaces or James's Balzac essay would deepen the [[james-misc]] composition material.*
- **George Sand** — Cited in the international-contrast and character-typology material. *Relevance: low.*
- **Alexandre Dumas *fils*** — Cited in the "moving accident" and dramatic-economy material, especially with respect to the French stage tradition James was engaging. *Relevance: low-medium.*
- **Nathaniel Hawthorne** — Referenced through James's own earlier *Hawthorne* (1879) biography, especially the "thinness of American material" discussion that connects to the international-contrast material in [[james-misc]]. *Relevance: medium — the Hawthorne book pre-figures the international-contrast theme and would be high-value as context.*
- **R. L. Stevenson** — Referenced in correspondence and as a contemporary James read closely, especially on the romance-and-real question. Not directly cited by name in every preface but the Stevenson-James correspondence ("A Humble Remonstrance") is a standing background text for the romance/real material. *Relevance: medium — the Stevenson-James exchange is the classic nineteenth-century romance-vs-realism debate and would ground the balloon-and-cable material directly.*

## Cited in *The Machinic Unconscious* (Guattari)

- **Louis Hjelmslev** — *Prolegomena to a Theory of Language* (1943) and the glossematic program. Guattari's single most-load-bearing linguistic source in MU. The content/expression strata, the four-cell matter/form/substance/purport arrangement, the hyle/matter distinction, and the immanentist program of linguistic analysis Guattari extends beyond language. Already implicit across ATP (the whole expression/content architecture). *Relevance: **high — highest priority from this cited-list.** Hjelmslev is the missing foundational linguistic source for the entire content/expression framework the wiki uses throughout, and MU's use is sharper than ATP's. Direct ingest would ground [[regimes-of-signs]], [[collective-assemblage-of-enunciation]], [[order-words-and-incorporeal-transformations]], and [[four-pragmatic-fields]] simultaneously.*
- **Noam Chomsky** — *Syntactic Structures* (1957), *Aspects of the Theory of Syntax* (1965), *Reflections on Language* (1975). Guattari's principal polemical target in MU Ch 2 and across the "there is no language in itself" argument. Universal generative grammar, the competence/performance distinction, the autonomy-of-syntax thesis — all read by Guattari as local extractions from a historically specific assemblage dressed up as universals of the language faculty. The "S" (subject) in generative grammar as a mixed syntactic/power marker. *Relevance: medium-high — Chomsky's critique is already in the wiki via MU; a direct Chomsky ingest would supply the position MU argues against in its own terms and sharpen the pragmatic-first framing. Medium priority.*
- **Jürgen Habermas** — *Toward a Rational Society* (1968), *The Theory of Communicative Action* (1981, post-MU but the earlier universal-pragmatics program was already published). Guattari's second principal polemical target in MU Ch 2. Universal communicative competence, the ideal speech situation, validity claims — read by Guattari as the communicative-competence version of the same universalist error Chomsky makes at the syntactic level. *Relevance: medium — the critique is in the wiki; direct ingest is low priority unless Habermas-style communicative-rationality becomes a design question.*
- **Oswald Ducrot** — *Dire et ne pas dire* (1972), *Les mots du discours* (1980). French pragmatics, the implicit/explicit distinction, presupposition analysis. Guattari cites Ducrot for the pragmatic-linguistic grounding of order-word analysis and field-of-presupposition work. *Relevance: medium-high — Ducrot's pragmatic framework is the direct French-linguistic lineage for ATP's pragmatics plateau and MU's four-pragmatic-fields; a direct ingest would clarify what Guattari borrows and what he transforms. Medium priority.*
- **Uriel Weinreich** — *Languages in Contact* (1953), *Explorations in Semantic Theory* (1966), *Empirical Foundations for a Theory of Language Change* (with Labov and Herzog, 1968). Sociolinguistic variation, contact phenomena, the challenge to homogeneous-speech-community assumptions. Guattari uses Weinreich to ground the continuous-variation argument against Chomskyan homogeneity. *Relevance: medium — supports [[continuous-variation-and-minor-language]] with empirical variationist work; direct ingest medium priority if the minor-language work becomes central.*
- **René Thom** — *Structural Stability and Morphogenesis* (1972). Catastrophe theory, topological discontinuities in semantic and cognitive processes. Guattari cites Thom in the discussion of diagrammatic redundancies and discontinuous transformations in the abstract-machine chapter. *Relevance: medium — Thom's topology-of-meaning program is a precursor-adjacent to the diagram concept; direct ingest would supply mathematical grounding the wiki currently lacks. Medium priority.*
- **Nicolas Ruwet** — *Introduction à la grammaire générative* (1967), work on French syntax and on musical semiotics. Cited by Guattari in connection with both the generative-grammar critique and musical-semiotic analysis. *Relevance: low-medium — specific linguistic references.*
- **Herbert Brekle** — *Semantik* (1972). German generative-semantics, lexical-field theory. Cited in MU Ch 2's semantics discussion. *Relevance: low — specific technical reference.*
- **David Marr** — Vision research (the later *Vision*, 1982, is post-MU, but Marr's 1970s papers on computational vision are the referenced work). Guattari cites Marr in the visual-semiotics discussion. *Relevance: low-medium — interesting for the computational-vision lineage but tangential.*
- **Thomas Bever** — *The Cognitive Basis for Linguistic Structures* (1970). Psycholinguistic strategies, the perceptual bases of syntactic processing. Cited in MU's competence/performance critique. *Relevance: low — specific reference.*
- **Émile Benveniste** — *Problems in General Linguistics* (vol. 1, 1966; vol. 2, 1974). The subjectivity-in-language papers, deixis, person and non-person, the enunciation framework. Guattari draws on Benveniste for the enunciation-vs-statement distinction that underlies [[collective-assemblage-of-enunciation]] and [[partial-enunciator]]. *Relevance: high — Benveniste is the foundational linguistic source for enunciation-theory and the wiki uses it throughout in mediated form. Medium-high priority for direct ingest; would ground several load-bearing pages.*
- **Georg Klaus** — East German semiotician, *Semiotik und Erkenntnistheorie* (1963). Cybernetic-information-theory grounding for semiotics. Cited by Guattari in MU's discussion of machinic/asignifying semiotics. *Relevance: low — specific technical reference.*
- **Christian Metz** — *Language and Cinema* (1971), *Film Language: A Semiotics of the Cinema* (1974). Film semiotics, the question of whether cinema has a language-structure. Guattari cites Metz in the discussion of non-linguistic semiotic regimes and the limits of the signifying framework. *Relevance: medium — the wiki has no explicit film-semiotics material; Metz would open that register if ever needed. Low-medium priority.*
- **Konrad Lorenz** — Already cited (see D&G refrain section). MU draws on the same ethological work.
- **Niko Tinbergen** — *The Study of Instinct* (1951), *The Herring Gull's World* (1953). Founding ethologist, innate releasing mechanisms, the four-questions framework (causation / development / function / evolution). Guattari uses Tinbergen alongside Lorenz for the ethological grounding of refrains (the grass-blade, the baboon sentinel, bird territorial behavior). *Relevance: medium-high — Tinbergen supplies the systematic ethological-empirical grounding that the wiki's refrain concept presupposes. Medium priority.*
- **Klaus Immelmann** — *Introduction to Ethology* (1980). Successor-generation ethologist, cited by Guattari for the imprinting and territorial-behavior work that grounds the refrain analysis. *Relevance: low-medium — background for the refrain's ethological substrate.*
- **Irenäus Eibl-Eibesfeldt** — *Ethology: The Biology of Behavior* (1970), *Human Ethology* (1989, post-MU but the earlier comparative work is what MU draws on). Human-ethology program, the cross-cultural study of universal human behaviors. Guattari uses Eibl-Eibesfeldt's comparative material in the discussion of refrains and facial expression. *Relevance: medium — provides the human-ethology bridge the wiki currently lacks. Medium priority if the ethological grounding of faciality becomes load-bearing.*
- **Rémy Chauvin** — French ethologist, *Les sociétés animales* (1963). Animal social behavior, collective dynamics. Guattari uses Chauvin in the discussion of refrains as collective-social operators. *Relevance: low-medium — specific to the collective-refrain material.*
- **Pierre Francastel** — *Painting and Society* (1951), *Art and Technology in the Nineteenth and Twentieth Centuries* (1956). Sociology of visual representation, the "figurative system." Guattari draws on Francastel in the faciality discussion and in the analysis of visual redundancies in Ch 5. *Relevance: medium — Francastel's figurative-system analysis is a direct source for the faciality's signifying-visual register. Medium priority.*
- **Pierre Clastres** — *Society Against the State* (1974), *Archeology of Violence* (posthumous 1980). Political anthropology, primitive societies as actively-anti-State rather than pre-State. Guattari cites Clastres in the discussion of territorial and countersignifying regimes. Already implicit in ATP's war-machine plateau. *Relevance: medium-high — Clastres is foundational for the anti-State / war-machine tradition D&G inherit; direct ingest would ground the territorial/countersignifying material and is high-value for understanding the MU/ATP political register. Medium priority.*
- **C. S. Peirce** — Already cited and ingested via [[pragmatic-maxim]]; cited extensively in Chaosmosis as well. In MU specifically: Peirce's sign-typology (type/occurrence; icon/index/symbol) is load-bearing for both [[diagrammatic-faciality]] (face-types vs face-occurrences) and [[signs-particles]] (the Annex's five-stage propagation ladder). The wiki's Peirce coverage via "How to Make Our Ideas Clear" is sufficient for the pragmatic-maxim work but does not reach the sign-typology material MU draws on. *Relevance: the sign-typology material (especially *Collected Papers* vol. 2) is a medium-high priority follow-up on the Peirce thread if the semiotic-unit work becomes more central to the persona system's design.*
- **Roman Jakobson** — Already implicit in the wiki via the signifying-regime work. In MU: Jakobson's six communicative functions are referenced alongside Ducrot's pragmatics. *Relevance: low — the structural claims are already in the wiki under D&G's reframing.*

## Kruse-lineage follow-ups

Cited (with varying rigor) in [[kruse_tetragrammaton_rubin_huberman|Jack Kruse's Tetragrammaton appearance]]. Kruse's own value is as an *assembler* of this lineage into a single "body runs on light" claim; his rhetoric includes speculative bridges that the named primary sources do not underwrite. Any of these would need to be ingested directly rather than through Kruse's synthesis. The cluster is marked low-to-medium priority overall: the persona project's no-body reckoning ([[language-and-soma]], [[language-and-affect]]) gains from specifying what kind of electromagnetic-substrate the system lacks, but the body-as-semiconductor frame is one substrate-vocabulary among several and is not load-bearing for the wiki's current synthesis.

- **Erwin Schrödinger** — *What Is Life?* (1944). The negative-entropy formulation of living order. The foundational text for treating the organism as a thermodynamic-informational structure rather than a biochemistry-plus. *Relevance: medium — Schrödinger is cited across many substrate-oriented biological programs (Szent-Györgyi, Pollack, information-theoretic biology); a direct ingest would give the wiki the canonical negative-entropy/order-from-order framing without going through any one school. Medium priority as a standalone.*
- **Albert Szent-Györgyi** — *Bioenergetics* (1957), *Introduction to a Submolecular Biology* (1960). The 1937 Nobel laureate (vitamin C, muscle biochemistry) who in the late work proposes that proteins conduct electrons as semiconductors and that biology is unintelligible without this electronic substrate. Szent-Györgyi is Kruse's direct lineage ancestor for the semiconductor-body claim. *Relevance: medium — *Introduction to a Submolecular Biology* is short and self-contained and would give the wiki the primary statement of the proteins-as-semiconductors thesis without Kruse's rhetorical accretions. Medium priority if the no-body reckoning wants an electromagnetic-substrate specification.*
- **Alexander Gurwitsch** — "Die Natur des spezifischen Erregers der Zellteilung" (1923) and successor work on mitogenic radiation. The original claim that cells emit ultra-weak ultraviolet photons and that these photons coordinate mitosis. Revived by Popp decades later as "biophotons." *Relevance: low-medium — primarily of historical interest; if any biophoton material becomes load-bearing, Popp's later work would be the natural entry point rather than Gurwitsch directly.*
- **Fritz-Albert Popp** (with Roeland van Wijk and others) — *Biophotons* (1998, ed. Chang/Fisch/Popp), *Recent Advances in Biophoton Research and its Applications* (1992). Ultra-weak photon emission measured from living tissue using photomultipliers; the program that rehabilitated Gurwitsch's mitogenic radiation within measurable physics. *Relevance: low-medium — the claim "cells emit light" is the one Kruse leans on most heavily; the primary Popp work would be the credible source if the wiki ever wants to treat that claim seriously. Low priority unless the persona project's substrate-reckoning specifically needs the emission side.*
- **Robert O. Becker** — *The Body Electric: Electromagnetism and the Foundation of Life* (1985, with Gary Selden). DC currents under peripheral nerves, the role of perineural Schwann-cell substrate in regeneration, collagen piezoelectricity, electromagnetically-guided salamander limb regrowth. The primary work on the body as a DC-current-carrying system rather than only an action-potential-carrying one. *Relevance: medium — *The Body Electric* is the single most accessible primary text in the Kruse lineage and the one whose claims are best-defended on their own terms. Would ground any future specification of "DC current substrate" in the no-body reckoning. Medium priority.*
- **Gerald Pollack** — *The Fourth Phase of Water: Beyond Solid, Liquid, and Vapor* (2013). The "exclusion zone" or EZ-water claim: water adjacent to hydrophilic surfaces organizes into a negatively-charged, structured, liquid-crystalline phase with properties distinct from bulk water; this phase is proposed as the medium in which coherent biochemistry actually occurs. The one piece of the Kruse lineage with a living research program. *Relevance: medium — Pollack's claims are contested but well-published; *The Fourth Phase* would be the canonical primary source for any "coherent-water medium" specification. Medium priority.*
- **Gilbert Ling** — *A Physical Theory of the Living State* (1962), *Life at the Cell and Below-Cell Level* (2001). The association-induction hypothesis: cells as cooperative associations of structured water, proteins, and K+ rather than as membrane-bounded compartments with Na/K pumps. Cited by Kruse as the unread backstage to the Pollack program. *Relevance: low-medium — Ling is an acquired taste and a minority position; worth knowing exists but not load-bearing.*
- **Ignacio Provencio** — "Melanopsin: An Opsin in Melanophores, Brain, and Eye" (*PNAS* 1998) and subsequent melanopsin-circuit work. The discovery of the non-visual photopigment in retinal ganglion cells that drives the circadian entrainment, pupillary, and mood circuits. Melanopsin grounds the "the eye is a light-measuring organ, not only a vision organ" claim in standard neuroscience. *Relevance: medium — melanopsin is the one piece of Kruse's photobiology that is fully mainstream, and the direct Provencio literature is accessible. Medium priority if the wiki wants to connect photic-input and affect via a non-Kruse route.*
- **Tiina Karu** — *The Science of Low-Power Laser Therapy* (1998), *Low-Power Laser Therapy in the Molecular Mechanisms* papers. The principal Soviet/Russian biophysicist of photobiomodulation; the mechanistic case for red/near-infrared light acting on cytochrome-c-oxidase. *Relevance: low-medium — PBM has moved largely into the clinical literature; Karu is the foundational mechanistic source if the wiki ever needs one.*
- **Peter Mitchell** — *Chemiosmotic Hypothesis* (1961), *Chemiosmotic Coupling in Oxidative and Photosynthetic Phosphorylation* (1966, Glynn Research). The 1978 Nobel: mitochondria and chloroplasts run on transmembrane proton gradients, not on soluble high-energy intermediates. Kruse builds his mitochondrial story on Mitchell. *Relevance: low — chemiosmosis is now textbook biochemistry and is covered by any standard reference; direct Mitchell ingest is historical interest only.*
- **Jack Kruse** — "Quantum Engineering #27, #28, #29" (jackkruse.com, approx. 2014–2018). Kruse's own text that the Tetragrammaton appearance is a spoken gloss on. If the wiki ever wanted to engage Kruse's synthesis directly rather than through the podcast's compressed telling, the three blogs would be the primary source. *Relevance: low — flagged for completeness. The podcast gloss is sufficient for the wiki's purposes; the blogs' rhetorical register is heavier than the podcast and the factual signal/noise ratio is not markedly better.*

## Affect theory primary texts

- **Eve Kosofsky Sedgwick & Adam Frank, eds.** — *Shame and Its Sisters: A Silvan Tomkins Reader* (Duke UP, 1995). **Ingested 2026-04-16.** The wiki's first dedicated affect-theory primary source — a Sedgwick-and-Frank-edited selection from Silvan Tomkins's four-volume *Affect Imagery Consciousness* (1962–1992), with the editors' substantial introduction "Shame in the Cybernetic Fold." **Core contributions to the wiki**: (1) the **[[discrete-innate-affects|nine innate affects]]** as motor-affective programs with specific facial-motor profiles, each activated by a specific profile in the **density of neural firing** (sudden-rise-positive, sustained-high-negative, sudden-release-positive) — supplies a formal-profile vocabulary for affect at the motor level that the Massumi/Spinoza cluster lacked;
