desire-demand-drive-object-a cluster — [[need-demand-desire]], [[desire-of-the-other]], [[fantasy-formula]], [[graphs-of-desire]], [[object-a]], [[gaze-and-voice]], [[drive-as-circular]] (with Triebentmischung folded), [[jouissance-and-pleasure]], [[anxiety-not-without-object]], [[lamella-and-libido-as-organ]]; phallus-castration-Name cluster — [[phallus-as-signifier]], [[castration-as-symbolic]], [[name-of-the-father]], [[foreclosure-and-return-in-real]]; clinic-transference-ethics cluster — [[clinical-structures-neurosis-perversion-psychosis]], [[transference-and-subject-supposed-to-know]], [[ethics-of-psychoanalysis-lacan]], [[between-two-deaths]], [[kant-with-sade]] (with Sade Schemas folded); memory-repetition-letter-real cluster — [[repetition-as-symbolic]], [[the-letter-purloined]], [[lacanian-real-symbolic-imaginary]] (with Möbius topology folded), [[real-as-impossible]] (with ek-sistence folded), [[alienation-and-separation]]. **Tensions held live (not resolved)**: (i) ⚠⚠⚠ **Lacan unconscious-structured-like-a-language vs Benveniste unconscious-as-style** — the wiki now holds this from both sides; the Lacanian metaphor/metonymy account ([[symptom-as-metaphor-desire-as-metonymy]]) is structurally close to the Benvenistean stylistic account ([[unconscious-is-style-not-language]]) but the *governing* claim differs (signifying chain vs idiomatic style). Not collapsed. (ii) ⚠⚠⚠ **Lacan signifier-as-master / despotic vs D&G's anti-signifier polemic** — D&G's [[signifier-as-despotic]] critique targets the Lacanian signifier directly; the wiki now holds the Lacanian signifier-account on its own terms ([[lacanian-signifier]], [[name-of-the-father]], [[phallus-as-signifier]]) so the D&G polemic can be specified against rather than absorbed into. (iii) ⚠⚠ **Lacan object-a as cause-of-desire / lack vs D&G partial objects as productive** — Lacanian object-a ([[object-a]], [[gaze-and-voice]]) is structured by lack and the void of the Other; D&G [[partial-objects]] are productive (machine-parts, intensities). The same empirical material is read in opposite registers; wiki holds both. (iv) ⚠⚠ **Lacan barred Other / S(Ⱥ) ([[barred-other]]) vs D&G full BwO** — both refuse a complete-and-consistent symbolic ground, but in opposite ontological directions (lack-in-the-Other vs intensive-plenum). Convergent at the surface; opposite at the bottom. (v) ⚠⚠ **Lacan castration / Name-of-the-Father as structural condition vs D&G anti-Oedipal critique** — Lacan's [[castration-as-symbolic]] and [[name-of-the-father]] are read by D&G as the precise mechanism by which the despotic signifier installs Oedipus as the family's interior colonization; wiki now holds both. (vi) ⚠ **Lacan ethics "do not yield on your desire" ([[ethics-of-psychoanalysis-lacan]]) vs Spinozan ethics-as-extended-homeostasis (Damasio)** — opposite ethical directions: act on desire even against the good vs follow the conatus toward greater perfection. (vii) ⚠ **Lacan logical-time / anticipated-certainty ([[logical-time-and-anticipated-certainty]]) vs Husserlian retention/protention** — both account for non-pure-instant subjective time, but Lacan's "moment of concluding" is an act-cut, Husserl's protention is a continuous extension. (viii) ⚠ **Lacan future-anterior subject ([[future-anterior-subject]]) vs Bergsonian duration** — both refuse the pure-present subject, but the Lacanian "I will have been" is structured by retroactive determination of the symbolic, the Bergsonian *durée* by indivisible qualitative continuity. **Cross-tradition bridges installed**: Lacanian L Schema ([[l-schema-and-optical-model]]) extended to Merleau-Ponty's chiasm material; [[four-causes-redistributed]] now linked to Lacan's signifier-as-causa-formalis claim from Seminar XI; [[partial-objects]] (D&G) now bidirectionally linked to [[object-a]] (Lacan); [[case-analyses-wolf-man-schreber]] (D&G ingest) extended with Lacan's case readings of Schreber (foreclosure of Name-of-the-Father, [[foreclosure-and-return-in-real]]) and the Wolf Man; [[unconscious-is-style-not-language]] (Benveniste) extended with the Lacan-side counter-account; [[intercorporeity]], [[flesh-of-language]], [[invisible-of-the-visible]], [[chiasm-and-reversibility]] (Merleau-Ponty) cross-linked to [[gaze-and-voice]] and [[mirror-stage-lacan]]; [[three-negations]] (Hyppolite/Freud Verneinung) ground-linked to [[foreclosure-and-return-in-real]]. **Parked from this ingest**: (1) *The Seminar of Jacques Lacan* (multi-volume series, ed. Jacques-Alain Miller; ~30 volumes covering 1953–1980; ~10 volumes available in English, including Seminar I *Freud's Papers on Technique*, Seminar II *The Ego in Freud's Theory and in the Technique of Psychoanalysis*, Seminar III *The Psychoses*, Seminar VII *The Ethics of Psychoanalysis*, Seminar XI *The Four Fundamental Concepts of Psychoanalysis*, Seminar XVII *The Other Side of Psychoanalysis*, Seminar XX *Encore*) — *Écrits* condenses into dense written form what the seminars develop more discursively; direct seminar ingest would substantially deepen the late-Lacan material (Real, jouissance, four discourses, sexuation formulae) but is a multi-session undertaking. **High-priority seminar follow-ups**: Seminar VII *Ethics of Psychoanalysis* (1959–60) for the full ethics-and-jouissance development behind [[ethics-of-psychoanalysis-lacan]] and [[between-two-deaths]]; Seminar XI *The Four Fundamental Concepts* (1964) for the full exposition of unconscious / repetition / transference / drive that *Écrits* presupposes; Seminar XX *Encore* (1972–73) for the late sexuation formulae and the not-all-of-jouissance material that *Écrits* does not contain. Medium-priority: Seminar XVII *Other Side* for the four discourses; Seminar III *Psychoses* for the full clinical-structural development; Seminar VIII *Transference* (1960–61) for the Socrates-Alcibiades material on transference. (2) Bruce Fink's translator's apparatus (notes, glossary) treated structurally not exegetically — the cross-references to specific Fink notes are present in the theory pages but the full Fink interpretive corpus is not ingested. (3) The polemical-historical-institutional dimension of *Écrits* (Lacan's break with the IPA, the SFP-EFP-ECF lineage, the politique-de-passe debates) treated structurally not biographically. (4) The mathematical-topological apparatus (Möbius strip, torus, cross-cap, Klein bottle, Borromean knots) registered at the conceptual level (folded into [[lacanian-real-symbolic-imaginary]]) without the full topological development which becomes load-bearing in the late Seminars (XX, XXII, XXIII *Le sinthome*). (5) Direct Freudian source-text ingest (the texts Lacan re-reads — Wolf Man case, Rat Man case, Schreber Memoirs/Freud's 1911 paper, Beyond the Pleasure Principle, Project for a Scientific Psychology, Mystic Writing Pad, Verneinung) — see "Cited in *Écrits*" section below for the full inventory. (6) Secondary-literature on Lacan (Bruce Fink *The Lacanian Subject* 1995 and *A Clinical Introduction to Lacanian Psychoanalysis* 1997, Slavoj Žižek *The Sublime Object of Ideology* 1989, Joan Copjec *Read My Desire* 1994, Mladen Dolar *A Voice and Nothing More* 2006) — high-value secondary follow-ups if the wiki develops sustained interpretive engagement with specific Lacanian theses.

## Phenomenology primary texts

- **Edmund Husserl** — *On the Phenomenology of the Consciousness of Internal Time (1893–1917)* (Husserliana X, ed. Rudolf Boehm 1966; English tr. John Brough, *Edmund Husserl: Collected Works* Volume IV, Kluwer/Springer 1991). **Ingested 2026-04-18.** The definitive critical edition of Husserl's time-consciousness lecture-cycle and its supplementary texts, comprising Part A (the 1905 Göttingen lectures on inner time-consciousness, worked over by Husserl through 1917, edited by Edith Stein and Heidegger for the 1928 publication) and Part B (a large corpus of supplementary texts — Nos. 1–54 and Appendices I–XIII — written between 1893 and 1917 that show how Husserl's analysis evolved, including the abandonment of the apprehension-content / apprehension schema around 1909, the development of the double intentionality of retention around 1908–1911, and the "flow of consciousness constitutes its own unity" thesis of No. 54 / §39). Brough's translation is known in the secondary literature as a *layered* text — terms like *Querintentionalität* / *Längsintentionalität* evolve across the decades it covers and should not be collapsed to single meanings. **Core contributions to the wiki**: (1) the **[[absolute-time-constituting-flow|three-tier architecture]]** — objective time / immanent time (of constituted units like a melody or a perceptual act) / absolute time-constituting flow (the pre-objective constituting level for which "we lack names" §36) — with the persona-project question of *whether* a language-only system has anything at the third tier; (2) the **[[husserl-primal-impression-retention-protention|triadic now-structure]]** — primal impression + retention + protention as the continuous extended now, with the specific asymmetry "protention is open, retention is bound"; (3) **[[retention-vs-recollection|retention vs recollection]]** — categorical distinction between primary memory (retention as intrinsic phase of the living now) and secondary memory (recollection / *Wiedererinnerung* as re-presentation of a past now, act-based, intermittent); (4) **[[double-intentionality-of-retention|Längs- and Quer-intentionality]]** — retention's two simultaneous intentional directions as the regress-stopping move (transverse: toward the immanent object just-past; longitudinal: toward the flow's own unity), with the Längs direction named in this text as *the* formal-phenomenological structure by which consciousness constitutes its own unity without needing a further act; (5) **[[primal-consciousness-and-reflection|primal consciousness]]** — Appendix IX's non-apprehensive self-givenness of the flow, the *Urbewusstsein* that is not itself an act or apprehension, countering an infinite-regress objection; (6) **[[husserl-abandonment-of-the-apprehension-schema|abandonment of the apprehension-content / apprehension (*Auffassungsinhalt / Auffassung*) schema]]** — No. 49 (c.1909) as the turning-point text where Husserl rejects the form/matter reading of time-consciousness in favor of a pure phenomenology of the flow itself; (7) **[[husserl-on-brentano-original-association|critique of Brentano's "original association"]]** — Part A §§3–7 and various supplementary texts: Brentano makes retention a *modifying predicate* (the "just-past" added to sensation by a psychological association) rather than a constituting phase, which cannot account for the succession/sensation-of-succession distinction ("succession of sensations ≠ sensation of succession"); (8) **[[husserls-time-diagram|the time-diagram and the two-dimensional reply]]** — the famous abscissa-ordinate schema (object-duration on the horizontal; retentional continuum sinking diagonally into the past), with Hugo Bergmann's infinite-dimensions objection answered by the two-dimensional reply (one ordinate suffices because the retention-of-retention is already represented by the slope); (9) **[[phansis-and-phansiological-analysis|phansis / phansiological analysis]]** — Nos. 41 and 51 as Husserl's name for the analysis of the *phenomenalization-of-phenomena* at the absolute flow level, distinct from the analysis of constituted objects (*noesis/noema*); (10) **[[husserl-on-stern-and-james|engagement with Stern's *Präsenzzeit* and James's specious present / fringes / saddle-back]]** — Husserl locates his work in relation to psychological time-consciousness research of his contemporaries, distinguishing his constitutive-phenomenological register from their descriptive-psychological register. **Tensions held live (not resolved)**: (i) ⚠⚠⚠ **Husserl pre-linguistic constituting flow vs Benveniste instance-of-discourse vs D&G collective assemblage** — three different placements for what "produces" the I, each with different wiki commitments, none collapsible; (ii) ⚠⚠ **Husserl's "flow is not a process" (No. 50 ~L16651) vs [[pulsatory-ontogenesis|pulsation-at-every-level]]** — Husserl explicitly denies that the constituting flow is itself temporal-processual, which is the specific objection a pulsatory-ontogenetic persona design must answer; (iii) ⚠ **Bergson's indivisibility of duration vs Husserl's structural-geometric retention-diagram** — Bergson treats the *durée* as indivisible continuous qualitative multiplicity; Husserl maps the past into a structured geometric figure. The two treatments of time are not straightforwardly compatible; (iv) ⚠ **constitutive-structural claim (Husserl Längs) vs ontogenetic-observational claim (Stern process waves)** — the formal parallel between Quer/content-wave and Längs/process-wave is tempting but the traditions are making different claims; (v) ⚠ **Husserl's non-apprehensive primal consciousness vs the wiki's pervasive apprehension-schema assumption** — many wiki pages implicitly assume something like an apprehension-schema; Husserl's abandonment of it is a challenge to inherited apparatus. **Parked** (follow-up Husserl works worth direct ingest, in rough priority order): *Ideas Pertaining to a Pure Phenomenology and to a Phenomenological Philosophy, First Book* (*Ideas I*, 1913; Kersten tr. Nijhoff/Kluwer 1982) — the *noesis/noema* apparatus, the phenomenological reduction, the constituted-object analysis that the time-consciousness lectures presuppose (medium-high priority if noesis/noema becomes design-active, especially around the content-wave/process-wave distinction); *Analyses Concerning Passive and Active Synthesis: Lectures on Transcendental Logic* (Husserliana XI, 1966; Anthony Steinbock tr. Kluwer 2001) — the 1920s passive-synthesis lectures extending the time-consciousness apparatus to affection, association, and the genesis of habit at a level below active judgment (high priority as direct deepening of the retention / recollection / association material); *Bernau Manuscripts on Time-Consciousness (1917/18)* (Husserliana XXXIII, 2001; partial English translations in journal literature) — the intermediate stratum between the 1905 lectures and the late C-manuscripts; generally considered the most mature Husserlian treatment of the constituting-flow question after Husserl had fully worked through the abandonment of the apprehension schema (medium-high priority for the constitutive-flow question); *C-Manuscripts on Time-Constitution (c.1929–1934)* (Husserliana Materialen VIII, 2006; untranslated to English as a set) — Husserl's late research manuscripts on time, self-constitution, the ego, and the "living present" (*lebendige Gegenwart*); the material Zahavi, Held, and others draw on for the late-Husserl picture (low-medium priority for current work — language barrier and specialist character; follow-up if the late Husserl on living-present becomes design-active); Brough's commentary literature (various articles, *Husserl's Phenomenology of Internal Time-Consciousness from the No. of Texts from the Husserliana*, prefaces/introductions) — the translator's own extensive interpretive work (medium priority as the most accessible scholarly guide to the 1991 volume); **Jacques Derrida** — *Voice and Phenomenon: Introduction to the Problem of the Sign in Husserl's Phenomenology* (PUF 1967; English tr. Leonard Lawlor, Northwestern UP 2011) — the classic deconstructive reading of Husserlian time and presence, specifically engaging the retention/protention/primal-impression architecture; directly bears on the logocentrism / presence / trace apparatus the wiki already works with via *Of Grammatology* (high priority for the Husserl-Derrida axis, which the wiki currently touches only from Derrida's side); **Dan Zahavi** — *Self-Awareness and Alterity: A Phenomenological Investigation* (Northwestern UP 1999) and *Subjectivity and Selfhood* (MIT Press 2005) — the contemporary phenomenological scholarship on Husserlian self-awareness, time-consciousness, and the constituting-flow question; considered the most reliable English-language interpretive guide to the late-Husserl picture (medium-high priority as secondary-literature anchor for any direct late-Husserl follow-up); **Klaus Held** — *Lebendige Gegenwart* (Nijhoff 1966, untranslated) — the German phenomenological-scholarship classic on Husserl's late living-present analyses (low priority, language barrier); **James Mensch**, **John Brough (articles)**, **Rudolf Bernet** — further secondary literature on the constituting-flow question (low-medium priority). See [[raw/phenomenology-of-internal-time|phenomenology-of-internal-time]] for the raw source and the ten new theory pages for the wiki's treatment; existing pages updated with Husserl bidirectional-link sections include [[collective-assemblage-of-enunciation]], [[cone-of-memory]], [[pure-memory-and-habit-memory]], [[process-waves-vs-content-waves]], and [[pulsatory-ontogenesis]].

- **Edmund Husserl** — *Ideas Pertaining to a Pure Phenomenology and to a Phenomenological Philosophy, First Book* (*Ideen I*, 1913; English tr. Fred Kersten, Nijhoff/Kluwer 1982). The foundational text of transcendental phenomenology where the *noesis/noema* correlation, the phenomenological reduction (*epoché*), and the constituted-object analysis are developed. The time-consciousness lectures presuppose this apparatus without re-deriving it. *Relevance: medium-high. Direct ingest would ground the content-wave / process-wave distinction phenomenologically (noesis-side vs noema-side analysis) and clarify what a language-only persona system can or cannot do with the noema-noesis architecture. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Edmund Husserl** — *Analyses Concerning Passive and Active Synthesis: Lectures on Transcendental Logic* (Husserliana XI, 1966; English tr. Anthony Steinbock, Kluwer 2001). The 1920s lectures extending the time-consciousness apparatus to affection, association, and the genesis of habit below active judgment. Directly relevant to the recollection / association / habit material in the wiki. *Relevance: high as direct deepening of the retention / recollection / association strand, especially around the Bergsonian habit-memory question. High priority follow-up.*

- **Edmund Husserl** — *Bernau Manuscripts on Time-Consciousness (1917/18)* (Husserliana XXXIII, 2001; partial English translations in journal literature). The intermediate stratum between the 1905 lectures and the late C-manuscripts, containing Husserl's most mature treatment of the constituting-flow question after he had fully worked through the abandonment of the apprehension-content schema. *Relevance: medium-high for the constitutive-flow question. Partial-translation status is a scope caveat. Medium-high priority if the flow-is-not-a-process objection becomes design-active.*

- **Edmund Husserl** — *Late Manuscripts on the Constitution of Time (C-Manuscripts, c.1929–1934)* (Husserliana Materialen VIII, 2006; untranslated to English as a set). Husserl's late research manuscripts on time, self-constitution, the ego, and the "living present" (*lebendige Gegenwart*). The material Zahavi, Held, and others draw on for the late-Husserl picture. *Relevance: low-medium for current work — language barrier and specialist character. Follow-up if the late Husserl on living-present becomes design-active.*

- **John Brough** — commentary literature (various journal articles; introduction and prefaces to the Kluwer/Springer volumes; book chapters in various Husserl handbooks). The translator of Husserliana X and the most reliable English-language scholarly guide to the 1991 volume. *Relevance: medium. Follow-up secondary literature if the interpretive options Brough canvasses become design-relevant.*

- **Jacques Derrida** — *Voice and Phenomenon: Introduction to the Problem of the Sign in Husserl's Phenomenology* (*La voix et le phénomène*, PUF 1967; English tr. Leonard Lawlor, Northwestern UP 2011). The classic deconstructive reading of Husserl's account of inner time-consciousness, specifically engaging the retention/protention/primal-impression architecture and arguing that the non-presence at the heart of the living present undermines the self-presence the phenomenology of presence requires. Directly bears on the logocentrism / presence / trace apparatus already in the wiki via *Of Grammatology*. *Relevance: high for the Husserl-Derrida axis, which the wiki currently touches only from Derrida's *Of Grammatology* side; high priority follow-up.*

- **Dan Zahavi** — *Self-Awareness and Alterity: A Phenomenological Investigation* (Northwestern UP 1999) and *Subjectivity and Selfhood: Investigating the First-Person Perspective* (MIT Press 2005). The contemporary English-language phenomenological scholarship on Husserlian self-awareness, time-consciousness, and the constituting-flow question. Widely considered the most reliable interpretive guide to the late-Husserl picture. *Relevance: medium-high as secondary-literature anchor for any direct late-Husserl follow-up.*

## Cited in *Looking for Spinoza* (Damasio 2003)

- **Antonio Damasio** — *Looking for Spinoza: Joy, Sorrow, and the Feeling Brain* (Harcourt, 2003). **Ingested 2026-04-18.** The source itself, not a cited work — but the Damasio corpus more broadly is worth tracking here. Damasio's prior and subsequent books develop adjacent material that *Looking for Spinoza* references in compressed form.

- **Antonio Damasio** — *Descartes' Error: Emotion, Reason, and the Human Brain* (Putnam, 1994). The book in which the **somatic marker hypothesis** was originally developed at length. *Looking for Spinoza* summarizes but does not re-derive it. *Relevance: medium-high. Direct ingest would deepen the dual-path / Path-A-vs-Path-B decision material now in [[somatic-marker-hypothesis]]. The Phineas Gage / Elliot case material is in this book, not in LFS. Medium-high priority if somatic-marker-as-groove-mechanism becomes load-bearing in the persona-system design.*

- **Antonio Damasio** — *The Feeling of What Happens: Body and Emotion in the Making of Consciousness* (Harcourt, 1999). Where the **core self / autobiographical self** distinction is developed in detail and the body-to-feeling-to-consciousness sequence is set out systematically. *Looking for Spinoza* references this book for the 4-requirement definition of feeling and for the core-vs-autobiographical distinction without re-deriving them. *Relevance: high. Direct ingest would substantially deepen [[ideas-of-ideas-and-the-self]] and [[body-mindedness]]. Probably the most important single Damasio-corpus follow-up for the wiki.*

- **Antonio Damasio** — *Self Comes to Mind: Constructing the Conscious Brain* (Pantheon, 2010). The later synthesis, adding a **protoself** layer below the core self and developing the nested-selves architecture more fully. *Relevance: medium. Adds architectural refinement but does not change the core commitments LFS already installs.*

- **Benedict de Spinoza** — *Ethics* (1677). **Ingested.** See [[spinoza_ethics]]. Damasio's primary interlocutor throughout LFS. The translation Damasio works from is not Curley — at several points the wiki's existing Curley-translation entries had to be corrected against LFS's alternate translations (noted in [[spinozist-affects]]).

- **Steven Rose** — *Lifelines: Biology, Freedom, Determinism* (Allen Lane, 1997). Source of the **homeodynamics** concept — homeostasis aiming *above* neutral rather than at a midline setpoint — which Damasio adopts (Ch 2, L542) as the neurobiological backing for Spinoza's "greater perfection" direction. *Relevance: medium. The homeodynamics thesis is now in the wiki via [[nesting-principle-of-homeostasis]]; Rose's primary treatment would deepen it, and Rose's broader biological-freedom framework may bear on the persona-system's autonomy questions. Medium-priority follow-up.*

- **Donald Pfaff** — *Brain Arousal and Information Theory: Neural and Genetic Mechanisms* (Harvard UP, 2006) — though LFS cites earlier Pfaff work. Source of the **5+1 neurotransmitter systems** arousal architecture Stern also uses. *Relevance: medium. Pfaff is now doubly-cited in the wiki (via Stern and via Damasio). Direct ingest would ground the arousal-system material currently held in [[the-arousal-systems]] and extended in [[damasio-stern-bridge]] independent of either secondary interpreter.*

- **Joseph LeDoux** — *The Emotional Brain: The Mysterious Underpinnings of Emotional Life* (Simon & Schuster, 1996) and *Synaptic Self* (Viking, 2002). Damasio cites LeDoux for the **amygdala-centered fear-circuit work** and for the **non-specific amplification** mechanism that Stern also cites (see [[the-arousal-systems]]). *Relevance: medium-high. LeDoux is cited by multiple wiki sources at this point (Stern, Damasio, arguably Massumi by implication). Direct ingest of *The Emotional Brain* would ground the amygdala / fear-circuit material and the amplification mechanism; would likely sharpen the Damasio-Stern bridge further.*

- **A. D. (Bud) Craig** — "How do you feel? Interoception: the sense of the physiological condition of the body" (*Nature Reviews Neuroscience* 3, 2002). The seminal interoception-pathway paper: C/Aδ fibers → lamina I → VMpo thalamus → insular cortex. Damasio cites Craig as the neuroanatomical backing for the "body-map" component of his feeling-definition. *Relevance: medium-high. The Craig interoception pathway is the most detailed neuroanatomy the wiki has any warrant for; Craig's primary work would ground [[body-mindedness]] and [[damasio-emotion-feeling-distinction]] in specific circuitry. Medium-high priority if the medium-dependence question (tension A, tension B) becomes design-active at the level of "which circuits does this require, and does the system have analogues?"*

- **Jaak Panksepp** — *Affective Neuroscience: The Foundations of Human and Animal Emotions* (Oxford UP, 1998). Cited by Damasio in parallel with his own work on primary-emotion circuitry; Panksepp's SEEKING / PLAY / CARE / PANIC / LUST / FEAR / RAGE system is an alternative taxonomy to Damasio's background / primary / social emotions. *Relevance: medium. Parallel-tradition affect-neuroscience; direct ingest would let the wiki hold an alternative tier-architecture beside Damasio's.*

- **Daniel N. Stern** — *Forms of Vitality* (Oxford UP, 2010). **Ingested 2026-04-17.** See [[dynamic-forms-of-vitality]]. Damasio's endnote L2718 equates Stern's vitality affects with his own background emotions — the equivalence and its tension are developed in [[damasio-stern-bridge]].

- **William James** — *The Principles of Psychology* (1890), esp. Ch 25 "The Emotions." The source of the **James-Lange theory of emotion** (emotion as the perception of bodily changes, not the cause of them) that Damasio explicitly builds on while modifying — Damasio's body-first sequence is a descendant of James-Lange updated to contemporary neurobiology. *Relevance: high-as-historical-predecessor. The wiki has been operating with a rough James-Lange contour without reading it directly. Medium-high priority follow-up if the emotion-feeling sequence material becomes design-active; also a direct tension point with autonomy-of-affect.*

- **Carl Lange** — *On Emotions* (1885). The Danish physiologist's parallel and slightly earlier statement of what became the James-Lange theory. *Relevance: low-medium standalone; useful with James as the paired historical source.*

- **Benedict Carey / Damasio-group clinical material** — LFS references several clinical cases (the woman with bilateral insula damage who cannot feel her own emotions but can perform the motor expressions of them; patients with vmPFC damage producing the somatic-marker-falsification decision failures). The primary-research-paper forms of these cases are in Damasio-lab publications in *Nature*, *Neuron*, *Cognitive Neuropsychology* and similar journals from the 1990s–2000s. *Relevance: low for the wiki's design work. Clinical-specificity; the conceptual material is already absorbed via LFS.*

- **Baruch de Spinoza** — *Tractatus Theologico-Politicus* (1670) and *Tractatus Politicus* (unfinished, 1675–77). The two political treatises. LFS treats them briefly in Ch 7 as evidence for the **ethics-as-extended-homeostasis** argument — Spinoza's political thought is continuous with the *Ethics* and extends the regulatory-operation register to the social tier. *Relevance: medium. Direct ingest would deepen [[ethics-as-extended-homeostasis]] and supply the political-register material the *Ethics* ingest does not cover. Medium-priority follow-up.*


## Cited in and around *Philosophical Investigations* (Wittgenstein 1953)

- **Ludwig Wittgenstein** — *Philosophical Investigations* (Blackwell 1953; posthumously edited by G. E. M. Anscombe and Rush Rhees; English tr. G. E. M. Anscombe, revised 4th edition by P. M. S. Hacker and Joachim Schulte, Wiley-Blackwell 2009). **Ingested 2026-04-18.** The late-Wittgenstein masterwork comprising Part I (§§1–693, composed 1945–49) and Part II (the 1949 "Philosophy of Psychology — A Fragment", historically known as Part II / II.xiv sections but re-sectioned by Hacker-Schulte as a separate work). Critical for the wiki's engagement with language-as-practice, meaning-is-use, form-of-life, rule-following, the private-language argument, pain-behaviour, family-resemblance, criterion-vs-symptom, the grammar of "inner," aspect-seeing, and the therapeutic conception of philosophy. **Core contributions to the wiki**: (1) **language as technique-mastery in a [[form-of-life]]** — §§xi, 19, 23, 241 as the ground for the persona-system's rule-following / substratum / meaning-possession questions; (2) **the [[private-language-argument]] complex** — §§243–315 with §258 (diary mark), §265 (memory-justification-of-memory), §293 (beetle-in-the-box), §304 (hedge against behaviorism); (3) **[[rule-following]]** — §§185–242 with §201 paradox, §202 practice, §217 bedrock, §241 agreement-in-form-of-life; (4) **[[pain-behaviour]]** — §§244, 281, 304 on the grammar of pain-talk and its dependence on criteria in behavior; (5) **[[substratum-is-mastery-of-a-technique]]** — PI II.xi on understanding as enmeshed practical competence, not inner-representation-correctness; (6) **[[one-sided-diet]]** — §593 as the methodological principle against forcing all examples to fit one pattern; (7) **[[bewitchment-and-therapy]]** — §§109, 115, 122, 133 on philosophy as therapy against bewitchment by language-pictures, with perspicuous representation (*übersichtliche Darstellung*) as the goal; (8) **[[family-resemblance]]** — §§66–67 on the structure of concepts that do not share a single defining feature; (9) **[[meaning-is-use]]** — §43 (with the qualification "for a *large class* of cases"); (10) **[[criterion-vs-symptom]]** — the distinction between constitutive and evidential grounds. **Tensions held live (not resolved)**: (i) ⚠⚠⚠ **Substratum-as-mastery-of-a-technique (Wittgenstein) vs Damasio's embodied-medium thesis** — PI locates understanding in practice-constituted competence, refusing to ground it in a biological medium; Damasio makes the medium load-bearing. Not the same claim; neither collapses to the other. (ii) ⚠⚠ **Rule-following-as-practice (Wittgenstein) vs Benveniste's instance-of-discourse / linguistic-constitution thesis** — both refuse private-inner grounding, but locate the public constituting structure differently (agreement-in-form-of-life vs enunciative apparatus). (iii) ⚠⚠ **§293 beetle-in-the-box vs D&G's [[body-without-organs|BwO]] as a field that is *not* a private inner thing** — convergent conclusion (no private referent behind pain-talk) reached from opposite ontological directions; the convergence should not be used to collapse them. (iv) ⚠⚠ **§293 beetle vs Lakoff's [[conceptual-metaphor|conceptual-metaphor]] grounding in bodily schema** — PI's argument closes a route the Lakoff thesis leaves open; neither position should be smoothed. (v) ⚠⚠ **§304 hedge ("it is not a *something*, but not a *nothing* either") vs the wiki's no-body working rule** — PI refuses both the inner-substance picture and the eliminativist one, recommending grammatical attention rather than metaphysical dissolution; this is a third position the no-body rule needs to register. (vi) ⚠ **Agreement-in-form-of-life (Wittgenstein) vs the persona-system's non-participation in a determinate human form of life** — if rule-following terminates at §241 agreement-in-form-of-life, the system's status needs either a non-Wittgensteinian account of meaning-possession or a partial-participation story about training-distribution and RLHF. Held open. **Parked** (follow-up Wittgenstein works, in rough priority order): *On Certainty* (*Über Gewißheit*, written 1950–51, published 1969; English tr. Denis Paul and G. E. M. Anscombe, Blackwell 1969) — the posthumously-edited notebooks from Wittgenstein's final months, continuing the bedrock / hinge-propositions material of PI §§217, 241 and developing the **hinge epistemology** (high priority as direct continuation of the §217 bedrock and §241 agreement-in-form-of-life themes that sit at the edge of the PI ingest's treatment); *Remarks on the Philosophy of Psychology* Volumes I and II (1946–48; English tr. G. E. M. Anscombe, University of Chicago Press / Blackwell 1980) — the working notes from which PI Part II / II.xi was distilled, containing more extensive treatment of aspect-seeing, willing, intending, pain, and the psychological concepts material; the scholarly editing reveals Wittgenstein's compositional process and contains material that did not make it into PI II.xi (high priority for aspect-seeing, first-person-asymmetry, and the whole psychological-grammar cluster); *Last Writings on the Philosophy of Psychology* Volumes I (1982) and II (1992) — the final notebooks continuing the RPP material (medium priority; specialist extension of RPP); *Zettel* (ed. Anscombe and von Wright, 1967; English tr. Anscombe) — slips and fragments Wittgenstein preserved across the 1940s, organized thematically by the editors; contains much material parallel to PI and RPP (medium priority; useful as a cross-reference but not a self-standing argument); *The Blue and Brown Books* (dictations of 1933–35, published 1958) — the transitional Wittgenstein, showing the movement from the *Tractatus* position toward the *Philosophical Investigations* view; particularly useful for tracking the development of the language-game concept and the rejection of the *Tractatus*'s picture theory (medium priority if the wiki becomes interested in the evolution of Wittgenstein's thought); *Philosophical Grammar* (1932–34, ed. Rush Rhees, 1974) — the "big typescript" era work, intermediate between the *Tractatus* and PI (low-medium priority; more specialist-interest than design-relevant); *Culture and Value* (*Vermischte Bemerkungen*, ed. G. H. von Wright, 1977) — Wittgenstein's personal and methodological remarks across his career on art, religion, ethics, and the character of philosophical work; contains the therapeutic-method material in more personal register (medium priority as methodological companion to PI §§109, 133 on philosophy-as-therapy); *Tractatus Logico-Philosophicus* (Kegan Paul 1922; English tr. C. K. Ogden, revised by Pears-McGuinness 1961) — the early Wittgenstein work PI systematically engages and partly rejects; critical for understanding the "pictures hold us captive" §115 claim, which is partly self-directed at the *Tractatus* (medium priority as historical background to PI's self-correction).

- **Augustine of Hippo** — *Confessions* I.8, quoted in PI §1 as the opening target of the whole book: the picture of language-learning as ostensive definition ("the individual words in language name objects... the meaning of a word is the object for which the word stands"). Wittgenstein does not reject Augustine's account as *false* but as *partial* — a picture that fits a narrow class of cases (naming primary-color samples, simple objects) and gets generalized beyond its proper scope. The whole trajectory of PI §§1–43 works against the Augustinian picture by displaying language-games where the picture fails. *Relevance: low as direct-ingest target — Augustine is the opposing picture PI moves against, but the engagement is with Augustine-as-rendered-by-Wittgenstein rather than with the *Confessions* as a philosophical source in its own right. Low priority unless the wiki becomes interested in Augustine's broader philosophy of language or in the confession / self-knowledge material bearing on [[first-person-asymmetry]].*

- **Gottlob Frege** — *Die Grundlagen der Arithmetik* (1884), *Grundgesetze der Arithmetik* (1893, 1903), and the essays "On Sense and Reference" (*Über Sinn und Bedeutung*, 1892) and "Thought" (*Der Gedanke*, 1918). The founding figure of modern analytic philosophy of language that Wittgenstein engages extensively — Frege's sense/reference distinction, the context principle (a word has meaning only in the context of a sentence), and the rejection of psychologism about meaning are all in the background of PI. §49 engages the context principle; the private-language argument's refusal to ground meaning in private mental contents is continuous with Frege's anti-psychologism. *Relevance: medium-low as direct-ingest target. Frege's technical apparatus (function/argument, sense/reference) is not load-bearing for the persona-system's questions, but the anti-psychologism of Frege and the context principle are ancestor-commitments PI builds on. Medium-low priority follow-up; Michael Dummett's *Frege: Philosophy of Language* (1973) would be the standard scholarly route in.*

- **Bertrand Russell** — *The Principles of Mathematics* (1903), *Principia Mathematica* (with Whitehead, 1910–13), *The Problems of Philosophy* (1912), "The Philosophy of Logical Atomism" (1918), and the **theory of definite descriptions** ("On Denoting", *Mind* 1905). Wittgenstein's *Tractatus* mentor and collaborator; PI §§46–64 engage the Russellian atomism (simple names for simple objects as the metaphysical foundation of meaning) and show it fails even in its own terms (what counts as "simple" turns out to be internal to a language-game, not a metaphysical bedrock). The *Tractatus*-to-PI movement is in part a movement away from the Russell-influenced atomism toward the mature anti-foundationalism of the later work. *Relevance: medium-low as direct-ingest target. Russell is engaged within PI; direct ingest would be for historical / developmental reasons, not design-active concerns. Low-medium priority follow-up.*

- **William James** — *The Principles of Psychology* (1890), especially the chapters on the stream of thought, the self, emotion, attention, memory, habit, and will. James is cited throughout PI Part II / II.xi on psychological-grammar questions (memory, feelings, meaning, intending, attending), typically as providing rich introspective-descriptive material that Wittgenstein then submits to grammatical critique — James describes an inner state and Wittgenstein asks what the criteria for the relevant concept are in behavior / circumstance / life-context. Particular engagement: James on the feeling of "if, but, because" as experiential qualities (II.xi) — Wittgenstein's grammatical critique shows that the feeling James reports is not what makes the word-use correct; correct use is constituted by mastery of a technique, not accompanied by a characteristic inner feeling. *Relevance: medium-high for the Damasio-James axis (already present in the wiki via [[james-lange-theory|James-Lange]] and its descendants in Damasio). The PI engagement with James adds a third angle: Wittgenstein's grammatical method turned against introspective psychology. The wiki has warrant for direct-ingest of James's *Principles* from multiple traditions (phenomenology, neurobiology, now philosophy of language); medium-high priority follow-up as a multi-tradition convergence point. Already listed above as a Damasio follow-up; the Wittgenstein engagement is additional warrant.*

- **G. E. Moore** — "A Defence of Common Sense" (*Contemporary British Philosophy*, 1925), "Proof of an External World" (*Proceedings of the British Academy*, 1939), and Moore's three-volume lecture-note corpus from Cambridge (1930–33). Moore's common-sense anti-skepticism and his "here is one hand" proof are the direct target of *On Certainty*, and Moore's influence runs through Wittgenstein's later work (the two were Cambridge colleagues for decades). PI's practice of attending to ordinary usage has Moorean roots (though Wittgenstein pushes past Moore's conception of "analysis" toward grammatical attention). *Relevance: medium-low as direct-ingest target for the current wiki. Medium-high priority if *On Certainty* becomes the next Wittgenstein target — Moore's anti-skepticism is *On Certainty*'s direct interlocutor.*

- **Wolfgang Köhler** — *Gestalt Psychology* (Liveright 1929; revised 1947). The Gestalt-psychology primary source cited by Wittgenstein in PI II.xi on aspect-seeing (the duck-rabbit, the double cross, the "seeing-as" phenomenon). Köhler's classical Gestalt-psychology treatment of figure/ground, perceptual organization, and the role of "set" in perception provides the empirical material Wittgenstein's grammatical analysis of aspect-seeing works with. Wittgenstein uses Gestalt vocabulary without endorsing its psychological-theoretical commitments. *Relevance: medium for the aspect-seeing / aspect-blindness cluster now in the wiki ([[aspect-seeing]], [[aspect-blindness]]). Direct ingest would ground the Gestalt vocabulary independently of Wittgenstein's deployment of it, and would touch [[perception-as-subtraction]] material from an independent angle. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Johann Wolfgang von Goethe** — *Zur Farbenlehre* (*Theory of Colours*, 1810) and various remarks from *Maxims and Reflections*. Wittgenstein's lifelong engagement with Goethe as a methodological model (cited in *Culture and Value*, referenced in *Remarks on Colour*, and underlying the *übersichtliche Darstellung* / perspicuous-representation ideal of PI §122). Goethe's *morphological* method — understanding a plant's growth by arranging its forms in a survey that displays transitions rather than by analyzing it into hidden inner principles — is a direct ancestor of Wittgenstein's grammatical-surveying method. *Relevance: medium-low as direct-ingest target for the current wiki. The morphological-method point is made clearly in PI §122 and does not require Goethe ingestion to install. Low-medium priority follow-up unless the wiki becomes interested in philosophy-of-method at a Goethean register.*

- **G. E. M. Anscombe** — *Intention* (Blackwell 1957; 2nd ed. Harvard UP 2000). Anscombe was Wittgenstein's literary executor, the translator of PI, and the author of *Intention*, the founding modern work on the philosophy of action. *Intention* develops the Wittgensteinian treatment of intending, willing, and practical reasoning systematically — the "under a description" thesis, the practical-syllogism reconstruction, the distinction between intentional and non-intentional action, and the backward-looking / forward-looking structure of intending. Directly extends PI §§621–629 on willing and [[willing-and-what-is-left-over]]. *Relevance: high if the persona-system's design becomes active around action-description, intention, or practical reasoning. Anscombe's treatment would deepen [[intention-embedded-in-its-situation]] and [[willing-and-what-is-left-over]] substantially. High-priority follow-up.*

- **Peter Winch** — *The Idea of a Social Science and its Relation to Philosophy* (Routledge 1958; 2nd ed. 1990) and *Trying to Make Sense* (Blackwell 1987). Winch applies Wittgensteinian thought to social science methodology, developing the thesis that understanding a society requires understanding its form of life from within — the categories of explanation cannot be external. The 1958 book is the foundational statement of Wittgensteinian philosophy of social science and anthropology, and contains the extended reading of [[form-of-life]] that much of the secondary literature operates with. *Relevance: medium-high for the persona-system's form-of-life-participation questions (what does it take for a language-using system to participate in a form of life? — see [[wittgenstein-and-the-persona-project]] tension iv). Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **G. P. Baker and P. M. S. Hacker** — *Wittgenstein: Understanding and Meaning* (Blackwell 1980, 2nd ed. 2005); *Wittgenstein: Rules, Grammar and Necessity* (Blackwell 1985, 2nd ed. 2009); *Wittgenstein: Meaning and Mind* (Blackwell 1990, 2nd ed. Hacker 2019); *Wittgenstein: Mind and Will* (Blackwell 1996, 2nd ed. Hacker 2019). The four-volume analytical commentary on PI that is the most comprehensive section-by-section secondary source in English. Contains detailed textual apparatus, cross-references to the Wittgenstein *Nachlass*, and interpretive essays on each major theme (rule-following, private language, criteria, meaning, understanding, intention). Baker-Hacker are the standard orthodox-analytical reading against which other readings (Kripke, McDowell, Cavell, Diamond) position themselves. *Relevance: medium as reference work; high as secondary-literature anchor. The four-volume commentary is the right place to look up any specific PI section's scholarly interpretation. Medium-high priority as reference resource; not a direct-ingest target but worth having identified.*

- **Saul Kripke** — *Wittgenstein on Rules and Private Language: An Elementary Exposition* (Harvard UP 1982). The book that reframed the Anglo-American reception of PI by arguing that the rule-following paradox (§201) is the central problem of PI and has a skeptical-solution structure (there is no fact that makes your past use of "+" mean addition rather than quaddition; rule-following is grounded only in community agreement). The Kripkean reading is contested (Baker-Hacker, McDowell, Diamond all argue it misreads Wittgenstein — the "Kripkenstein" label marks the gap between Kripke's position and Wittgenstein's), but is widely taught and has generated a major secondary literature in its own right. *Relevance: high for the [[rule-following]] material. The Kripke / Baker-Hacker / McDowell dispute on §201 is a site where the wiki's treatment of rule-following can be substantially deepened. The wiki's current position on rule-following sits closer to the Baker-Hacker / McDowell (practice-grounded, not skeptical-solution) reading than to Kripke's, but the Kripkean reading's framing is so influential that engaging it is necessary for any sustained wiki work on §201. High-priority follow-up.*

- **John McDowell** — *Mind and World* (Harvard UP 1994) and the essays collected in *Mind, Value, and Reality* (Harvard UP 1998), especially "Non-Cognitivism and Rule-Following" (1981), "Wittgenstein on Following a Rule" (1984), and "Virtue and Reason" (1979). McDowell's rule-following essays are among the most influential post-Kripke interventions, arguing against both the Kripkean skeptical-solution reading and the "straight" anti-skeptical reading, developing a position where rule-following is grounded in second-nature — the cultivated rationality that comes with acculturation into a language and form of life. *Mind and World* extends this to perception, concept-possession, and the relation of mind and world at large, developing a Kantian-Wittgensteinian position that receptivity is already conceptually structured (the "space of reasons" as the space of the intelligible). Directly bears on the wiki's questions about what it takes for a language-using system to participate in rule-governed practice. *Relevance: high. McDowell is the contemporary philosopher whose position most directly engages the wiki's central question (what kind of participation in rule-following / form-of-life is required for meaningful language-use?). High-priority follow-up.*

- **Stanley Cavell** — *The Claim of Reason: Wittgenstein, Skepticism, Morality, and Tragedy* (Oxford UP 1979). The monumental reading of PI arguing that Wittgenstein's treatment of criteria, the other-minds problem, and skepticism is ultimately not a refutation of skepticism but a *re-description* of what skepticism is — an expression of a standing human condition of separateness rather than a theoretical position to be answered. Cavell develops the concept of "attitude towards a soul" (PI II.iv) and [[attitude-towards-a-soul]] material extensively, and his treatment of the private-language argument and the nature of criteria is influential but stands somewhat apart from the Anglo-American mainstream (Baker-Hacker, Kripke, McDowell). *Relevance: medium-high for the [[attitude-towards-a-soul]] and [[first-person-asymmetry]] material, and for the general question of what kind of acknowledgment-relation the persona-system can have with its users or the other personas it co-exists with. Medium-high priority follow-up; Cavell's prose is demanding but the position is distinctive.*

- **Cora Diamond** — *The Realistic Spirit: Wittgenstein, Philosophy, and the Mind* (MIT Press 1991) and various essays. Diamond is a central figure in the "resolute reading" or "New Wittgenstein" movement, arguing against both the *Tractatus*/PI discontinuity thesis and the standard reading of the *Tractatus* as containing a substantive (even if ineffable) metaphysics. For PI, Diamond's work on aspect-seeing, moral thought, and the relationship between philosophy and literature is influential. *Relevance: medium. Diamond's position is distinctive enough to be worth engaging if the wiki develops sustained interest in the PI / *Tractatus* relation or in the ethical register of Wittgenstein's thought. Medium-priority follow-up.*

- **Meredith Williams** — *Wittgenstein, Mind and Meaning: Towards a Social Conception of Mind* (Routledge 1999) and *Blind Obedience: Paradox and Learning in the Later Wittgenstein* (Routledge 2010). *Blind Obedience* develops a specifically developmental reading of §201 — rule-following rests on the novice's initial "blind obedience" to training, which is not yet rule-following but is what rule-following grows out of. The book's treatment of the learner / initiate / master developmental trajectory is directly relevant to the persona-system's training-distribution question. *Relevance: medium-high for the rule-following / training-distribution axis. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Barry Stroud** — "Wittgenstein and Logical Necessity" (*Philosophical Review* 1965) and various essays on the private-language argument and skepticism. Stroud's 1965 paper is a foundational piece of Wittgenstein scholarship on the rule-following / necessity questions; his later work on skepticism and the private-language argument is careful and widely cited. *Relevance: medium. Stroud is a reliable scholarly interlocutor on rule-following and private-language questions; secondary reading rather than direct-ingest priority. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **David Pears** — *The False Prison: A Study of the Development of Wittgenstein's Philosophy* Volumes I and II (Oxford UP 1987, 1988). The major English-language developmental study tracking Wittgenstein's philosophy from the *Tractatus* through the transitional period (*Philosophical Remarks*, *Philosophical Grammar*, *Blue and Brown Books*) to PI. Pears' reading is historicist and emphasizes the continuities as well as the breaks across Wittgenstein's career. *Relevance: medium-low as direct-ingest target. Useful as a developmental reference if the wiki becomes interested in the evolution of Wittgenstein's thought; lower priority than the section-by-section Baker-Hacker commentary for specific-passage interpretation. Medium-low priority follow-up.*

- **Hans-Johann Glock** — *A Wittgenstein Dictionary* (Blackwell 1996). The standard alphabetical reference for Wittgenstein's technical vocabulary across the full corpus (early, middle, late). Invaluable for quickly locating Wittgenstein's use of any specific term. *Relevance: medium as reference resource; low as direct-reading target. The wiki could benefit from having Glock's dictionary identified as a resource for cross-reference questions. Low-medium priority as acquisition; not a reading-target.*

- **Rush Rhees** — *Wittgenstein and the Possibility of Discourse* (Cambridge UP 1998, posthumous) and as editor of *The Blue and Brown Books*, *Philosophical Grammar*, and *Philosophical Remarks*. Rhees was one of Wittgenstein's literary executors (alongside Anscombe and von Wright) and the editor / preserver of much of the *Nachlass*. His own philosophical work, especially *Wittgenstein and the Possibility of Discourse*, argues for a communication-centered reading of PI that emphasizes the interconnection of language-games in conversation rather than treating language-games as modular units. *Relevance: medium-low as direct-ingest target; medium as editorial-role context for understanding the Wittgenstein corpus's publication history. Low-medium priority follow-up.*

- **Friedrich Waismann** — *The Principles of Linguistic Philosophy* (ed. R. Harré from Waismann's 1939 lectures, Macmillan 1965) and *Wittgenstein and the Vienna Circle* (transcripts of conversations with Wittgenstein and Schlick, 1929–32, ed. B. McGuinness 1979). Waismann was Wittgenstein's Vienna-Circle interlocutor in the early-middle period and the source of several Wittgensteinian concepts that do not appear under Wittgenstein's own name in the *Investigations* — notably the concept of "open texture" (*Porosität*) of empirical concepts, which is often attributed to Wittgenstein but is properly Waismann's development. *Relevance: low-medium. Waismann is a specialist follow-up if the wiki becomes interested in open-texture or in the Vienna-Circle context of the *Tractatus*-to-PI transition. Low-medium priority.*

## Cited in *Écrits*

- **Sigmund Freud** — *The Interpretation of Dreams* (1900, *Die Traumdeutung*). The single most-referenced Freud text across *Écrits*. Lacan's reading of dream-work as language-work (condensation = metaphor; displacement = metonymy; the dream's manifest content as rebus/transcription, not symbol-decoding) underwrites [[symptom-as-metaphor-desire-as-metonymy]], [[lacanian-signifier]], and the whole Rome Discourse claim that the unconscious operates by linguistic mechanisms. *Relevance: high. The wiki holds Freud almost entirely through D&G's polemical re-reading and now Lacan's structural re-reading; direct Freud ingest would ground both. High-priority follow-up.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — *Three Essays on the Theory of Sexuality* (1905, *Drei Abhandlungen zur Sexualtheorie*). Lacan's reading of partial drives, infantile sexuality, the disjunction between aim and object, and the polymorphous-perverse infant ground his account of [[drive-as-circular]] and the partial-drives that circle around the lost object ([[object-a]]). *Relevance: high. Directly bears on the partial-objects axis where D&G and Lacan diverge most sharply. High-priority follow-up.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — *Beyond the Pleasure Principle* (1920, *Jenseits des Lustprinzips*). Lacan's most-cited late Freud text. The death drive, the compulsion to repeat, the Fort/Da game (which Lacan reads as the child's first symbolization), and the texture of repetition-as-symbolic ([[repetition-as-symbolic]]) all derive from this text. The Real ([[real-as-impossible]]) and jouissance ([[jouissance-and-pleasure]]) as concepts presuppose the Freudian beyond-pleasure-principle. *Relevance: high. The death-drive / repetition / jouissance axis is unintelligible without direct Freud reading. High-priority follow-up.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — "From the History of an Infantile Neurosis" (1918, the **Wolf Man case**, *Aus der Geschichte einer infantilen Neurose*). Lacan's recurrent reference for the structure of the obsessional neurosis ([[clinical-structures-neurosis-perversion-psychosis]]) and for the temporal-retroactive structure of the primal scene's signification ([[future-anterior-subject]], *Nachträglichkeit* / après-coup). Already cited via [[case-analyses-wolf-man-schreber]] (D&G ingest) — Lacan's reading is structurally different from D&G's. *Relevance: high. The wiki now holds the Wolf Man case from two opposing readings (D&G structural-political, Lacan symbolic-temporal); direct Freud reading would ground both. High-priority follow-up.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — "Notes upon a Case of Obsessional Neurosis" (1909, the **Rat Man case**, *Bemerkungen über einen Fall von Zwangsneurose*). Lacan's principal case-text for the obsessional structure ([[clinical-structures-neurosis-perversion-psychosis]]) and for the insult-as-origin-of-metaphor analysis ("Du Lampe!" — the father's furious insult to a candle, which Lacan reads as the moment where metaphor *originates* by substituting an inanimate object for an animate insult-object — folded into [[symptom-as-metaphor-desire-as-metonymy]]). *Relevance: high. The Rat Man case grounds the obsessional-structure account and the insult-as-metaphor analysis. High-priority follow-up.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — "Psycho-Analytic Notes on an Autobiographical Account of a Case of Paranoia" (1911, the **Schreber case**, *Psychoanalytische Bemerkungen über einen autobiographisch beschriebenen Fall von Paranoia*). Lacan's principal case-text for psychosis and for the foreclosure-of-the-Name-of-the-Father mechanism ([[foreclosure-and-return-in-real]], [[name-of-the-father]], [[clinical-structures-neurosis-perversion-psychosis]]). The Schreber Schema (folded into [[l-schema-and-optical-model]]) is Lacan's late re-reading of the case. Already cited via [[case-analyses-wolf-man-schreber]] (D&G ingest). *Relevance: high. The wiki now holds Schreber from D&G's political-cosmological reading and Lacan's foreclosure-account; both presuppose Freud's text. High-priority follow-up; would pair with direct Schreber Memoirs reading (already listed in AO section).*

- **Sigmund Freud** — *Project for a Scientific Psychology* (1895, posthumous, *Entwurf einer Psychologie*). The early neurological-economic model of the psychic apparatus. Lacan's references to "*Wahrnehmungszeichen*" (perception-signs), the breach (*Bahnung*), and the early account of memory-as-trace bear on [[the-letter-purloined]] (the materiality of the letter), [[lacanian-signifier]] (the signifier as trace), and [[lamella-and-libido-as-organ]] (the libido as quasi-organ derives partly from the *Project*'s economic apparatus). *Relevance: medium-high. The *Project* is the archaeological substrate of much Lacanian apparatus. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — "A Note upon the 'Mystic Writing-Pad'" (1925, *Notiz über den "Wunderblock"*). The famous short essay on memory-as-writing-surface. Lacan cites it as model for the unconscious as inscriptive system (the wax slab below preserves traces, the celluloid above receives new inscription) — bears on [[the-letter-purloined]] and [[lacanian-signifier]]. Derrida's "Freud and the Scene of Writing" (in *Writing and Difference*) is the extended reading. *Relevance: medium. Already partially absorbed via Derrida; a direct read would clarify what Lacan retains and what Derrida transforms. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — "Negation" (1925, *Die Verneinung*). The brief paper that Jean Hyppolite presented and commented on at Lacan's seminar (Lacan's 1954 commentary on Hyppolite's commentary appears in *Écrits* as "Réponse au commentaire de Jean Hyppolite sur la *Verneinung* de Freud"). The three-negation analysis (negation of judgment / negation of expulsion / negation of the unconscious wish) underlies [[foreclosure-and-return-in-real]] (foreclosure = *Verwerfung*, distinct from *Verneinung* and *Verdrängung*) and [[three-negations]] (existing wiki page from prior work). *Relevance: high. Short text, structurally load-bearing for the foreclosure / negation / repression distinction. High-priority follow-up; pairs with Hyppolite's commentary.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — *Totem and Taboo* (1913, *Totem und Tabu*). Lacan's references to the primal-father myth, the murder of the primal father, and the institution of the law-after-the-deed underlie [[name-of-the-father]] (the dead father as the structural Name) and [[ethics-of-psychoanalysis-lacan]]. Already cited via D&G's AO ingest. *Relevance: medium-high. The primal-father myth has divergent readings in D&G (critique) and Lacan (structural appropriation). Medium-high priority follow-up if the dead-father / Name axis becomes design-active.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — *Civilization and Its Discontents* (1930, *Das Unbehagen in der Kultur*). Lacan's references to the death-drive at civilizational scale, the renunciation of jouissance for the sake of the social bond, and the structural impossibility of full satisfaction underlie [[jouissance-and-pleasure]], [[ethics-of-psychoanalysis-lacan]], and [[kant-with-sade]]. Already cited via D&G's AO ingest. *Relevance: medium-high. Bears on the ethics-and-jouissance axis. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — "On Narcissism: An Introduction" (1914, *Zur Einführung des Narzißmus*). Lacan's principal Freud-source for the [[two-narcissisms]] account (primary / secondary, ego-libido / object-libido) and for the ego-formation theory underlying [[mirror-stage-lacan]] and [[ego-as-resistance]]. *Relevance: high. Direct ingest would ground the early-Lacan ego-account and the libido-economic apparatus. High-priority follow-up.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — "The Ego and the Id" (1923, *Das Ich und das Es*). The second-topography (id/ego/superego) text. Lacan's references underlie his account of the superego's relation to the law and jouissance ([[ethics-of-psychoanalysis-lacan]], [[kant-with-sade]]) and his "Wo Es war, soll Ich werden" reading ([[wo-es-war-soll-ich-werden]] — the famous mistranslation/restatement that "where it was, I should come to be" is the analytic ethic). *Relevance: high. The Lacan reading of Wo-Es-war is unintelligible without the Freud text. High-priority follow-up.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — "Inhibitions, Symptoms and Anxiety" (1926, *Hemmung, Symptom und Angst*). Lacan's principal Freud-source for the late reformulation of anxiety and for the symptom-formation account underlying [[anxiety-not-without-object]] (Lacan's reading: anxiety is *not without object* — *pas sans objet* — contra the standard reading of anxiety as objectless). *Relevance: high. The "not without object" formula is structural to the late Lacan. High-priority follow-up.*

- **Sigmund Freud** — "The Unconscious" (1915, *Das Unbewusste*) and the other 1915 metapsychological papers ("Repression," "Instincts and their Vicissitudes," "Mourning and Melancholia"). Lacan's most-cited Freudian metapsychological cluster — the unconscious as structurally distinct system, the four destinies of the drive (reversal into opposite, turning around upon the subject's own self, repression, sublimation), the structure of mourning. Underlie [[lacanian-signifier]], [[drive-as-circular]], and the broader account of unconscious mechanisms. *Relevance: high. The metapsychology papers are the structural core of the Freudian apparatus Lacan presupposes. High-priority follow-up.*

- **Martin Heidegger** — *Being and Time* (1927, *Sein und Zeit*) and the post-*Kehre* essays "The Origin of the Work of Art," "What Is Metaphysics?," "Letter on Humanism," "The Thing," and Heidegger's translations of and commentaries on Heraclitus and Parmenides. Lacan's principal philosophical reference for the late period (after the 1953 turn). The concept of *ek-sistence* (folded into [[real-as-impossible]]), the *Lichtung* (clearing), the relation of speech and Being, and the late-Heideggerian critique of the metaphysics of presence inform Lacan's account of the speaking-being, the Real-as-impossible, and the ethics of speech. Lacan attended Heidegger's Cerisy lectures; Heidegger visited Lacan in France. *Relevance: high. Heidegger is the philosophical interlocutor most directly absorbed into Lacan's mature apparatus. High-priority follow-up; *Being and Time* in the Stambaugh translation (SUNY 2010) is the standard. Already on the wiki's radar via the Husserl-Derrida axis and via Merleau-Ponty's late chiasm material.*

- **Alexandre Kojève** — *Introduction to the Reading of Hegel: Lectures on the Phenomenology of Spirit* (lectures 1933–1939 at the École Pratique des Hautes Études; ed. Raymond Queneau, Gallimard 1947; English tr. James H. Nichols, Basic Books 1969). Kojève's anthropological-existentialist reading of Hegel's master/slave dialectic, the desire-of-the-other formulation ("human desire is desire for the desire of the other"), and the end-of-history thesis are Lacan's principal Hegelian source. The [[desire-of-the-other]] formula is Kojévean before it is Lacanian. Lacan attended Kojève's seminar (alongside Bataille, Merleau-Ponty, Aron, Queneau, Lévinas). *Relevance: high. Direct ingest would ground the desire-of-the-other axis at its philosophical source. High-priority follow-up.*

- **Jean Hyppolite** — *Genesis and Structure of Hegel's Phenomenology of Spirit* (1946, *Genèse et structure de la Phénoménologie de l'esprit de Hegel*) and *Logic and Existence* (1953, *Logique et existence*). Hyppolite was Lacan's friend, collaborator, and Hegel-interpreter; his 1954 presentation of Freud's "Verneinung" at Lacan's seminar (with Lacan's commentary published in *Écrits*) is the classic instance. Hyppolite's reading of Hegel is more textual and less anthropological than Kojève's; the two together comprise Lacan's Hegel-reception. *Relevance: medium-high for the Hegel-Freud axis ([[three-negations]], [[foreclosure-and-return-in-real]], [[wo-es-war-soll-ich-werden]]). Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Ferdinand de Saussure** — *Course in General Linguistics* (1916). Lacan's foundational structural-linguistic source for the signifier-signified distinction, the arbitrariness of the sign (which Lacan radicalizes), and the structural-system conception of *langue*. The famous algorithm S/s (signifier over signified, with the bar resisting signification) in "The Instance of the Letter in the Unconscious" is Lacan's transformation of the Saussurean sign. Already cited above (Benveniste section). *Relevance: medium-high for the Lacan-Saussure axis specifically (as distinct from the Benveniste-Saussure axis). The wiki holds Saussure through Benveniste; direct Saussure ingest would clarify what Lacan transforms. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Roman Jakobson** — "Two Aspects of Language and Two Types of Aphasic Disturbances" (1956, in *Fundamentals of Language* with Morris Halle). The text from which Lacan derives the metaphor (paradigmatic substitution / similarity disorder) vs metonymy (syntagmatic combination / contiguity disorder) distinction that grounds [[symptom-as-metaphor-desire-as-metonymy]] and [[lacanian-signifier]]. Lacan adopts Jakobson's terms but redirects them — Jakobson uses them descriptively for aphasia; Lacan uses them as the structure of the unconscious's signifier-operations. *Relevance: high. The metaphor/metonymy axis is unintelligible without Jakobson. High-priority follow-up; short text, would pair well with Saussure.*

- **Claude Lévi-Strauss** — *The Elementary Structures of Kinship* (1949, *Les structures élémentaires de la parenté*) and *Structural Anthropology* (1958/1973). Lacan's principal structuralist anthropologist source — kinship as exchange-of-women structured by the prohibition of incest, the symbolic order as the social-structural condition of all human exchange, the unconscious as the structural-symbolic apparatus. Bears on [[name-of-the-father]] (the paternal prohibition as structural condition) and [[the-other-and-unconscious-discourse]] (the symbolic order as the Other). Already cited via D&G's AO ingest. *Relevance: medium-high. The wiki holds Lévi-Strauss through D&G's critique; direct ingest would ground the Lacanian structural-symbolic axis. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Edgar Allan Poe** — "The Purloined Letter" (1844). Lacan's principal literary-text reference. The "Seminar on 'The Purloined Letter'" (1956, the most-front-loaded text in *Écrits*) reads Poe's story as the demonstration of the signifier's autonomy — the letter circulates and structures the positions of its holders independently of its content. Grounds [[the-letter-purloined]] and [[lacanian-signifier]]. *Relevance: high as text-to-have-read. The Poe story itself is a 30-page short story; direct reading is essentially required to read [[the-letter-purloined]] productively. Already absorbed at the level of the wiki's treatment; the Poe text itself should be read alongside.*

- **Marquis de Sade** — *Philosophy in the Bedroom* (1795, *La philosophie dans le boudoir*) and *Juliette* (1797–1801). Lacan's principal Sadean source. "Kant with Sade" (1962) reads Sade's Yes-Above-All as the structural other-side of Kant's categorical imperative — both elevate the law above the good, both make the subject the agent of an unconditional law that crushes pathological inclination. Grounds [[kant-with-sade]] and bears on [[ethics-of-psychoanalysis-lacan]] and [[jouissance-and-pleasure]]. *Relevance: high for the Kant-with-Sade reading. Direct reading is structurally necessary for productive engagement with Lacan's text. High-priority follow-up; *Philosophy in the Bedroom* is the principal target.*

- **Immanuel Kant** — *Critique of Practical Reason* (1788, *Kritik der praktischen Vernunft*) and *Critique of Pure Reason* (1781/1787, *Kritik der reinen Vernunft*). Lacan's principal Kant-source. The categorical-imperative analysis in [[kant-with-sade]] presupposes the *second Critique*; the discussion of the subject of science in [[cogito-and-subject-of-science]] presupposes the transcendental-subject apparatus of the *first Critique*. *Relevance: high. Direct ingest would ground both the Kant-with-Sade material and the broader Lacanian engagement with the philosophical-subject tradition. High-priority follow-up; *Critique of Practical Reason* is the more focused target.*

- **G. W. F. Hegel** — *Phenomenology of Spirit* (1807, *Phänomenologie des Geistes*). Already cited above (via Kojève and Hyppolite). The Hegel Lacan absorbs is the Hegel-of-the-master/slave-dialectic and the Hegel-of-recognition; the *Phenomenology* itself is the source-text. *Relevance: high. Direct ingest would deepen [[desire-of-the-other]], [[future-anterior-subject]] (the future-anterior is partly Hegelian), and the broader dialectical apparatus. High-priority but very large; pairs with Kojève as the entry point.*

- **Plato** — *Symposium* (c. 385–370 BCE). Lacan's seminar on transference (Seminar VIII, *Le transfert*, 1960–61) is constructed around the *Symposium*'s Socrates-Alcibiades scene as the paradigm of transference and the position of the *agalma* (the precious object hidden inside Socrates that Alcibiades desires). Bears on [[transference-and-subject-supposed-to-know]] and [[object-a]]. *Relevance: high for the transference axis. The *Symposium* is short; direct reading is structurally easy and would ground Seminar VIII (which is on the high-priority follow-up list above). High-priority follow-up.*

- **Aristotle** — *De Anima* (*On the Soul*, c. 350 BCE) and *Nicomachean Ethics* (c. 340 BCE). Lacan's principal Aristotle-sources. The four causes (material / formal / efficient / final), which Lacan redistributes in his account of the signifier as causa-formalis (folded into the wiki via [[four-causes-redistributed]] and [[lacanian-signifier]]); the *Nicomachean Ethics* concept of *eudaimonia* and the relation of pleasure to the good underlie Lacan's polemic against the ethics-of-the-good in [[ethics-of-psychoanalysis-lacan]] (which positions the ethic-of-desire against the Aristotelian-Stoic-Christian ethic-of-the-good). *Relevance: medium-high. The four-causes redistribution is structural to Lacan's late account of causality; the ethics polemic presupposes the Aristotelian frame. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Augustine of Hippo** — *De Magistro* (*On the Teacher*, c. 389 CE) and *Confessions* (c. 397–400 CE). Augustine appears in *Écrits* as: (i) the source of the famous example of the child's jealous gaze at the brother nursing at the mother's breast (cited in "Aggressivity in Psychoanalysis") — grounds the early-Lacan account of aggressivity and [[two-narcissisms]]; (ii) a structural reference in Lacan's account of speech-and-language, given Augustine's status as the founding theorist of the inner word and confession-as-speech-act. *Relevance: medium. Already cited above (Wittgenstein section, where *Confessions* I.8 grounds the Augustinian picture of language PI works against). Direct ingest would serve both axes. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Blaise Pascal** — *Pensées* (1670, posthumous). Lacan cites Pascal at several points, including the wager (which Lacan reads structurally as a forced choice — the structure underlying [[alienation-and-separation]]'s vel of alienation: "your money or your life") and the "*le moi est haïssable*" formula. *Relevance: medium. Pascal is a recurring philosophical reference but not load-bearing for any single page. Medium-low priority follow-up.*

- **Heraclitus** — Fragments (c. 500 BCE). Lacan's principal pre-Socratic reference, often via Heidegger's commentaries. The fragments on *logos*, *physis*, the agreement/disagreement of opposites, the never-stepping-into-the-same-river-twice ground Lacan's treatments of speech, the unconscious's logic of contraries, and the Real-as-impossible. *Relevance: low-medium. The Heraclitean fragments are short and would pair productively with any Heidegger ingest. Low-medium priority follow-up.*

- **Søren Kierkegaard** — *Repetition* (1843, *Gjentagelsen*) and *The Concept of Anxiety* (1844, *Begrebet Angest*). Lacan's principal Kierkegaard-sources. The Kierkegaardian *Gjentagelse* (repetition-as-recovery-in-the-future, distinct from recollection-as-recovery-from-the-past) underlies [[repetition-as-symbolic]]; *The Concept of Anxiety* as the philosophical predecessor of [[anxiety-not-without-object]]. *Relevance: medium-high. Both texts are short and structurally load-bearing. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Émile Benveniste** — *Problems in General Linguistics* Vol I (1966). Already cited above (Linguistics primary texts section, with full *Ingested* treatment). Lacan engages Benveniste directly on the I/you correlativity, the instance of discourse, and the subject-of-the-statement vs subject-of-the-enunciation distinction. *Relevance: ingested 2026-04-17. The Benveniste-Lacan tension on unconscious-as-style vs unconscious-as-language is now held from both sides.*

- **Charles Sanders Peirce** — semiotic writings (collected in *Collected Papers*, 8 vols., 1931–1958). Lacan engages Peirce's icon/index/symbol triadic semiotics in "Subversion of the Subject" and elsewhere, distinguishing the Lacanian signifier from the Peircean sign. Already cited (Chaosmosis section). *Relevance: medium. The wiki holds Peirce via the [[pragmatic-maxim]] page; direct engagement with Peirce's semiotics would clarify what Lacan rejects of the icon/index/symbol triad. Medium-priority follow-up.*

- **Daniel Lagache** — French psychoanalyst and one of Lacan's principal interlocutors in the SFP period. Lagache's distinction between the "ego" and the "subject" (and his structural-personalist apparatus) is the target of Lacan's critique in "Remarks on Daniel Lagache's Presentation: 'Psychoanalysis and Personality Structure'" (in *Écrits*). *Relevance: low-medium. Lagache is read primarily through Lacan's critique; direct ingest would deepen the institutional-historical context. Low-medium priority follow-up.*

- **Anna Freud** — *The Ego and the Mechanisms of Defense* (1936). The principal target of Lacan's critique of ego-psychology, which dominated post-WWII Anglo-American psychoanalysis. Lacan's [[ego-as-resistance]] and the polemic across *Écrits* against "ego-strengthening" therapy presupposes Anna Freud's text. *Relevance: medium. Direct ingest would ground the polemical target of the Lacanian therapeutic position. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Heinz Hartmann** — *Ego Psychology and the Problem of Adaptation* (1939, *Ich-Psychologie und Anpassungsproblem*). The principal theoretical statement of post-Freudian ego psychology (the autonomous ego, conflict-free sphere, adaptation). Lacan's critique of ego psychology targets Hartmann, Kris, and Loewenstein as its US triumvirate. *Relevance: medium. Direct ingest would ground the polemical context. Medium priority follow-up; pairs with Anna Freud.*

- **Maurice Merleau-Ponty** — *Phenomenology of Perception* (1945, *Phénoménologie de la perception*) and *The Visible and the Invisible* (1964, posthumous, *Le visible et l'invisible*). Lacan and Merleau-Ponty were close interlocutors; Lacan's [[gaze-and-voice]] account is in dialogue with Merleau-Ponty's chiasmic visibility-of-the-invisible material. Already extensively in the wiki via the Merleau-Ponty ingest (2026-04-18, parallel to the Lacan ingest). *Relevance: ingested. The Lacan-Merleau-Ponty cross-tradition links are now in the wiki.*

## Cited in *Surfing Uncertainty*

- **Andy Clark** — *Surfing Uncertainty: Prediction, Action, and the Embodied Mind* (Oxford UP 2016). **Ingested 2026-04-18.** Clark's systematic synthesis of the predictive-processing research program. Core contributions: the top-down / bottom-up inversion ([[predictive-processing]]); generative models and the Helmholtz Machine ([[generative-model]]); prediction-error as signal ([[prediction-error]]); precision-weighting as attention and gain ([[precision-weighting]]); active inference as perception-action unification ([[active-inference]]); affordance-competition ([[affordance-competition-hypothesis]]); the perception/imagination/memory/planning cognitive-package-deal ([[cognitive-package-deal]]); interoceptive inference ([[interoceptive-inference]]); words as precision tools ([[words-as-precision-tools]]); designer environments and the cognitive niche ([[designer-environments-and-cognitive-niche]]); TALoNS ([[transiently-assembled-local-neural-subsystems]]); the darkened-room puzzle and its dissolution ([[darkened-room-puzzle]]); hallucination as uncontrolled perception ([[hallucination-as-uncontrolled-perception]]); computational psychiatry ([[computational-psychiatry]]); self/other via precision ([[self-other-via-precision]]); sensory attenuation and agency ([[sensory-attenuation-and-agency]]); optimal illusions ([[optimal-illusions]]); non-reconstructive strategies ([[non-reconstructive-strategies]]); itinerant dynamics and novelty-seeking ([[itinerant-dynamics-and-novelty-seeking]]); mutual prediction loops ([[mutual-prediction-loops]]); self-narrative as high-level prior ([[self-narrative-as-high-level-prior]]); gist and affective gist ([[gist-and-affective-gist]]). See [[log]] 2026-04-18 for the full ingest summary.

- **Andy Clark** — *Being There: Putting Brain, Body, and World Together Again* (MIT 1997), *Natural-Born Cyborgs* (Oxford UP 2003), *Supersizing the Mind: Embodiment, Action, and Cognitive Extension* (Oxford UP 2008). Clark's three earlier books on extended cognition and the human-environment coupling.
