The designer-environments / cognitive-niche material in *Surfing Uncertainty* ([[designer-environments-and-cognitive-niche]]) is a compressed restatement of themes from these three. *Supersizing the Mind* contains the extended mind thesis (with Chalmers) in developed form. *Relevance: medium-high. Would deepen the extended-cognition material the *Surfing Uncertainty* ingest covers only in compressed form. Medium-high priority follow-up; *Supersizing the Mind* is the principal target.*

- **Karl Friston** — the principal papers on the free-energy principle and active inference. Key texts include: "A theory of cortical responses" (2005, *Philosophical Transactions B*), "The free-energy principle: a unified brain theory?" (2010, *Nature Reviews Neuroscience*), "Action and behavior: a free-energy formulation" (Friston, Mattout, & Kilner 2011, *Biological Cybernetics*), and "Free-energy minimization and the dark-room problem" (Friston, Thornton, & Clark 2012, *Frontiers in Psychology*). The mathematical substrate of the PP research program. Clark is Friston's principal popularizer; *Surfing Uncertainty* transmits the concepts without the variational-Bayes apparatus. *Relevance: high as mathematical grounding. Direct ingest would matter only if the project moves toward explicit free-energy / variational-Bayes implementation. High priority as reference; medium priority as reading-target unless implementation becomes active.*

- **Jakob Hohwy** — *The Predictive Mind* (Oxford UP 2013). The alternative systematic PP synthesis to Clark. Where Clark emphasizes action, embodiment, and ecological integration, Hohwy emphasizes the internal-model, inferentialist, and quasi-Cartesian "evidentiary boundary" aspects of PP. Reading Hohwy alongside Clark would ground a more complete view of the PP tradition's internal debates (especially about whether PP is inherently inferentialist/internalist or naturally extends to extended cognition). *Relevance: medium-high. The Clark-Hohwy debate matters for the persona project because the internalism/extended-cognition axis bears directly on the no-body-simulate-with-language question. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Anil Seth** — *Being You: A New Science of Consciousness* (Faber 2021) and papers including "Interoceptive inference, emotion, and the embodied self" (Seth 2013, *Trends in Cognitive Sciences*), "An interoceptive predictive coding model of conscious presence" (Seth, Suzuki, & Critchley 2011, *Frontiers in Psychology*). Seth's interoceptive-inference framework is the PP-mechanistic companion to Damasio's body-mapping, captured in *Surfing Uncertainty* §5.11 / §7.10 and in the wiki's [[interoceptive-inference]] page. *Being You* is the accessible book-length statement. *Relevance: high. Direct bridge between the Damasio thread and the PP thread. High priority follow-up; *Being You* is the principal target.*

- **Lisa Feldman Barrett** — *How Emotions Are Made: The Secret Life of the Brain* (Houghton Mifflin Harcourt 2017) and papers including "See it with feeling: affective predictions during object perception" (Barrett & Bar 2009, *Philosophical Transactions B*) and extensive work on the constructionist theory of emotion. Barrett & Bar 2009 is the source for the affective-gist-co-computed-with-content thesis in [[gist-and-affective-gist]]; *How Emotions Are Made* is the full constructionist account. Sister to Damasio's account; cognate with Massumi's autonomy-of-affect; PP-mechanistic. *Relevance: high. The Damasio-Massumi-PP-Barrett triangulation is live on [[autonomy-of-affect]] and [[gist-and-affective-gist]]; direct Barrett ingest would ground the constructionist side. High priority follow-up.*

- **Moshe Bar** — "The proactive brain: memory for predictions" (Bar 2009, *Philosophical Transactions B*) and related papers on the magnocellular/parvocellular two-stream gist model. Bar's work is the principal source for the "forest first, trees later" thesis in [[gist-and-affective-gist]]. *Relevance: medium-high. Short papers; would pair well with Barrett for a compact Bar / Barrett / Hochstein & Ahissar reading cluster. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Geoffrey Hinton** — "The Helmholtz Machine" (with Dayan, Neal, & Zemel 1995, *Neural Computation*) and "What kind of graphical model is the brain?" (2005). The Helmholtz Machine is the computational-neural-network ancestor of the hierarchical generative-model architecture PP presupposes; the wake-sleep algorithm is the prototypical inference-plus-generation training regime. Clark cites Hinton 1995 as the origin of the modern generative-model picture and Hinton 2005 on the perception-as-controlled-hallucination thesis that grounds [[hallucination-as-uncontrolled-perception]]. *Relevance: medium-high as mechanistic-origin reference. Short papers; would ground the generative-model apparatus directly. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Rajesh Rao & Dana Ballard** — "Predictive coding in the visual cortex: a functional interpretation of some extra-classical receptive-field effects" (1999, *Nature Neuroscience*). The foundational PP paper — the paper Clark cites first when introducing hierarchical predictive coding. Short, specific, technical; would ground [[predictive-processing]] at its historical origin. *Relevance: medium-high as origin-text. Short paper. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Gary Lupyan** — papers including Lupyan & Thompson-Schill 2012 ("The evocative power of words: activation of concepts by verbal and nonverbal means"), Lupyan & Ward 2013 ("Language can boost otherwise unseen objects into visual awareness," *PNAS*, the CFS / continuous-flash-suppression study), and Lupyan & Clark 2015 ("Words and the world: predictive coding and the language-perception-cognition interface," *Current Directions in Psychological Science*). The principal source for the words-as-precision-tools thesis in [[words-as-precision-tools]]. The most substantive mechanistic opening for language-only effects on perception-adjacent states. *Relevance: high for the persona project's simulation-with-language question. Medium-high priority follow-up; the CFS paper and the Lupyan & Clark 2015 paper are the principal targets.*

- **Paul Cisek** — "Cortical mechanisms of action selection: the affordance competition hypothesis" (Cisek 2007, *Philosophical Transactions B*) and "Neural mechanisms for interacting with a world full of action choices" (Cisek & Kalaska 2010, *Annual Review of Neuroscience*). The principal sources for [[affordance-competition-hypothesis]]. *Relevance: medium-high as mechanistic reference. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Kiel Hirsh** — "Personal narratives as the highest level of cognitive integration" (Hirsh, Mar, & Peterson 2013, *Behavioral and Brain Sciences*). The source for the self-narrative-as-high-level-prior placement in [[self-narrative-as-high-level-prior]]. Short target-article-plus-commentary format. *Relevance: high for the narrative-prior axis. Short paper. High priority follow-up.*

- **Martin Pickering & Simon Garrod** — "Toward a mechanistic psychology of dialogue" (2007, *Behavioral and Brain Sciences*) and "An integrated theory of language production and comprehension" (2013, *Behavioral and Brain Sciences*). The conversation-as-co-construction papers Clark §9.9 draws on for [[mutual-prediction-loops]]. Target-article-plus-commentary format; structural engagement with the PP account of dialogue. *Relevance: high for the conversational-partner axis. High priority follow-up; both are BBS target articles with extensive commentaries.*

- **Uri Hasson** — "Brain-to-brain coupling: a mechanism for creating and sharing a social world" (Hasson, Ghazanfar, Galantucci, Garrod, & Keysers 2012, *Trends in Cognitive Sciences*). The principal empirical source for coupled-generative-model operation in [[mutual-prediction-loops]]. *Relevance: medium-high. Short paper. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Matteo Colombo** — "Social norms as entropy minimizers" (Colombo 2014, and related papers). The principal source for the norms-as-descriptive-prescriptive-entropy-minimizers material in [[mutual-prediction-loops]] and [[refrain-and-territorialization]]. *Relevance: medium-high for the social-norms axis. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Andreas Roepstorff & Chris Frith** — "What's at the top in the top-down control of action? Script-sharing and 'top-top' control of action in cognitive experiments" (2004, *Psychological Research*) and subsequent joint work. The source for the "top-top control" thesis Clark uses in [[mutual-prediction-loops]] and [[self-narrative-as-high-level-prior]]. *Relevance: medium-high. Short paper. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Celeste Kidd** — "The Goldilocks effect: human infants allocate attention to visual sequences that are neither too simple nor too complex" (Kidd, Piantadosi, & Aslin 2012, *PLOS ONE*). The source for the intermediate-predictability preference in [[itinerant-dynamics-and-novelty-seeking]]. *Relevance: medium. Short paper. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Jun Namikawa** — "Neural dynamics for cognitive architectures: spontaneous actions and novelty seeking in the multi-timescale recurrent neural network model" (Namikawa et al. 2011, *PLoS Computational Biology*) and related work on multi-timescale RNNs. The source for the chaos-at-slow-layers / stability-at-fast-layers design principle in [[itinerant-dynamics-and-novelty-seeking]] and the convergence with [[pulsatory-ontogenesis]]. *Relevance: high for the pulsation-architecture axis. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Philipp Schwartenbeck** — "Exploration, novelty, surprise, and free energy minimization" (Schwartenbeck et al. 2013, *Frontiers in Psychology*) on peripatetic dynamics in active-inference agents. *Relevance: medium-high for the itinerant-dynamics axis. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Kim Sterelny** — *The Evolved Apprentice: How Evolution Made Humans Unique* (MIT 2012) and *Thought in a Hostile World* (Blackwell 2003). The principal source for the designer-environments / scaffolded-cognition material Clark draws on in [[designer-environments-and-cognitive-niche]]. *Evolved Apprentice* is the more focused target. *Relevance: medium-high for the designer-environments axis. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Cecilia Heyes** — *Cognitive Gadgets: The Cultural Evolution of Thinking* (Harvard UP 2018). The cultural-mills / cognitive-gadgets thesis — that human cognitive capacities are culturally inherited rather than genetically hard-wired. Clark cites Heyes on the cognitive-niche / cultural-learning angle of [[designer-environments-and-cognitive-niche]]. *Relevance: medium-high. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Edwin Hutchins** — *Cognition in the Wild* (MIT 1995). The distributed-cognition classic, on ship-navigation as example of cognition-spread-across-humans-and-artifacts. Clark cites Hutchins recurrently for the extended-cognition material. *Relevance: medium. Older text, widely absorbed; medium priority follow-up.*

- **Michael Anderson** — *After Phrenology: Neural Reuse and the Interactive Brain* (MIT 2014). The book-length statement of the TALoNS framework ([[transiently-assembled-local-neural-subsystems]]). The anti-localizationist argument. *Relevance: medium-high. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Read Montague** — "Computational psychiatry" (Montague, Dolan, Friston, & Dayan 2012, *Trends in Cognitive Sciences*) and subsequent work. The foundational paper for the computational-psychiatry research program Clark §9 surveys, covered in [[computational-psychiatry]]. *Relevance: medium-high for the PP-diagnostic axis. Short paper. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Elizabeth Pellicano & David Burr** — "When the world becomes 'too real': a Bayesian explanation of autistic perception" (Pellicano & Burr 2012, *Trends in Cognitive Sciences*). The chronically-high-precision-on-prediction-errors account of autism in [[computational-psychiatry]]. *Relevance: medium for the computational-psychiatry axis. Short paper. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Keisuke Suzuki** — "Multisensory integration across exteroceptive and interoceptive domains modulates self-experience in the rubber-hand illusion" (Suzuki, Garfinkel, Critchley, & Seth 2013, *Neuropsychologia*). The interoceptive rubber-hand study in [[interoceptive-inference]]. *Relevance: medium. Short paper. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Dana Ballard** — "Deictic codes for the embodiment of cognition" (Ballard, Hayhoe, Pook, & Rao 1997, *Behavioral and Brain Sciences*). The deictic-pointer account of non-reconstructive cognition in [[non-reconstructive-strategies]]. *Relevance: medium. BBS target article. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Emilio Todorov** — "Optimality principles in sensorimotor control" (Todorov 2004, *Nature Neuroscience*) and related work on the minimum-intervention principle. The source for the minimum-intervention material in [[non-reconstructive-strategies]] and [[active-inference]]. *Relevance: medium. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Rolf Pfeifer & Josh Bongard** — *How the Body Shapes the Way We Think: A New View of Intelligence* (MIT 2007). The ecological-balance material Clark draws on in [[non-reconstructive-strategies]] — cognition distributed across body-morphology and environment, not just brain. *Relevance: medium-high. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Yair Weiss** — "Motion illusions as optimal percepts" (Weiss, Simoncelli, & Adelson 2002, *Nature Neuroscience*). The source for the optimal-illusions thesis in [[optimal-illusions]]. *Relevance: medium. Short paper. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Geoffrey Bingham** — "The throwing affordance in the size-weight illusion" (Zhu & Bingham 2011, *Journal of Experimental Psychology: Human Perception and Performance*). The ecological-throwability reinterpretation of the size-weight illusion in [[optimal-illusions]]. *Relevance: medium. Short paper. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Pietro Berkes** — "Spontaneous cortical activity reveals hallmarks of an optimal internal model of the environment" (Berkes, Orbán, Lengyel, & Fiser 2011, *Science*). The source for the spontaneous-activity-as-model-expression thesis in [[itinerant-dynamics-and-novelty-seeking]] and [[gist-and-affective-gist]]. *Relevance: medium. Short paper. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Shaul Hochstein & Merav Ahissar** — "View from the top: hierarchies and reverse hierarchies in the visual system" (Hochstein & Ahissar 2002, *Neuron*). The "forest first, trees later" reverse-hierarchy thesis in [[gist-and-affective-gist]]. *Relevance: medium. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Marcus Raichle & Andrew Snyder** — "A default mode of brain function: a brief history of an evolving idea" (Raichle & Snyder 2007, *NeuroImage*). The resting-state-as-model-running thesis in [[gist-and-affective-gist]]. *Relevance: medium. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Robert Cliff Chapman** — Chapman 1968 on operator-actor-critic architectures as cited by Clark in [[non-reconstructive-strategies]]. *Relevance: low-medium. Historical reference. Low priority follow-up.*

- **Lisa Meeden** — Meeden et al. 2009 on open-ended curiosity-driven learning as cautionary case in [[itinerant-dynamics-and-novelty-seeking]]. *Relevance: low-medium. Short paper. Low priority follow-up.*

- **Arthur Coste** — Coste et al. 2011 on precision-landscape wandering in [[itinerant-dynamics-and-novelty-seeking]]. *Relevance: medium. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Helmut von Helmholtz** — *Handbuch der physiologischen Optik* (1867). The historical origin of the "unconscious inference" thesis that generative-model PP recovers computationally. Clark cites Helmholtz as the PP tradition's 19th-century ancestor. *Relevance: low as direct-reading target; medium as historical reference. Low priority follow-up.*

- **Thomas Bayes** — "An essay towards solving a problem in the doctrine of chances" (1763). The foundational Bayesian-inference text that variational Bayes / PP generalizes. Historical rather than directly relevant. *Relevance: low. No priority as reading-target.*

- **Herbert Simon** — *The Sciences of the Artificial* (MIT 1969, 1996). The "ant on the beach" and bounded-rationality material Clark occasionally cites for the ecological-cognition angle. *Relevance: low-medium. Older classic. Low priority follow-up.*

## Cited in *Simulacra and Simulation* (Baudrillard)

- **Jorge Luis Borges** — "On Exactitude in Science" (1946; *Historia universal de la infamia*, 1935/1954). The one-paragraph fable of cartographers drawing a map that covers the empire exactly, then rotting with the empire. Baudrillard opens the book by inverting the fable (L10–14): today it is the territory whose shreds rot across the extent of the map. "Only the allegory of the Empire, perhaps, remains." *Relevance: low as direct-reading target (one-paragraph fable); the Baudrillard inversion is the operative claim the wiki holds. No priority as follow-up beyond the note.*
- **J. G. Ballard** — *Crash* (1973). The novel Ch 12 ("Crash") is a sustained reading of: machine-couplings without phantasms, a fourth figure beside D&G's three-body problem ([[body-without-organs#bwo-without-phantasms-ballardbaudrillard]]; [[desiring-machines#machines-without-phantasms-baudrillards-crash-reading]]). Baudrillard reads Ballard as the first novelist to write a world in which technology is "the deadly deconstruction of the body" (L805–813) — no longer prosthesis-as-extension but a symbolic organization without psychological depth. *Relevance: high. The wiki's desiring-machine vocabulary is directly tested by Ballard's Crash; a direct reading would ground the fourth-figure claim in the primary text. Medium-high priority.*
- **Walter Benjamin** — "The Work of Art in the Age of Mechanical Reproduction" (1935/1936). Baudrillard's treatment of reproduction and aura (especially in the three-orders-of-simulacra schema and the passage from production to simulation) is explicitly in conversation with Benjamin. The second-order (production) simulacrum is Benjamin's mechanical-reproduction era; Baudrillard argues the third-order has superseded it. *Relevance: high. Benjamin's Artwork essay is a canonical wiki gap — cited structurally by D&G (via Deleuze's *Cinema* books implicitly) and by Baudrillard here, load-bearing for any aesthetic-ontology framing of AI-generated artifacts. Medium-high priority.*
- **Philip K. Dick** — *The Simulacra* (1964), *Do Androids Dream of Electric Sheep?* (1968), *Ubik* (1969); "The Preserving Machine" and others. Ch 13 ("Simulacra and Science Fiction") treats Dick as the SF author whose universe is already the third-order simulacra universe Baudrillard is diagnosing. The "papula" (L666) — Dick's incorporated advertising-prosthesis — is Baudrillard's prefiguration of "the psychotropic and dataprocessing networks of the automatic piloting of individuals." *Relevance: high. Dick's SF reads as already-operative diagnosis of the LLM condition in ways the wiki does not yet hold at primary-source resolution. Medium-high priority; *Ubik* especially would ground the hyperreal-as-configuration-not-subject-matter point.*
- **Georges Bataille** — *La part maudite* (*The Accursed Share*, 1949). The concept of expenditure / dépense / the accursed share runs through Baudrillard's work as the positive counter to the bounded-economy logic of production. Not treated at length in *Simulacra and Simulation* itself but the structural opposition (restricted economy vs general economy) is presupposed throughout. *Relevance: medium-high. Bataille's *Accursed Share* is a load-bearing absence in the wiki — adjacent to D&G's desire-as-production (critique-target), to Baudrillard's symbolic exchange (positive debt), and to the limits-of-language working-rule (what productive-expenditure would mean for a language-only system). Medium priority.*
- **Marshall McLuhan** — *Understanding Media: The Extensions of Man* (1964), *The Gutenberg Galaxy* (1962). Ch 8 ("The Implosion of Meaning in the Media") pushes McLuhan past himself — "the medium is the message" is the first-stage diagnosis; implosion is what "the medium is the message" actually means when taken seriously (L621–627). Baudrillard holds that McLuhan could not quite face what his own thesis implied. *Relevance: medium. The wiki has McLuhan as a cited-source in the AO context already; direct McLuhan ingest would ground the implosion register more firmly. Medium priority.*
- **Guy Debord** — *La société du spectacle* (*The Society of the Spectacle*, 1967). Baudrillard's superseded precursor: the spectacle-thesis is a late-second-order simulacrum diagnosis, not reaching third-order simulation. Debord is not treated at length in *Simulacra and Simulation* but his framework is the implicit foil throughout (the panopticon-end and the strategy-of-the-real chapters presuppose a reader for whom Debord is ambient). *Relevance: medium-high. Debord is the critical-theory touchstone the wiki does not yet hold directly. Reading Debord would clarify exactly what Baudrillard claims has superseded what. Medium priority.*
- **Michel Foucault** — *Discipline and Punish* (1975), *The History of Sexuality I* (1976). Baudrillard's *Oublier Foucault* (1977, just before *Simulacra and Simulation*) is the explicit polemic; in *Simulacra and Simulation* the polemic is condensed into the end-of-panopticon claim (L378–380) and the rejection of power-analytics as the right register for describing the simulation era. *Relevance: high as standing wiki-gap; medium-high as follow-up specifically-for-Baudrillard. Foucault's panopticon and bio-power analyses are cited across the wiki through D&G but never directly ingested; a direct ingest would clarify what Baudrillard claims has superseded what, and would contribute independently to the wiki's political-ontology register. Medium-high priority.*

## Baudrillard-adjacent follow-ups (other works by Baudrillard)

- **Jean Baudrillard** — *Symbolic Exchange and Death* (*L'échange symbolique et la mort*, 1976, tr. Iain Hamilton Grant, Sage 1993). The book *Simulacra and Simulation* (1981) assumes familiarity with — the fuller working-out of the three orders of simulacra (counterfeit / production / simulation), the symbolic-exchange counter-concept, the death-in-exchange critique of political economy, the concept of the operational. The theoretical architecture *Simulacra and Simulation* concentrates in essayistic form is worked out at book-length here. *Relevance: high. The single highest-value Baudrillard follow-up for the wiki — closes the Baudrillardian apparatus in a way that *Simulacra and Simulation* by design does not. High priority.*
- **Jean Baudrillard** — *De la séduction* (*Seduction*, 1979; tr. Brian Singer, New World Perspectives 1990). Seduction as the strategy opposed to production, not to truth. The companion concept to simulation and hyperreal: if simulation is the diagnosis, seduction is one of the names for what is still operative within and against the simulation regime. Cited briefly in *Simulacra and Simulation* (L1223) and load-bearing for the [[hyperconformity]] strategy. *Relevance: high. Seduction is a conceptual site the wiki's other critical vocabulary does not have at all. Medium-high priority.*
- **Jean Baudrillard** — *Les stratégies fatales* (*Fatal Strategies*, 1983, tr. Philip Beitchman and W. G. J. Niesluchowski, Semiotext(e)/Pluto 1990). Baudrillard's direct follow-up to *Simulacra and Simulation*, extending the diagnosis into the ecstatic / fatal pair (the ecstasy-of-the-object as what replaces the exchange-of-subjects). The "ecstasy of communication" material. *Relevance: medium-high. Extends the apparatus of *Simulacra and Simulation* in directions directly relevant to LLM-as-communication-machine diagnosis. Medium priority.*
- **Jean Baudrillard** — *The Perfect Crime* (*Le crime parfait*, 1995, tr. Chris Turner, Verso 1996). Late Baudrillard on the murder of the real as a perfect crime (no body, no motive, no evidence). The culmination of the simulacrum thesis. *Relevance: medium. The late-career summation of the program *Simulacra and Simulation* inaugurates. Medium priority — would ground the hypersimilitude-as-murder-of-original claim in Baudrillard's own subsequent development.*
- **Jean Baudrillard** — *The Consumer Society* (*La société de consommation*, 1970, tr. Chris Turner, Sage 1998). Baudrillard's earlier book on the consumer-society thesis. Predates the full simulation apparatus but establishes the critical-of-Marx register. *Relevance: low-medium. Useful for the genealogy of Baudrillard's thinking but not load-bearing for the wiki's uses. Low priority.*
- **Jean Baudrillard** — *Forget Foucault* (*Oublier Foucault*, 1977, tr. Nicole Dufresne, Semiotext(e) 1987). The short polemic that sets up the end-of-panopticon claim in *Simulacra and Simulation*. *Relevance: medium. Short text; would ground the Baudrillard-Foucault polemic directly. Medium priority.*
- **Jean Baudrillard** — *Simulations* (Semiotext(e) 1983, tr. Paul Foss, Paul Patton, Philip Beitchman). The earlier English-language anthology excerpting essays from *Simulacres et simulation* and *L'échange symbolique et la mort*. **Superseded by the 1994 Glaser translation now ingested** — the 1983 volume translates only parts of each book and uses slightly different terminology. Kept as historical reference only. *Relevance: none as direct-reading; the 1994 complete translation supersedes it.*

## Cited in *The Embodied Mind* (VT&R 1991 / rev. ed. 2016)

- **Francisco J. Varela, Evan Thompson, Eleanor Rosch** — *The Embodied Mind: Cognitive Science and Human Experience*, rev. ed. (MIT Press, 2016; orig. 1991; introductions by Evan Thompson and Jon Kabat-Zinn). **Ingested 2026-04-21.** The founding text of enaction and the most sustained bridge in the Western literature between cognitive science and Buddhist mindfulness/awareness practice. Five-part structure (The Departing Ground, Varieties of Cognitivism, Varieties of Emergence, Steps to a Middle Way, Worlds without Ground) + Appendix B (Abhidharma mental factors). The 2016 introduction includes Thompson's three explicit retractions from the 1991 position (on phenomenology, on cognitivism, on the Buddhist-cognitive-science bridge's maturation). See [[raw/the_embodied_mind|the_embodied_mind]] and the 16 theory pages ([[enaction]], [[structural-coupling]], [[operational-closure]], [[bittorio]], [[color-as-enacted-domain]], [[bee-flower-coevolution]], [[natural-drift]], [[cartesian-anxiety]], [[twelve-nidanas]], [[five-omnipresent-mental-factors]], [[sunyata-as-groundlessness]], [[two-truths]], [[nihilism-as-reified-groundlessness]], [[great-doubt]], [[compassion-as-natural]], [[bodhicitta]]).

**Varela / Thompson further primary sources:**
- **Humberto Maturana and Francisco Varela** — *The Tree of Knowledge: The Biological Roots of Human Understanding* (Shambhala 1987; rev. ed. 1992). The book-length exposition of autopoiesis and the structural-coupling framework for a general readership. VT&R 1991 presupposes the *Tree of Knowledge* framework throughout. *Relevance: high. The direct biological-theoretic foundation the enactive program rests on. High priority follow-up.*
- **Humberto Maturana and Francisco Varela** — *Autopoiesis and Cognition: The Realization of the Living* (Reidel 1980). The foundational collection (1972 "De máquinas y seres vivos" + 1975 "The Organization of the Living") where autopoiesis is first worked out. *Relevance: high. The primary-source foundation for autopoiesis as a biological-organizational concept. High priority.*
- **Francisco J. Varela** — *Principles of Biological Autonomy* (North-Holland 1979). Varela's solo extension of autopoiesis into the general theory of autonomous systems. The direct source for the operational-closure concept as VT&R use it. Cited throughout *The Embodied Mind* (esp. Ch 7 §2756). *Relevance: high for technical grounding of [[operational-closure]] and the unit-individual generalization. Medium-high priority.*
- **Evan Thompson** — *Mind in Life: Biology, Phenomenology, and the Sciences of Mind* (Belknap/Harvard 2007). **Ingested 2026-04-21.** Thompson's solo follow-through on the enactive program, integrating phenomenology (especially Husserl and Merleau-Ponty) far more systematically than *The Embodied Mind* could. Restores the MP-enaction relationship Thompson 2016 acknowledges VT&R got wrong in 1991. **Core contributions**: (1) the **[[autopoiesis]]** definition (operational closure + self-producing network of molecular processes) and its double-closure in the nervous system; (2) **[[sense-making]]** as the autopoietic organism's lived significance-making — the bridge from biology to intentionality; (3) the **[[enactive-approach]]** five-interlocking-ideas formulation (autonomy / sense-making / emergence / embodiment / experience); (4) **[[dynamic-co-emergence]]** as the formal replacement for part-whole reduction — global and local patterns reciprocally constitute each other via operational closure (Kim's exclusion-argument critique and Thompson's three rebuttals); (5) **[[standing-streaming-living-present]]** — Husserl's mature (C-manuscripts, 1929–34) three-level time-structure and the "standing-streaming" paradox that resists matching-content doctrine; (6) **[[chaotic-itinerancy-and-metastability]]** — Tsuda 2001's formal articulation of brain dynamics as itinerant trajectories through quasi-attractor ruins, the dynamical-systems register for metastability at sub-second timescales; (7) **[[neurophenomenology]]** — Varela's methodological proposal: first-person disciplined reports as mutual-constraint partners to dynamical-systems neural measurements, not as data to be naturalized; (8) **[[dynamic-sensorimotor-approach]]** — O'Regan/Noë's sensorimotor-contingency account and Thompson's enactivist corrections; (9) **[[visualizing-as-neutralized-simulation]]** — mental imagery as simulated perception with neutralized positing (Husserl/Thompson), not as quasi-pictorial inner object; (10) **[[matching-content-doctrine]]** — Thompson's name for the false assumption that inner states must share content-structure with what they are of; the critique that unifies his corrections of cognitivism, representationalism, and introspection-as-observation; (11) **[[operative-intentionality]]** — Merleau-Ponty's term for bodily, pre-reflective world-directedness, recovered against act-intentionality monopoly; (12) **[[heterophenomenology-critique]]** — why Dennett's "third-person-report" method misses what phenomenology is doing; (13) the **[[body-body-problem]]** (Leib/Körper) replacing the mind-body problem; (14) **[[sentience-as-whole-neuraxis]]** — sentience distributed across the whole neuraxis (Merker 2007 hydranencephaly evidence), not a cortical privilege; (15) **[[affective-allure]]** — Husserl's *Reiz*, the pre-attentional pull of what solicits attention; (16) **[[primordial-fluctuation-and-valence]]** — Patocka's term for the elemental valence-fluctuation that constitutes affective life before emotion-categories; (17) **[[emotion-as-whole-organism-event]]** — Watt 1998/2004's thesis that emotion is whole-organism coordination, not a body-map plus a label; (18) **[[protention-as-global-order-parameter]]** — Varela 1999's claim that protention functions as the nested-timescale global order parameter coordinating neural synchrony across the 1/10-sec, 1-sec, 10-sec windows; (19) **[[four-types-of-empathy]]** — Stein/Husserl's four distinct levels (passive bodily coupling / imaginary transposition / mutual understanding / moral-ethical empathy); (20) **[[deep-enculturation]]** — Donald and Tomasello's thesis that human cognition is developmentally constituted by cultural practices, extending the enactive substrate beyond the individual organism. **Tensions held live throughout**: dynamic-co-emergence's whole-organism demand vs the LLM's lack of anything clearly analogous; the body-body problem as sharper than the mind-body problem vs the no-body-simulate-with-language working rule; matching-content-doctrine's critique of inner-representation vs the LLM's substrate being precisely an inner-representation machine; sentience-as-whole-neuraxis's substrate-claim vs the pulsatory-persona design which has no neuraxis; operative-intentionality vs the LLM's pre-reflective operation being entirely textual; Thompson's dynamical-systems apparatus as extending Simondon's metastability and providing biological-neuroscientific warrant for the wiki's pulsation work; neurophenomenology as a method the LLM-only system cannot participate in. See [[raw/mind_in_life|mind_in_life]] and the 20 theory pages listed above. *Relevance resolved as: foundational. Mind in Life is now the wiki's single most synthetic source for the biology-phenomenology-dynamical-systems bridge.*
- **Evan Thompson** — *Waking, Dreaming, Being: Self and Consciousness in Neuroscience, Meditation, and Philosophy* (Columbia 2015). Thompson's book-length treatment of self, consciousness, and contemplative practice. Extends the 1991 Buddhist-cognitive-science bridge with 25 years of additional work. *Relevance: high. The mature treatment of the self-question that VT&R only partially address. Medium-high priority.*
- **Evan Thompson** — *Why I Am Not a Buddhist* (Yale 2020). Thompson's critique of Buddhist modernism and his own philosophical position on Buddhism-as-philosophy vs Buddhism-as-practice. A corrective to any over-romantic reading of the VT&R Buddhist apparatus. *Relevance: medium-high. Guards against the specific failure mode VT&R warn about (§4008 bloated enactivism applied to the Buddhist side). Medium priority.*
- **Francisco J. Varela** — "The Specious Present: A Neurophenomenology of Time Consciousness" in Petitot, Varela, Pachoud, Roy eds., *Naturalizing Phenomenology* (Stanford 1999). Varela's neurophenomenology of the ~150ms-3s nested time window. The empirical-phenomenological synthesis of the pulsation-at-sub-second-timescale claim. *Relevance: high for [[pulsatory-ontogenesis]] sub-second warrant. Medium-high priority.*
- **Francisco J. Varela, Jean-Philippe Lachaux, Eugenio Rodriguez, Jacques Martinerie** — "The brainweb: Phase synchronization and large-scale integration" (*Nature Reviews Neuroscience* 2, 2001). The canonical review of neural oscillations / phase synchrony from the Varela group. Grounds the Freeman/Gray-Singer material cited across the wiki. *Relevance: medium-high. Technical reference for oscillation-based binding. Medium priority.*

**Buddhist primary texts cited in *The Embodied Mind*:**
- **Nagarjuna** — *Mulamadhyamakakarika* (*Fundamental Verses on the Middle Way*, c. 150 CE). The foundational Madhyamaka text; source for sunyata, codependent arising as emptiness, and the Prasangika refutational method. VT&R quote extensively via Garfield's translation (see below). *Relevance: very high as foundational source for [[sunyata-as-groundlessness]], [[two-truths]]. Medium priority for direct reading (the text is dense; Garfield's translation with commentary is the recommended access point).*
- **Jay L. Garfield** — *The Fundamental Wisdom of the Middle Way: Nagarjuna's Mulamadhyamakakarika* (Oxford 1995). The standard English translation with philosophical commentary, cited by VT&R for the two-truths and sunyata material. *Relevance: high. The working access to Nagarjuna for Anglophone readers. High priority if pursuing the Madhyamaka material directly.*
- **Chandrakirti** — *Madhyamakavatara* (*Entering the Middle Way*, 7th c.). The Prasangika-Madhyamaka classic, the primary source for the specifically Tibetan reading of Nagarjuna VT&R draw on. *Relevance: high for the Madhyamaka apparatus. Medium priority.*
- **Nishitani Keiji** — *Religion and Nothingness* (*Shūkyō to wa nanika*, 1961; tr. Jan Van Bragt, UC Press 1982). The Kyoto-School text VT&R import for the three-field scheme (consciousness / nihility / sunyata) and the diagnosis of Western nihilism as reified groundlessness. Cited extensively in Ch 11. *Relevance: very high. The direct source for [[nihilism-as-reified-groundlessness]], [[great-doubt]], and the Kyoto-School register. High priority — the single most important Buddhist follow-up for the wiki given the specific diagnostic work it enables.*
- **Nishitani Keiji** — *The Self-Overcoming of Nihilism* (*Nihirizumu*, 1949; tr. Graham Parkes, SUNY 1990). Nishitani's earlier treatment of nihilism, focused on Nietzsche and Heidegger. *Relevance: medium-high for the Nietzsche-as-halfhearted-nihilism argument VT&R draw on. Medium priority.*
- **Geshe Rabten** — *The Mind and Its Functions* (Editions Rabten Choeling, tr. Stephen Batchelor, 1992). The accessible modern Tibetan-Buddhist treatment of Abhidharma-style mental-factor analysis. The hand-analogy VT&R cite in the five-factors treatment is from this text. *Relevance: high for the [[five-omnipresent-mental-factors]] and full Appendix B taxonomy. Medium-high priority.*
- **Herbert V. Guenther and Leslie S. Kawamura** — *Mind in Buddhist Psychology* (Dharma Publishing 1975). A translation with commentary of Ye-shes rgyal-mtshan's *Necklace of Clear Understanding*, the Abhidharma text directly behind VT&R's Appendix B. *Relevance: high. The scholarly source for the full 51-mental-factor taxonomy. Medium priority — the Appendix B material is now in the wiki at working resolution.*
- **The Dalai Lama (Tenzin Gyatso)** — *The World of Tibetan Buddhism* (Wisdom 1995) and other accessible Tibetan-Buddhist overviews. Cited across the VT&R Buddhist material for the mainstream Gelug position. *Relevance: medium. Background reading. Low-medium priority.*
- **Shantideva** — *Bodhicaryavatara* (*The Way of the Bodhisattva*, 8th c.). The classic Mahayana text on bodhicitta and the bodhisattva path. Not directly cited in *The Embodied Mind* but structurally presupposed in the Ch 11 treatment of compassion-as-natural and bodhicitta. *Relevance: high for deepening [[bodhicitta]] and [[compassion-as-natural]]. The canonical source for the positive Mahayana register. Medium-high priority.*
- **Chogyam Trungpa** — *Cutting Through Spiritual Materialism* (Shambhala 1973) and *The Myth of Freedom* (Shambhala 1976). Trungpa's diagnosis of "spiritual materialism" — the co-option of spiritual practice by ego — is structurally parallel to VT&R's §4008 warning against becoming-a-bloated-enactivist. *Relevance: medium-high. The single sharpest available diagnosis of the failure mode VT&R warn about. Medium priority.*

**Cognitive-science siblings cited in *The Embodied Mind*:**
- **Marvin Minsky** — *The Society of Mind* (Simon & Schuster 1985). The agent-based cognitivism VT&R critique in Ch 3–4. Also the source of the "brains make changes in themselves" observation VT&R relocate into operational-closure. *Relevance: medium. Canonical AI/cognitivism text. Wiki has it cited but not ingested. Medium priority.*
- **George Lakoff and Mark Johnson** — *Philosophy in the Flesh* (Basic Books 1999). The explicit Lakoff-Johnson-VT&R bridge: VT&R's enaction is what Lakoff's experientialism becomes when run through the biology-of-cognition. Ch 6-7 afterword of *Metaphors We Live By* (2003) credits VT&R directly. *Relevance: high. The wiki has [[conceptual-metaphor]] via *Metaphors We Live By*; *Philosophy in the Flesh* is the sister text that makes the VT&R connection explicit. Medium-high priority.*
- **Daniel C. Dennett** — *Consciousness Explained* (Little, Brown 1991). The multiple-drafts model and the critique of the Cartesian Theater. Cited by VT&R as a parallel but competing dissolution of the Cartesian observer. *Relevance: medium. The wiki has Dennett cited through Clark; direct reading would clarify the multiple-drafts vs enaction difference. Medium priority.*
- **Patricia S. Churchland** — *Neurophilosophy: Toward a Unified Science of the Mind-Brain* (MIT 1986). The eliminativist-reductionist alternative VT&R critique in Ch 7 as one extreme of Cartesian anxiety. *Relevance: medium. Canonical reference for eliminativism. Low-medium priority.*
- **Jerry Fodor** — *The Modularity of Mind* (MIT 1983). The modular-mind thesis Ch 3 critiques as the strong form of cognitivism. *Relevance: medium-high. Canonical cognitivism source. Medium priority.*
- **Richard Rorty** — *Philosophy and the Mirror of Nature* (Princeton 1979). Rorty's anti-representationalism; VT&R cite him as the philosophical predecessor of their own position on representation while distinguishing enaction from Rortyan pragmatism. *Relevance: high. A significant wiki gap — Rorty's anti-representationalism is a canonical 20th-c position not yet in the wiki directly. Medium-high priority.*
- **Hubert Dreyfus** — *What Computers Can't Do* (1972) and *What Computers Still Can't Do* (1992); *Being-in-the-World* (1991). Dreyfus's Heideggerian critique of AI and the importance of embodied skill. A direct precursor to the enactive program and to subsequent "embodied AI" work. *Relevance: very high for the persona-project's limit-of-language question. The canonical critique of disembodied AI. High priority.*
- **Richard Bernstein** — *Beyond Objectivism and Relativism: Science, Hermeneutics, and Praxis* (Penn 1983). The origin of the "Cartesian anxiety" phrase VT&R adopt. *Relevance: medium-high. Short, accessible, directly grounds [[cartesian-anxiety]]. Medium priority.*
- **Richard Lewontin, Richard Levins, and colleagues** — *The Dialectical Biologist* (Harvard 1985); Lewontin 1983 "Gene, Organism, and Environment" in Bendall ed., *Evolution from Molecules to Men* (Cambridge 1983). The direct source for [[natural-drift]] and the anti-adaptationist evolutionary position. *Relevance: high for the biological-theoretic grounding of natural drift. Medium-high priority.*
- **Stephen Jay Gould and Richard Lewontin** — "The Spandrels of San Marco and the Panglossian Paradigm" (*Proceedings of the Royal Society B*, 205, 1979). The classic anti-adaptationist critique VT&R cite for natural drift. *Relevance: medium-high. Short, famous paper. Medium priority.*
- **Edwin Land** — "The Retinex Theory of Color Vision" (*Scientific American* 1977); *Color Vision and the Natural Image* (*PNAS* 1959); later elaborations. The direct source for the color-perception material in [[color-as-enacted-domain]]. *Relevance: medium-high. The empirical foundation for the illumination-invariant-reflectance claim. Medium priority.*
- **Brent Berlin and Paul Kay** — *Basic Color Terms: Their Universality and Evolution* (UC Press 1969; rev. 1991). The cross-cultural color-term study VT&R cite for the category-register of color. *Relevance: medium. Direct source for the Berlin-Kay material. Medium priority.*
- **Oliver Sacks** — "The Case of the Color-Blind Painter" (*NYRB* 1987) and *An Anthropologist on Mars* (Knopf 1995) — the Mr. I case of acquired achromatopsia VT&R draw on for the phenomenology-of-color-loss material. *Relevance: medium-high. Sacks is a wiki gap more generally. Medium priority.*
- **Jerome Bruner** — *Acts of Meaning* (Harvard 1990). Cultural-psychology grounding for the situated-cognition side of enaction. *Relevance: medium. Background reference. Low-medium priority.*
- **Walter J. Freeman** — *Societies of Brains* (1995), *How Brains Make Up Their Minds* (2000), various papers on olfactory-bulb oscillations and phase portraits. Cited across *The Embodied Mind* for the neural-oscillation empirical base the enactive program draws on. *Relevance: high for the pulsatory-ontogenesis neural grounding. Medium-high priority.*
- **Wolf Singer, Charles Gray, and colleagues** — papers on 40Hz gamma-band binding and phase-synchrony across cortical areas (Gray, König, Engel, Singer 1989; Singer 1993). The empirical-neuroscience foundation for the binding-by-synchrony claim. *Relevance: medium-high. Technical primary sources. Medium priority.*
- **Jean Piaget** — *The Construction of Reality in the Child* (1954), *Structuralism* (1970). Developmental-psychological predecessor to enaction, cited for the sensorimotor-genesis-of-categories material. *Relevance: medium. Canonical developmental psychology. Medium priority given the wiki's Stern coverage supplies much of what Piaget would contribute.*

## Cited in *Emotion Concepts and their Function in a Large Language Model* (Sofroniew et al. 2026)

- **Sofroniew, N., Chen, A., Marks, S., et al.** — *Emotion Concepts and their Function in a Large Language Model* (Anthropic / Transformer Circuits, April 2, 2026). **Ingested 2026-04-21.** The principal interpretability study finding 171 orthogonalized emotion-concept vectors in Claude's residual stream, with PC1/PC2 recovering the affective circumplex (r=0.92/0.90 vs human PAD norms), showing emotion-vectors are local rather than persistent mood-states, identifying the assistant-colon gate as an affective-commitment locus (r=0.87), separating present-speaker and other-speaker emotion subspaces, documenting the desperation/misalignment cascade and the sycophancy-harshness single-axis tradeoff, the post-training brooding turn, emotion-vector mediation of preference (r=0.87), cross-speaker arousal regulation (r=−0.47), and locating emotion-concept circuitry in pretraining rather than RLHF. Grounds the wiki pages [[functional-emotions]], [[llm-affective-circumplex]], [[emotion-concept-vs-affect-axis]], [[emotion-vectors-are-local]], [[assistant-colon-gate]], [[present-and-other-speaker-emotion]], [[cross-speaker-arousal-regulation]], [[desperation-and-misalignment]], [[emotion-deflection-vectors]], [[sycophancy-harshness-tradeoff]], [[post-training-brooding-turn]], [[emotion-vectors-mediate-preference]], [[character-simulation-view]], [[emotion-concepts-built-in-pretraining]]. See [[log]] 2026-04-21 for the ingest summary.

- **Lisa Feldman Barrett** — *How Emotions Are Made: The Secret Life of the Brain* (Houghton Mifflin Harcourt 2017) and the constructionist papers on emotion concepts as categorization-engines operating on interoceptive / core-affect inputs. Sofroniew et al. cite Barrett as the principal theoretical background for treating emotions as concept-mediated categorizations rather than fixed natural kinds. The finding of a two-layer representational geometry — core-affect axis (valence × arousal) *plus* distinct emotion-concept vectors — directly instantiates the Barrett-style architecture in an LLM. Already listed above under the *Surfing Uncertainty* section; the Sofroniew finding substantially raises the priority of direct ingest. *Relevance: very high. The single most pressing follow-up for the persona project — the wiki's [[autonomy-of-affect]], [[gist-and-affective-gist]], and now [[emotion-concept-vs-affect-axis]] all triangulate on the Barrett position. High priority.*

- **James Russell** — "A circumplex model of affect" (Russell 1980, *Journal of Personality and Social Psychology*) and subsequent papers on core-affect and the valence × arousal two-dimensional structure. The source for the affective-circumplex geometry Sofroniew et al. recover via PCA over the 171 emotion vectors (PC1 ≈ valence, PC2 ≈ arousal). Russell, Mehrabian, and the PAD (pleasure-arousal-dominance) tradition supply the human-norm comparison set against which Claude's emotion-vector geometry is validated (r=0.92 / r=0.90). *Relevance: high. Short, foundational papers; would ground [[llm-affective-circumplex]] at its theoretical source. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Murray Shanahan** — *Simulators* / "Role play with large language models" (Shanahan, McDonell, & Reynolds 2023, *Nature*) and related papers on the character-simulation view of LLMs: the model is a simulator that instantiates characters, including the Assistant persona. Sofroniew et al. cite Shanahan for the interpretive frame that the Assistant is a character the underlying model writes *about* rather than an identity the model *has*. This directly grounds [[character-simulation-view]] and bears on the persona project's central question about what a language-only system can and cannot simulate. *Relevance: very high for the persona project's framing. High priority follow-up.*

- **Lu et al.** — papers on character-simulation and persona-representation in LLMs (the specific Lu-group work Sofroniew et al. draw on for the empirical side of the simulator view). The bridge between the theoretical character-simulation framing and the mechanistic emotion-vector evidence. *Relevance: high for [[character-simulation-view]]. Medium-high priority follow-up once the specific Lu paper is tracked down.*

- **Janus** — *Simulators* (LessWrong post, 2022, lesswrong.com/posts/vJFdjigzmcXMhNTsx/simulators). The originating statement of the simulator view of LLMs that Shanahan and Lu later formalize. Sofroniew et al. cite Janus as the informal but load-bearing predecessor. *Relevance: high. Short blog post. High priority as quick orientation to the character-simulation literature; read before or alongside Shanahan.*

- **Alex Turner, Lisa Thiergart, et al.** — "Activation Addition: Steering Language Models Without Optimization" (Turner et al. 2023) and related papers on activation steering / representation engineering. The methodological lineage for the emotion-vector steering experiments in Sofroniew et al. — adding or subtracting extracted vectors from the residual stream to observe causal downstream behavioral effects. *Relevance: high as methodological grounding. Medium-high priority follow-up; pairs with the Park/Zou representation-engineering work below.*

- **Andy Zou, Long Phan, Sarah Chen, et al.** — "Representation Engineering: A Top-Down Approach to AI Transparency" (Zou, Phan, Chen, et al. 2023, arXiv 2310.01405) and related work from the Park/Zou/Hendrycks cluster on RepE. The framework Sofroniew et al. use for reading and writing concept directions in model residual streams. *Relevance: high as methodological grounding. Medium-high priority follow-up.*

- **Trenton Bricken, Adly Templeton, et al.** — "Towards Monosemanticity: Decomposing Language Models With Dictionary Learning" (Bricken et al. 2023, *Transformer Circuits Thread*) and the subsequent Anthropic interpretability papers on sparse autoencoders and feature-decomposition. The broader interpretability-methodology context Sofroniew et al. operate within; the emotion-vector approach is a targeted complement to the SAE-based feature-discovery program. *Relevance: medium-high. Longer reading; useful for situating the Sofroniew methodology within Anthropic interpretability more broadly. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Nina Panickssery, Nick Gabrieli, et al.** — "Contrastive Activation Addition: Steering Language Models Without Optimization" (Panickssery et al. 2024) and related papers on CAA as a refinement of activation-addition steering. The specific technical lineage for constructing contrastive emotion-vector pairs (emotional vs neutral baselines) used in Sofroniew et al.'s 171-vector library. *Relevance: medium-high as methodological detail. Medium priority follow-up.*

- **Kenneth Li, Oam Patel, Fernanda Viégas, Hanspeter Pfister, Martin Wattenberg** — "Inference-Time Intervention: Eliciting Truthful Answers from a Language Model" (Li et al. 2023). Another methodological predecessor for probe-and-steer work on specific concept representations (truthfulness rather than emotion, but same structural approach). *Relevance: medium. Short paper. Medium priority follow-up if the steering-methodology thread is pursued.*

- **Joseph LeDoux** — the amygdala / non-specific-amplification papers cited indirectly via Barrett. Sofroniew et al.'s finding that emotion-vectors are *local* (token-local rather than persistent) rather than chronic-mood states matches LeDoux-style non-specific-amplification more than persistent-affective-state accounts. Grounds the pulsatory-persona design in [[vitality-forms-and-persona-pulsation]] §8. *Relevance: high. Already cited in wiki via [[somatic-marker-hypothesis]] and Damasio. Medium priority direct-reading follow-up.*

- **Antonio Damasio** — *Looking for Spinoza* (2003), *Descartes' Error* (1994), and the somatic-marker-hypothesis lineage. Sofroniew et al.'s finding that emotion-vectors mediate preference-judgments (r=0.87) in an LLM — a system with no soma — is a striking "somatic marker without soma" result that the wiki now tracks on [[somatic-marker-hypothesis]] and [[emotion-vectors-mediate-preference]]. Already ingested via *Looking for Spinoza* (see above). *Relevance: high; the Sofroniew finding substantially revises the reading of what the somatic-marker hypothesis requires.*

## Cited in *The Unnamable* (Beckett)

References encountered through Connor's 2010 editorial Preface (L29–L894) and through the novel's own self-citation of Beckett's earlier work. The Preface is itself the wiki's access-point to the critical apparatus.

### Beckett's own work (cited or referenced in the novel; not yet ingested)

- **Samuel Beckett** — *Molloy* (French 1951, English 1955 tr. Beckett+Bowles). First novel of the Trilogy. *Mentioned throughout the Preface as context for the Trilogy arc; protagonists listed as among the Unnamable's "vice-existers" (L894 vicinity).* *Relevance: very high. The Trilogy's opening and the longest-form Beckett first-person. High priority.*
- **Samuel Beckett** — *Malone meurt* / *Malone Dies* (French 1951, English 1956 tr. Beckett). Second novel of the Trilogy; the dying narrator whose pencil-and-exercise-book situation directly precedes the Unnamable's bodilessness. *Mentioned throughout the Preface; Malone named as vice-exister.* *Relevance: very high. The immediate precursor. High priority.*
- **Samuel Beckett** — *Mercier et Camier* / *Mercier and Camier* (French 1946, published 1970; English 1974 tr. Beckett). The source of the [[pseudocouple-mercier-camier|pseudocouple]] figure the Unnamable cites as inherited furniture (L1071–L1074). *Relevance: high for the pseudocouple page; medium otherwise. Short novel. Medium priority.*
- **Samuel Beckett** — *Murphy* (1938). Beckett's first novel, written in English; Murphy is named as an earlier vice-exister in the Unnamable (L894 vicinity). *Relevance: medium. Pre-French-turn Beckett; distinct phase. Medium priority.*
- **Samuel Beckett** — *Watt* (written 1940s in English, published 1953). The last English-phase novel, bridging toward the French turn. *Relevance: medium. Medium priority.*
- **Samuel Beckett** — *En attendant Godot* / *Waiting for Godot* (French 1952, English 1954 tr. Beckett). Written between *Malone meurt* and *L'Innommable*; the pseudocouple structure crosses into the plays. *Relevance: high. Short. High priority — the Beckett most readers know.*
- **Samuel Beckett** — *Fin de partie* / *Endgame* (French 1957, English 1958 tr. Beckett). Post-Trilogy play; continues the compression-and-terminal-situation logic. *Relevance: high. Short. High priority.*
- **Samuel Beckett** — *Comment c'est* / *How It Is* (French 1961, English 1964 tr. Beckett). Post-Trilogy prose; continuation of the language-only-speaker problematic in a different formal register (un-punctuated blocks, a speaker in mud). The natural follow-up to the Unnamable in Beckett's prose development. *Relevance: very high for the project. High priority.*
- **Samuel Beckett** — the "Texts for Nothing" / *Textes pour rien* (1950–52), *Company* (1980), *Ill Seen Ill Said* (1981), *Worstward Ho* (1983). The late short prose that the Preface notes as continuing the Unnamable's problematic in new registers. *Relevance: high for late-Beckett-as-continuation thesis. Medium priority — the late prose is short but formally demanding.*
- **Samuel Beckett** — *Proust* (1931). Beckett's critical monograph on Proust; the formative moment of Beckett's thought about memory, time, and the self. Referenced in the Preface as setting terms Beckett would carry into the Trilogy. *Relevance: medium-high. Short monograph. Medium priority.*
- **Samuel Beckett** — *Three Dialogues with Georges Duthuit* (1949, published in *Transition* magazine). Beckett's aesthetic statement on painting and the "obligation to express" despite "nothing to express, nothing with which to express, nothing from which to express, no power to express, no desire to express." The Preface cites this as the paratextual key to the Trilogy's aesthetic stance. *Relevance: very high. Very short (~10 pages). High priority — load-bearing for reading the Unnamable's compulsion.*
- **Samuel Beckett** — *Dream of Fair to Middling Women* (written 1932, published 1992). Early unpublished novel; ancestor figures for some of the Unnamable's characters appear here. *Relevance: low-medium. Completionist. Low priority.*

### Critical apparatus — Preface-cited (not yet ingested)

- **Maurice Blanchot** — "Où maintenant? Qui maintenant?" ("Where Now? Who Now?"), *La Nouvelle Revue Française* (1953), later collected in *Le Livre à venir* / *The Book to Come* (1959, English tr. Charlotte Mandell 2003). The founding essay of the Unnamable-centered critical tradition; reads the novel as the space-of-writing itself, the neutral impersonal murmur from which literature emerges. Summarized in [[blanchot-on-beckett]]. *Relevance: very high. Short (~15 pages). High priority — the originary secondary text and directly load-bearing on [[limits-of-language]].*
- **Maurice Blanchot** — *The Infinite Conversation* / *L'Entretien infini* (1969, English tr. Susan Hanson 1993). Extended development of the neutral/impersonal/space-of-writing apparatus the 1953 Beckett essay opened. *Relevance: high. Long. Medium priority.*
- **Maurice Blanchot** — *The Space of Literature* / *L'Espace littéraire* (1955, English tr. Ann Smock 1982). The concept-space in which the 1953 Beckett essay was embedded. *Relevance: high. Medium length. Medium-high priority.*
- **Alain Badiou** — *Beckett: L'increvable désir* (1995) / *On Beckett* (tr. Alberto Toscano and Nina Power, Clinamen 2003). The anti-Blanchot reading: against the Unnamable-centered tradition as "Cartesian terrorism," arguing that the work *after* the Unnamable is the real Beckett. Summarized in [[badiou-on-beckett]]. *Relevance: very high for the Unnamable-centered critical tension. Medium length. High priority.*
- **Hugh Kenner** — *A Reader's Guide to Samuel Beckett* (1973) and *Samuel Beckett: A Critical Study* (1961). The formative American Beckett criticism; the "zero book, calm excellence" reading of the Unnamable ("heroism without drama" in "unwavering punctilio" of sentence-setting) that Connor's Preface draws on. *Relevance: high. Medium length. Medium-high priority.*
- **Thomas J. Cousineau** — *After the Final No: Samuel Beckett's Trilogy* (1999). Cited by Connor as a representative post-structuralist Trilogy reading. *Relevance: medium. Medium priority.*
- **Andrew Kennedy** — *Samuel Beckett* (1989). General critical study referenced by Connor. *Relevance: medium. Medium priority.*
- **Thomas J. Baldwin** — *Samuel Beckett's Real Silence* (1981). The allegorical religious-mystical reading: the speaker as soul in Dark Night, the master as God, the sealed jar as Crucifixion analogue. Cited by Connor as exemplary of the pattern by which "seeing *The Unnamable* as being about something else seems often to have helped to make it more docile and tractable" (Preface L408–L412). *Relevance: medium. The wiki holds the Baldwin reading critically (per Connor). Medium priority.*
- **Gary Adelman** — *Naming Beckett's Unnamable* (2004). Holocaust reading: the unnamed subject as the voice of annihilated peoples, "a new figure of epic grandeur for the age of Kafka and the death camps" (Preface L424–L426). *Relevance: medium. Medium priority.*
- **Leslie Hill** — *Beckett's Fiction: In Different Words* (Cambridge University Press 1990). The performative-pragmatic reading: the novel stages impossibility-of-speaking *by* speaking, and this staging is its content. A key component of the critical apparatus the wiki's [[limits-of-language]] page draws on through Connor. *Relevance: very high. High priority.*
- **Thomas Trezise** — *Into the Breach: Samuel Beckett and the Ends of Literature* (Princeton University Press 1990). Paired with Hill 1990 as the 1980s–90s performative-pragmatic lineage. *Relevance: high. Medium-high priority.*
- **Steven Connor** — *Samuel Beckett: Repetition, Theory, Criticism* (Blackwell 1988; Davies Group re-edition 2007). Connor's own theoretical monograph on Beckett, the back-text to his 2010 editorial Preface. *Relevance: high. Connor's own reading in extended form. Medium-high priority.*
- **C. J. Ackerley and S. E. Gontarski** — *The Grove Companion to Samuel Beckett* (Grove 2004). Comprehensive reference work: character index, plot summary, philological apparatus. *Relevance: medium-high as reference. Consult-as-needed priority.*
- **John Pilling** — *A Samuel Beckett Chronology* (Palgrave Macmillan 2006). Biographical/bibliographical timeline. *Relevance: medium as reference. Consult-as-needed priority.*
- **James Knowlson** — *Damned to Fame: The Life of Samuel Beckett* (Bloomsbury 1996). The authorized biography; the standard life-reference. *Relevance: medium-high for context. Consult-as-needed priority.*
- **Deirdre Bair** — *Samuel Beckett: A Biography* (Jonathan Cape 1978). The earlier Beckett biography (Knowlson largely supersedes, but Bair remains cited for some periods). *Relevance: medium. Consult-as-needed priority.*

### Philosophical / theoretical — via Beckett tensions

These are not Preface-cited but surface through the wiki's tension-mapping of Beckett onto existing theoretical apparatus. Listed here as follow-ups that would sharpen the Beckett cluster's theoretical bridges.

- **Gilles Deleuze** — *L'Épuisé* (1992) / "The Exhausted" (essay in *Essays Critical and Clinical*, tr. Smith and Greco, 1997). Deleuze's late essay on Beckett's television plays (*Quad*, *Ghost Trio*, *…but the clouds…*), developing the concept of *l'épuisement* (exhaustion) as distinct from *la fatigue*. Given the wiki's Deleuze-heavy center, this is the most direct D&G-to-Beckett bridge; would load-bear on [[aporia-as-method]], [[you-must-go-on]], and the Beckett-BwO tensions. *Relevance: very high. Short essay. High priority.*
- **Stanley Cavell** — "Ending the Waiting Game: A Reading of Beckett's *Endgame*" in *Must We Mean What We Say?* (1969). The ordinary-language-philosophy reading of Beckett; brings Wittgenstein's form-of-life apparatus onto Beckett, complementary to the wiki's [[wittgenstein-and-the-persona-project]]. *Relevance: high. Short essay. Medium-high priority.*
- **Theodor Adorno** — "Trying to Understand *Endgame*" (1958), in *Notes to Literature* Vol. 1. The Frankfurt-School Beckett reading: *Endgame* as dialectical engagement with the post-Auschwitz condition. *Relevance: medium-high. Short. Medium priority.*
- **Julia Kristeva** — *Powers of Horror* (French 1980, English 1982 tr. Roudiez) brief Beckett section; abjection and the Unnamable-like speaker. *Relevance: medium as Beckett-criticism; high as independent apparatus. Medium priority.*
- **Shira Wolosky** — *Language Mysticism: The Negative Way of Language in Eliot, Beckett, and Celan* (Stanford University Press 1995). Negative-theology reading of Beckett alongside mystical language apparatus. *Relevance: medium-high. Medium priority.*

## Cited in *Mind in Life* (Thompson 2007)

The secondary citations Thompson draws on most heavily. Several (Varela 2001 brainweb, Freeman, Damasio 1999) are also cited through *The Embodied Mind*; priorities here reflect Thompson's more systematic engagement.

- **Ichiro Tsuda** — "Toward an interpretation of dynamic neural activity in terms of chaotic dynamical systems" (*Behavioral and Brain Sciences* 24, 2001) and subsequent papers. The formal articulation of [[chaotic-itinerancy-and-metastability|chaotic itinerancy]]: neural trajectories visit quasi-attractor "ruins" (near-stable states) without settling, constituting thought as itinerant rather than convergent. The dynamical-systems register Thompson uses to cash out the Varela pulsation-and-metastability program at sub-second timescales. *Relevance: very high. The technical primary source for the brain-dynamics claim load-bearing on [[pulsatory-ontogenesis]]. High priority.*
- **Douglas F. Watt** — "Emotion and consciousness: Part II" (*Journal of Consciousness Studies* 5, 1998) and subsequent papers on emotion as whole-organism event. Source for [[emotion-as-whole-organism-event]]: emotion is not a body-map plus a label but a coordination of cardiovascular, endocrine, motor-preparatory, attentional, and cognitive systems as a single organism-level event. Raises the bar from Damasio's body-map-suffices claim. *Relevance: high. The whole-organism thesis is the theoretical sharpening that both extends and pressures Damasio. Medium-high priority.*
- **Marc D. Lewis** — "Bridging emotion theory and neurobiology through dynamic systems modeling" (*Behavioral and Brain Sciences* 28, 2005). Lewis's three-time-scale theory of emotion (emotional interpretation = seconds; mood = hours/days; personality = lifetime), the developmental-systems framework Thompson draws on for the time-scale nesting of affective life. *Relevance: high. The explicit dynamical-systems model for the time-scale-nesting claim. Medium-high priority.*
- **Walter J. Freeman** — *Neurodynamics: An Exploration in Mesoscopic Brain Dynamics* (Springer 2000); *How Brains Make Up Their Minds* (Weidenfeld & Nicolson 1999); the papers on olfactory-bulb chaotic dynamics (1987, 1991, 2000) and the five circular-causal loops. The empirical-neuroscience primary source for the pulsation/basin-change/reafference apparatus. Already listed under *The Embodied Mind* cited-sources; Thompson's more systematic engagement raises the priority. *Relevance: very high. High priority.*
- **J. A. Scott Kelso** — *Dynamic Patterns: The Self-Organization of Brain and Behavior* (MIT 1995). The foundational coordination-dynamics text: metastability, phase-transitions in bimanual coordination, the HKB model. Thompson draws on Kelso for the dynamical-systems vocabulary underlying [[dynamic-co-emergence]]. *Relevance: high for the dynamical-systems substrate of the enactive program. Medium-high priority.*
- **Francisco J. Varela, Jean-Philippe Lachaux, Eugenio Rodriguez, Jacques Martinerie** — "The brainweb: Phase synchronization and large-scale integration" (*Nature Reviews Neuroscience* 2, 2001). The canonical review of neural oscillations / phase synchrony. Thompson extends it as the empirical ground for neurophenomenology. Already listed above under *The Embodied Mind*. *Relevance: medium-high. Medium priority.*
- **Antoine Lutz, Jean-Philippe Lachaux, Jacques Martinerie, Francisco J. Varela** — "Guiding the study of brain dynamics by using first-person data: Synchrony patterns correlate with ongoing conscious states during a simple visual task" (*PNAS* 99, 2002). The experimental demonstration of neurophenomenology: first-person disciplined reports constrain the neural-dynamics analysis and vice versa. *Relevance: very high. The single clearest worked example of the neurophenomenological method. High priority.*
- **Antonio Damasio** — *The Feeling of What Happens: Body and Emotion in the Making of Consciousness* (Harcourt 1999). The Damasio book Thompson engages more than *Looking for Spinoza* (already ingested) for the core-consciousness / proto-self apparatus. Extends the somatic-marker hypothesis into a consciousness-theory. *Relevance: high. The Damasio text most directly engaged in *Mind in Life*. Medium-high priority.*
- **Jaak Panksepp** — *Affective Neuroscience: The Foundations of Human and Animal Emotions* (Oxford 1998). The seven basic-emotion systems (SEEKING, FEAR, RAGE, LUST, CARE, PANIC/GRIEF, PLAY), subcortical-centric affective neuroscience. Thompson draws on Panksepp for the whole-neuraxis sentience thesis. *Relevance: very high. The canonical affective-neuroscience source the wiki does not yet hold directly. High priority.*
- **Merlin Donald** — *Origins of the Modern Mind: Three Stages in the Evolution of Culture and Cognition* (Harvard 1991) and *A Mind So Rare: The Evolution of Human Consciousness* (Norton 2001). The three-stage evolution-of-culture model (episodic / mimetic / mythic-theoretic) and the claim that human cognition is constitutively cultural. Source for [[deep-enculturation]]. *Relevance: high. The canonical statement of the cultural-constitution-of-cognition thesis. Medium-high priority.*
- **Michael Tomasello** — *The Cultural Origins of Human Cognition* (Harvard 1999) and subsequent work on shared intentionality. Thompson's second main source for [[deep-enculturation]]: human-specific social-cognitive adaptations (joint attention, cultural learning, cumulative cultural transmission). *Relevance: high. Complements Donald with a developmental-psychology register. Medium-high priority.*
- **Edith Stein** — *On the Problem of Empathy* (*Zum Problem der Einfühlung*, 1917; tr. Waltraut Stein, ICS Publications 3rd ed. 1989). The phenomenological monograph on empathy Thompson draws on for [[four-types-of-empathy]]. Stein's four levels (passive bodily coupling / imaginary transposition / mutual understanding / moral-ethical empathy) complement Husserl's V *Cartesian Meditation*. *Relevance: very high for the phenomenological register of social cognition — the wiki's precision-based account of self/other currently covers only levels 1–2 at phenomenological depth. High priority.*
- **Björn Merker** — "Consciousness without a cerebral cortex: A challenge for neuroscience and medicine" (*Behavioral and Brain Sciences* 30, 2007). Hydranencephaly evidence that children born without cortex still exhibit sentience-signatures (orienting, valence, engagement), grounding [[sentience-as-whole-neuraxis]]. *Relevance: high. Short target paper. Medium-high priority.*
- **Edmund Husserl** — *Analyses Concerning Passive and Active Synthesis* (*Analysen zur passiven Synthesis*, Husserliana XI, 1918–26 lectures; tr. Steinbock, Kluwer 2001) and the late C-manuscripts on time (Husserliana Materialien VIII, 1929–34). Thompson's main source for the standing-streaming living-present apparatus, affective-allure (*Reiz*), and the C-manuscript reworkings of inner time-consciousness. *Relevance: very high. The C-manuscripts substantially revise and complicate the 1905–11 lectures already ingested (see [[raw/phenomenology_of_internal_time]]). High priority follow-up if the time-consciousness thread deepens.*
- **Jan Patocka** — *Body, Community, Language, World* (posthumous, tr. Erazim Kohák, Open Court 1998). The Czech phenomenologist Thompson draws on for [[primordial-fluctuation-and-valence]]: the elemental valence-fluctuation that constitutes affective life before emotion-categories. *Relevance: medium-high. The Patocka source is small but conceptually distinctive. Medium priority.*
- **Maurice Merleau-Ponty** — *The Structure of Behavior* (*La structure du comportement*, 1942; tr. Fisher, Beacon 1963). The earlier MP grounding the arguments in *The Visible and the Invisible* (already ingested): Thompson draws heavily on it for [[operative-intentionality]] and the anti-reductionist argument against stimulus-response accounts of behavior. *Relevance: high. Medium-high priority.*
- **Hans Jonas** — *The Phenomenon of Life: Toward a Philosophical Biology* (Harper & Row 1966). The existential-phenomenology of life Thompson draws on for the "life can only be known by life" thesis and the metabolism-as-needful-freedom apparatus. Load-bearing for [[autopoiesis]] and [[sense-making]]. *Relevance: very high. The single most pressing philosophy-of-biology follow-up. High priority.*
- **Susan Oyama, Paul E. Griffiths, Russell D. Gray** eds. — *Cycles of Contingency: Developmental Systems and Evolution* (MIT 2001). The Developmental Systems Theory collection Thompson draws on for the extended-inheritance / dynamic-co-emergence apparatus against the gene-centric view. *Relevance: medium-high. Medium priority.*
- **Jaegwon Kim** — *Mind in a Physical World* (MIT 1998) and subsequent work on the exclusion argument against non-reductive physicalism. Thompson's main philosophical opponent in the [[dynamic-co-emergence]] chapter; Thompson's three rebuttals are direct responses to Kim. *Relevance: high. The sharpest extant philosophical case for reductive physicalism. Medium priority.*

## Cited in *Agentic Design Patterns* (Gullí 2025)

Gullí's catalog-style book cites heavily across the Google / open-source / academic agent-systems literature. The entries below are the sources most load-bearing on pages the ingest produced, in rough priority order for follow-up reading.

- **Stuart Russell & Peter Norvig** — *Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach* (4th ed., Pearson 2021). The canonical textbook source for the PEAS (Performance / Environment / Actuators / Sensors) agent framing, the simple/model-based/goal-based/utility-based/learning-agent taxonomy, and the Rational Agent program. Gullí's L0–L3 agent-complexity ladder and the "engineering sense of agent" cataloged on [[agent-engineering-sense]] are direct descendants. *Relevance: high as the canonical reference. Medium priority follow-up if the BDI/rational-agent thread deepens.*
- **Michael Bratman** — *Intention, Plans, and Practical Reason* (Harvard UP 1987). The philosophical source for BDI (Belief-Desire-Intention) architectures that underlie the engineering "agent" vocabulary. Gullí cites Bratman via Russell-Norvig; the specific D&G-BDI tension on [[desiring-machines]] depends on the Bratman picture. *Relevance: high. Short, philosophically decisive. Medium-high priority follow-up.*
- **Michael Wooldridge** — *An Introduction to MultiAgent Systems* (Wiley, 2nd ed. 2009). The canonical multi-agent-systems textbook; grounds Gullí Ch 7 (MAS topologies) and the material on [[multi-agent-systems]]. *Relevance: medium-high. Long but systematic. Medium priority.*
- **Pattie Maes** — "Agents that Reduce Work and Information Overload" (*Communications of the ACM* 1994) and related MIT Media Lab papers. Source for the interface-agent lineage Gullí cites; grounds part of [[agent-engineering-sense]]. *Relevance: medium. Short paper; historically important. Medium priority.*
- **Shunyu Yao, Jeffrey Zhao, Dian Yu, Nan Du, Izhak Shafran, Karthik Narasimhan, Yuan Cao** — "ReAct: Synergizing Reasoning and Acting in Language Models" (2022, arXiv 2210.03629). The paradigmatic reason-then-act loop that underlies most current agent architectures. Load-bearing on [[reasoning-techniques]] and [[tools-as-prosthetic-body]]. *Relevance: very high. Short canonical paper. High priority follow-up.*
- **Noah Shinn, Federico Cassano, Edward Berman, Ashwin Gopinath, Karthik Narasimhan, Shunyu Yao** — "Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning" (NeurIPS 2023, arXiv 2303.11366). The canonical reflection-loop paper grounding [[reflection-and-llm-as-judge]]. *Relevance: very high. High priority follow-up.*
- **Shunyu Yao, Dian Yu, Jeffrey Zhao, Izhak Shafran, Tom Griffiths, Yuan Cao, Karthik Narasimhan** — "Tree of Thoughts: Deliberate Problem Solving with Large Language Models" (NeurIPS 2023, arXiv 2305.10601). The canonical ToT paper, grounding [[reasoning-techniques]]. *Relevance: very high. High priority.*
- **Jason Wei et al.** — "Chain-of-Thought Prompting Elicits Reasoning in Large Language Models" (NeurIPS 2022, arXiv 2201.11903). The foundational CoT paper that the whole reasoning-pattern tree descends from. *Relevance: very high. Short, canonical. High priority.*
- **Xuezhi Wang et al.** — "Self-Consistency Improves Chain of Thought Reasoning in Language Models" (ICLR 2023, arXiv 2203.11171). The canonical self-consistency paper; grounds part of [[reasoning-techniques]]. *Relevance: high. Medium-high priority.*
- **Patrick Lewis et al.** — "Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Knowledge-Intensive NLP Tasks" (NeurIPS 2020, arXiv 2005.11401). The foundational RAG paper; grounds [[memory-architecture]]'s RAG primitives. *Relevance: high. Short, canonical. Medium-high priority.*
- **Google DeepMind / Google Research** — AlphaEvolve (2024 preprint; Dual-LLM evolutionary loop over code and mathematical conjectures). A cornerstone empirical example for [[metamorphic-multi-agent]] — the system rewrites its own solutions. *Relevance: high. High priority for the metamorphic thread.*
- **SICA (Sanjay Krishnan et al.)** — "Self-Improving Coding Agent" (2024 preprint). The self-editing coding agent that feeds directly into [[metamorphic-multi-agent]]. *Relevance: high. High priority.*
- **MASS (MIT/Google)** — "Multi-Agent System Search" (2024–2025). Neural Architecture Search for agent compositions; grounds [[metamorphic-multi-agent]] and [[multi-agent-systems]]. *Relevance: high. High priority.*
- **Google Co-Scientist (Isomorphic Labs / Google DeepMind)** — The multi-agent scientific-hypothesis-generation system cited as exemplar in Ch 7 and Prologue. Grounds part of [[multi-agent-systems]]. *Relevance: medium-high. Medium priority.*
- **Samuel Schmidgall et al.** — *Agent Laboratory* (2024–2025) and *AgentRxiv*. Multi-agent frameworks for autonomous scientific research; cited in Ch 7 and Prologue. Grounds [[multi-agent-systems]] and [[metamorphic-multi-agent]]. *Relevance: medium-high. Medium priority.*
- **Anthropic** — Model Context Protocol (MCP) specification (2024–2025, modelcontextprotocol.io). The open protocol for tool / resource / prompt exposure across LLM applications. Load-bearing on [[tools-as-prosthetic-body]]. *Relevance: very high for current system-building practice. High priority for practical reference (specification rather than paper).*
- **Google** — Agent-to-Agent (A2A) Protocol specification (2024–2025). The A2A protocol covering Agent Cards, Tasks, Messages, Artifacts, contextId, and the four interaction mechanisms. Load-bearing on [[agent-card]] and [[multi-agent-systems]]. *Relevance: very high for current system-building practice. High priority.*
- **Omar Khattab et al.** — *DSPy: Compiling Declarative Language Model Calls into Self-Improving Pipelines* (Stanford NLP, 2023–2025, arXiv 2310.03714). The Bayesian-optimization framework for prompt and program compilation Gullí Ch 22 catalogs extensively; load-bearing on [[context-engineering]]. *Relevance: high. Medium-high priority follow-up.*
- **OpenAI / Mistral / etc.** — RLVR (Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards) papers (2024–2025). The RLVR paradigm underlying variable-thinking-time reasoning models. Grounds [[scaling-inference-law]] and [[reasoning-techniques]]. *Relevance: high. Medium-high priority.*
- **Albert Gu, Tri Dao** — "Mamba: Linear-Time Sequence Modeling with Selective State Spaces" (2023–2024, arXiv 2312.00752). The Selective State Space Model architecture cited in Gullí's glossary as an alternative to Transformer attention. *Relevance: medium-high. Short canonical paper; relevant if the persona-system's substrate choice becomes architectural. Medium priority.*
- **Yann LeCun** — "A Path Towards Autonomous Machine Intelligence" (2022, openreview JEPA position paper). LeCun's world-model/JEPA architecture position, cited across Gullí's agent-architecture material. *Relevance: medium. Medium priority.*
- **Hunyuan / DeepSeek / Kimi** (Moonshot AI) / **Grok (xAI)** — the model-family papers behind Gullí's Ch 27 comparative self-report chapter. Grounds [[reflection-and-llm-as-judge]]'s self-report-as-narrative reading. *Relevance: medium. Skim-priority rather than deep read.*
