- **Andrej Karpathy** — the Karpathy blog-post / talk introducing "vibe coding" terminology (2024). Grounds [[vibe-coding]]. *Relevance: medium. Short. Medium priority for cultural context.*


## Cited in *The Dialogic Imagination* (Bakhtin)

DI's citations span two registers: the primary literary texts that *are the object of analysis* (the novels and pre-novel works whose stylistic/chronotopic structure Bakhtin dissects), and the critical-theoretical sources he argues with. The primary-text list is not a follow-up-reading list in the usual sense — most of these are canonical works the wiki's project can treat as reference-points rather than sources-to-ingest — but listing them makes explicit which specific novels carry each DI claim. The critical-theoretical register *does* contain genuine follow-up candidates.

### Primary literary objects of DI's analysis

The works Bakhtin analyses in detail. Listed with the DI concepts they most directly anchor, for cross-reference from wiki pages back to the literary material.

- **François Rabelais** — *Gargantua and Pantagruel* (1532–1564). The chronotope essay's central case for the [[folkloric-chronotope]] in its most direct literary manifestation; also the paradigm of [[rogue-clown-fool]] (via Panurge) and of carnivalization's literary presence. Engaged far more fully in Bakhtin's [[raw/rabelais_and_his_world|*Rabelais and His World*]] (still-unread; highest Bakhtin follow-up priority for the wiki).
- **Miguel de Cervantes** — *Don Quixote* (1605/1615). Paradigm case of [[auto-criticism-of-discourse]] (the literary-man-hero tradition), of [[two-stylistic-lines]] transition, and of the novel's self-testing against literary discourse. The central structural-analogy case for the persona project's AI-assistant diagnosis.
- **Johann Wolfgang von Goethe** — *Wilhelm Meister's Apprenticeship* (1795–96), *Wilhelm Meister's Travels* (1821/1829), *Poetry and Truth* (1811–33). The paradigm of the [[prufungsroman-vs-bildungsroman|Bildungsroman]], and the central case of Bakhtin's "Bildungsroman essay" (the partially-surviving unfinished essay in DI's appendix region). *Wilhelm Meister* is specifically the Bildungsroman-of-human-emergence type.
- **Laurence Sterne** — *Tristram Shandy* (1759–1767). Paradigm of [[auto-criticism-of-discourse]]'s laying-bare-of-the-device form (the self-interrupting narrator, meta-narrative commentary, visual page-experiments). The English Second-Line counter-case alongside Fielding.
- **Henry Fielding** — *Tom Jones* (1749), *Joseph Andrews* (1742). Central English case for the [[two-stylistic-lines|Second Stylistic Line]] and for the authorial-narrator's [[image-of-a-language|image-of-a-language]] orchestration.
- **Charles Dickens** — multiple novels (*Little Dorrit*, *Oliver Twist*, *Pickwick Papers*, etc., used especially in the [[hybrid-construction]] and [[character-zones]] analyses). The canonical case for hybrid construction in extended form; Dibelius's Dickens monograph is Bakhtin's secondary source.
- **Alexander Pushkin** — *Eugene Onegin* (1833). Central Russian case for novelistic prose's [[dialogism]] in verse; the chapter on Tatyana's letter analyzed for its layered voices.
- **Nikolai Gogol** — *Dead Souls* (1842), tales. Used for [[hybrid-construction]] and for the [[character-zones|character-zone]] / authorial-voice entanglement analysis.
- **Ivan Turgenev** — novels. Cited for his handling of [[authoritative-vs-internally-persuasive-discourse]] in generational-conflict narratives.
- **Lev Tolstoy** — *Anna Karenina*, *War and Peace*. Used as First-Line-leaning counter-case and for [[no-evolution-of-thought|thought-as-given-field]] discussions.
- **Apuleius** — *The Golden Ass* (*Metamorphoses*, ca. 2nd c. CE). One of the two ancient paradigms of the [[folkloric-chronotope]] in its adventure-everyday variant; also direct predecessor to [[menippea|menippean]] structure.
- **Petronius** — *Satyricon* (ca. 1st c. CE). The other ancient paradigm of [[folkloric-chronotope]]; the menippean-carnivalistic ancestor line through Latin literature.
- **Heliodorus** — *Aethiopica* (ca. 3rd–4th c. CE). The canonical Greek-romance case for [[prufungsroman-vs-bildungsroman|Prüfungsroman]] structure in its ancient form.
- **Achilles Tatius** — *Leucippe and Clitophon* (ca. 2nd c. CE). Second Greek-romance case.
- **Longus** — *Daphnis and Chloe* (ca. 2nd c. CE). Greek-romance with idyllic-chronotope features.
- **Chariton**, **Xenophon of Ephesus** — earlier Greek romances. Cited for the Greek-romance chronotope's range and its stabilization in the [[prufungsroman-vs-bildungsroman|Prüfungsroman]] type.
- **Plato** — Socratic dialogues (esp. *Apology*, *Phaedo*, *Symposium*, *Republic*). Cited in DI and PDP both as menippean ancestry and as the paradigm of [[reduced-laughter]] in a seemingly serious genre.

### Critical-theoretical sources cited in DI

The genuine follow-up-reading candidates — sources Bakhtin argues with and whose independent apparatus extends or pressures DI's claims.

- **Wilhelm Dibelius** — *Charles Dickens* (Teubner 1916). Bakhtin's main secondary source for Dickens, cited extensively in the Discourse essay's treatment of [[hybrid-construction]] and [[character-zones]]. Dibelius supplies the specific stylistic observations Bakhtin then re-accentuates theoretically. *Relevance: medium-high for the Dickens case-studies. Specialized. Medium priority.*
- **Leo Spitzer** — various stylistic-linguistic works, especially the early Italian-dissertation work and later stylistic analyses. Bakhtin's primary interlocutor in thinking about *what stylistics can and cannot see* — Spitzer represents the tradition Bakhtin both draws on and argues past. *Relevance: high for [[metalinguistics]]'s methodological stakes. Multiple works, not a single volume. Medium-high priority for the methodological-history thread.*
- **Vladimir Vinogradov** — *On Fictional Prose* (*O xudožestvennoj proze*, 1930), *Poetics of Russian Literature*, other works on Russian poetic language and stylistics. The canonical Soviet stylistician Bakhtin most consistently differentiates himself from. Vinogradov's apparatus is the dominant-Soviet-stylistics Bakhtin's metalinguistics positions itself *against*. *Relevance: high. The specific Soviet context of [[metalinguistics]]'s development. Medium-high priority.*
- **Pavel Medvedev** (with Bakhtin) — *The Formal Method in Literary Scholarship: A Critical Introduction to Sociological Poetics* (*Formal'nyj metod v literaturovedenii*, 1928; tr. Wehrle, Johns Hopkins 1978). The Bakhtin-circle work critiquing Russian Formalism and developing a sociological poetics. Shares Bakhtin's authorship-questions (like the Vološinov books — some readings treat these as co-authored, others as entirely Bakhtin's work under attribution); either way the apparatus is continuous with DI's on stratification, ideology, and genre. *Relevance: very high. Short. High priority follow-up for the Bakhtin-circle register — with [[raw/the_dialogic_imagination]] and [[raw/problems_of_dostoevsky_poetics]] in place, Medvedev fills the sociological-poetics hole.*
- **Valentin Vološinov** (with Bakhtin) — *Marxism and the Philosophy of Language* (*Marksizm i filosofija jazyka*, 1929; tr. Matejka and Titunik, Harvard/Seminar 1973). Already listed in the PDP cited-sources section above. Status unchanged; DI's heteroglossia / utterance / [[cuzoj-svoj]] apparatus is continuous with Vološinov's. *Relevance: very high. Still the single most important Bakhtin-circle follow-up. High priority.*
- **Erwin Rohde** — *Der griechische Roman und seine Vorläufer* (*The Greek Novel and its Forerunners*, 3rd ed. 1914). Bakhtin's source for Greek romance scholarship; grounds the [[prufungsroman-vs-bildungsroman|Prüfungsroman]]'s ancient-Greek lineage. *Relevance: medium-high for the Greek-romance thread; specialized philology. Medium priority.*
- **Otmar Schissel von Fleschenberg** — studies of the Greek novel (early 20th c.). Secondary Bakhtin source for Greek-romance structure, cited alongside Rohde. *Relevance: medium. Specialized. Low priority.*
- **Wilhelm von Humboldt** — language-philosophical writings (*Über die Verschiedenheit des menschlichen Sprachbaues*, 1836 etc.). Humboldt's *energeia* (language as activity, not system) and *innere Sprachform* (inner form of language) ground Bakhtin's [[stratification]]-as-process and [[galilean-vs-ptolemaic-language-consciousness]] apparatus. Bakhtin's anti-Saussurean positioning leans on Humboldt. *Relevance: high. The philosophical-linguistics ancestor Bakhtin reads with. Medium-high priority.*
- **Ernst Cassirer** — *The Philosophy of Symbolic Forms* (1923–29). Cited in DI's language-philosophical register, especially around [[ideologeme]] and the claim that "a language *is* a world-view." *Relevance: medium-high. Long, but the relevant material is concentrated. Medium priority.*
- **Vladimir Propp** — *Morphology of the Folktale* (*Morfologija skazki*, 1928; tr. Scott 1968). Propp's structural analysis of the folktale is Bakhtin's primary foil for his own [[folkloric-chronotope]] analysis — Propp treats folktales as pure structure; Bakhtin insists they carry a specific chronotope with substantive temporal-spatial commitments. *Relevance: high. Short, canonical. Medium-high priority follow-up for the folkloric thread.*
- **Wolfgang Kayser** — *Das sprachliche Kunstwerk* (*The Verbal Work of Art*, 1948). German stylistics textbook Bakhtin cites as representative of the stylistics-tradition his [[metalinguistics]] argues past. *Relevance: medium. Reference-level rather than read-cover-to-cover. Low-medium priority.*
- **Gustav Shpet** — *Aesthetic Fragments* (*Éstetičeskie fragmenty*, 1922–23). The Russian phenomenologist-hermeneut whose apparatus prefigures some of Bakhtin's on word-as-event. Cited in DI's margins; more foregrounded in the Bakhtin-circle tradition's self-understanding. *Relevance: medium-high for the Russian-philosophical ancestry of Bakhtin's thought. Short. Medium priority.*
- **Georg Wilhelm Friedrich Hegel** — *Aesthetics: Lectures on Fine Art* (1835–38; Knox tr. 1975) and related material on epic/novel distinction. Bakhtin's ambivalent interlocutor on epic-vs-novel and the philosophical history of genre. *Relevance: medium. Long; the relevant sections are concentrated. Medium priority for the genre-philosophy ancestor register.*
- **Georg Lukács** — *The Theory of the Novel* (*Die Theorie des Romans*, 1916/1920; tr. Bostock, MIT 1971). Lukács's pre-Marxist theory of the novel as "transcendental homelessness" — the most important 20th-century predecessor Bakhtin engages on [[epic-vs-novel]] and on the novel's formal logic. Convergences and divergences both substantial. *Relevance: very high. Short, beautiful, canonical. High priority follow-up — the single most important predecessor-text for [[epic-vs-novel]].*
- **Roland Barthes** / Russian Formalists (Shklovsky, Tynianov, Eichenbaum) — engaged indirectly throughout DI as the formalist tradition Bakhtin's sociological poetics positions itself against. Medvedev is the more direct Bakhtin-circle statement of this critique. *Relevance: high as historical-methodological context. Already partially held in the wiki's poststructuralist cluster.*

### Not directly cited but adjacent — follow-ups for the DI cluster

Sources the wiki's DI reading would benefit from but that DI itself does not engage directly. Listed as possible follow-ups that would sharpen specific DI-cluster pages.

- **Bakhtin** — *Speech Genres and Other Late Essays* (*Éstetika slovesnogo tvorčestva*, 1979 posthumous; tr. McGee, Texas 1986). Bakhtin's late essays on speech genres, the problem of the text, the Bildungsroman, the author-hero relation, response-theory. The "Speech Genres" essay in particular extends [[utterance]] into a systematic genre-theory. *Relevance: very high. The natural next-Bakhtin after DI and PDP. High priority.*
- **Bakhtin** — *Art and Answerability: Early Philosophical Essays* (ed./tr. Holquist and Liapunov, Texas 1990). The 1919–24 ethical-aesthetic writings (including "Author and Hero in Aesthetic Activity"). Pairs with *Toward a Philosophy of the Act* (already noted as still-unread). *Relevance: high. Medium-high priority.*
- **Bakhtin** — *Rabelais and His World* (*Tvorčestvo Fransua Rable*, 1965; tr. Iswolsky, MIT 1968). Already noted as still-unread in PDP's caveat. The carnival-chronotope developed far beyond DI's treatment. *Relevance: very high. Highest-priority Bakhtin follow-up for the carnival register.*
- **Michael Holquist** — *Dialogism: Bakhtin and His World* (Routledge 1990/2002). The English-language introductory systematization; usefully cross-references DI and PDP and the Vološinov-Medvedev disputed works into a single apparatus. *Relevance: medium-high for the English-language Bakhtin reception register. Medium priority.*
- **Katerina Clark & Michael Holquist** — *Mikhail Bakhtin* (Harvard UP 1984). The canonical biographical-intellectual study grounding the wiki's Bakhtin-context knowledge. *Relevance: high for biographical and contextual depth. Medium priority.*
- **Caryl Emerson** — *The First Hundred Years of Mikhail Bakhtin* (Princeton UP 1997). The translator's systematic survey of Russian and Anglophone Bakhtin reception. *Relevance: medium-high for the reception-history register. Medium priority.*
